Now capped by a memory budget: a very long clip loses temporal resolution instead of crashing; resolution stays full. Also: (1) a clip inside a HIDDEN GROUP is now correctly SILENT in the export (the picture already hid it via group-ancestor visibility, but the audio mix only checked the clip's own flag, so hidden-group clips were invisible yet audible). (2) Cancel now works during the "Decoding frames" phase — it was only checked in the encode loop, so on a long reverse clip Cancel did nothing for minutes; the decode progress % is now shown too. (3) The per-frame export seek resolves immediately when a video can't seek (undecodable blob, not-ready, or already on the target frame) instead of burning the full 1500ms safety timeout every frame — a stuck clip used to make a 30s export look hung for 20+ minutes. (4) The frame-cache build clears its in-flight flag even if it throws (a media swap mid-build used to leave reverse/slow-mo permanently without a cache). (5) Reversed-clip preview audio scheduled ahead of the playhead now scales its delay by the preview speed (at 2× it started 2s late, at 0.5× early). Verified: groupHidden true for a hidden-group member / false for a visible group + loose layer; exporter API intact; effects still render; app boots clean; console clean.
- v3.18 — HOME SCREEN & phone sheet, audit round 11 (9 fixes). On the phone, an OS-cancelled inspector-sheet swipe (notification banner, incoming call) no longer COMMITS the dismiss and deselects your layer — pointercancel now snaps the sheet back instead. Bulk-deleting projects that include the currently-open one is dramatically faster: deleting the open project triggers a full project-switch (media decode), and doing it first made every other doomed project get fully opened in turn — the open one is now deleted LAST so that switch happens once. "Export project file" from a card's ⋯ no longer silently switches you into that project (the OPEN badge moved and a reload landed in the wrong project) — it exports and switches back. Project/template cards are now keyboard-activatable (Enter/Space) and properly labelled for screen readers (the ⋯ read as "horizontal ellipsis"; now "Project actions"). Ticking projects in Select mode updates the tapped card in place instead of rebuilding the whole grid and re-fetching every thumbnail from IndexedDB each tick (each card flashing its placeholder on a big library). Rapid taps on two different project cards no longer kick off two overlapping loads (leaked media + racing refresh) — a load in flight ignores further taps. "Import project file" closes the home screen only on a SUCCESSFUL import, not on a blind 400ms timer that dumped you into the editor even if you cancelled the picker. And inserting a template whose data was evicted from IndexedDB now says so instead of falsely toasting "Inserted". Verified: cards role=button + aria-label + Enter activates; ⋯ aria "Project actions"; select-tick preserves the card node (no rebuild) and applies the selected state; importFile takes a callback; console clean.
- v3.19 — two more round-3 fixes. (1) Playback could FREEZE in an infinite loop: set an export/loop region near the end, then shorten the project (delete the last clip) — the stale loop-in now sat past the timeline end, so every playback frame wrapped to a point beyond the end with no progress (100% CPU, frozen picture). autoFitDuration now clamps/clears a loop region that no longer fits, and the playback wrap is guarded against an out-of-range target. (2) Tapping the version label to force-update within ~0.6s of an edit no longer loses that edit — it flushes the pending autosave before nuking caches and reloading. Verified: shrinking the project past the loop-in clears the region; a region fully inside the timeline survives; console clean.
- v3.20 — SECURITY, audit round 12 (3 fixes; the security lens of the final fleet run). FreeMotion projects are shareable .fmotion.json files, so an imported file is UNTRUSTED input — and two real holes were open. (1) A malicious project could set a shape's media-fill to a REMOTE url (e.g. https://attacker.tld/beacon.png?id=you); on the very first render the browser fetched it, leaking your IP / user-agent / open-time with zero clicks (a tracking beacon, and a way to probe your local network) — the app already blocked this for embedded video/image media but layer.fillImage slipped through. Import now strips any fillImage that isn't a data: URL. (2) A project with an absurd canvas size (e.g. 16000×16000) allocated ~1GB per buffer and OOM-crashed the tab on open — and because it got autosaved as the current project, it crashed again on every relaunch: an unrecoverable brick. Import now clamps width/height to an even value in [16, 7680], fps to a standard rate, duration to ≤ 3600s, and refuses a file with > 2000 layers. (3) The AI op-validator's transform whitelist used `key in TABLE`, which lets inherited prototype keys (constructor, __proto__) pass; now uses hasOwnProperty. Verified: a remote-fillImage import has the URL stripped while a data: URL is kept; a 16000×16000/fps-999 import is clamped to 7680/30; a 3000-layer file is refused; console clean. (The multi-agent security audit that found these IS the security review for this batch.)
- v3.21 — TOOLS & gesture teardown, audit round 13 (10 fixes). Free Crop no longer stays stuck to the old layer when you tap a different clip mid-crop (it had no self-close, so the old layer composited uncropped while the inspector showed the new one) — selecting another layer, or deleting the selection, or pressing Escape all close it now, and re-opening crop can't orphan the previous overlay. A fast SECOND layer-reorder right after a drop is no longer silently wiped by the first drop's 150ms settle timer (a regression from the v3.13 settle animation) — a new grab is ignored until the previous one finishes. Every touch value-scrubber, the Move trackpad and the rotate dial now commit on pointercancel, so an iOS-interrupted drag (notification, call) doesn't leave an uncommitted change that the next unrelated action sweeps into its undo step. The eyedropper aborts cleanly on a cancelled sample instead of leaving its magnifier loupe + animation loop spinning forever; a cancelled freehand stroke is finalized instead of lost. Escape now also closes the Touch-up (Remove Object) picker. And a context menu taller than the screen scrolls instead of clipping its top items off-screen (the tall layer ⋯ menu on a short phone). Verified: single reorder lands on the exact row after settle, a second reorder after settle works, no stuck rows; crop-tool has its lifecycle API; ctx-menu scrolls; console clean.
- v3.22 — AI (BYOK) hardening, audit round 14 (5 fixes). The important one: re-rolling an AI task (the ↻ button on a finished build) deleted that task's layers BEFORE calling the model, so if the network dropped or the model returned nothing, the content was permanently GONE while the UI reassuringly said "kept" — it now snapshots first and restores the original if the reroll fails or comes back empty. Cancelling an AI build no longer sticks: the abort flag was never reset, so every later re-roll/refine silently ran with all network retries disabled — it's cleared at the start of each. Refining with no API key from the demo/template screen no longer wipes the whole done bar down to a lone "Back" button (it shows a toast instead). A hostile/huge server Retry-After header can no longer freeze the panel for minutes (clamped to 30s). And an AI caption track now spans all its segments instead of being truncated to a dead 5s box (a 20s caption run stayed 20s). Verified: AI + aiOps API intact; a 0–20s caption track built via applyOps is 20s long, not 5; console clean.
- v3.23 — EFFECT BROWSER, audit round 15 (3 fixes) — the last of the deep audit. Tapping ★ to favourite an effect no longer jumps the Recents/Favourites pager back to page 1 and restarts the featured carousel from the left — it now rebuilds only that one section and keeps your page. Opening a category sub-screen now pauses the featured carousel's auto-scroll and its live thumbnail ticker underneath (they were repainting invisibly the whole time you browsed a category), resuming when you go Back. And the effect search is debounced (~120ms), so fast typing no longer tears down and rebuilds the entire result grid — re-mounting a live-render canvas per match — on every single keystroke. Verified: browser opens with pager + 13 categories + search; star toggle keeps the pager; category view opens and closes cleanly; console clean.
- v3.24 — the "audit the audit" batch + three new asks (Ezra). NEW FEATURES: (a) dragging on a layer header to paint-select multiple layers now AUTO-SCROLLS the timeline when you reach the top/bottom edge — same eased, capped, time-based feel as the reorder handle — and painting is drift-tolerant (it keeps selecting even if your finger slides sideways onto the clip lanes, and clamps to the nearest row past the ends); (b) BIG-MONITOR layout: the timeline is no longer a fixed 232px — on ≥1100px screens it grows with the viewport (up to 300px), on ≥1600px up to ~33vh/460px, the inspector widens (up to 340–380px) so controls breathe, and the transport's 7 buttons cluster in a centred 780px band instead of being flung to the far corners of a wide screen; PLUS a drag-to-resize handle on the timeline's top edge (desktop) so you can size it yourself — persisted across sessions; (c) mobile: selecting a clip now shows a ⋯ button in the top bar that opens the full clip menu (split, lock, reverse, replace media, reset transform, loop, blend, parent…) — before, only duplicate/delete/group were reachable on phone. REGRESSION FIXES — a fresh 8-agent adversarial audit reviewed *my own* v3.08–v3.23 fixes and found 8 real bugs the fixes introduced, all fixed here: (1) editing a value while PLAYING wrote the keyframe ~half a frame off the (snapped) playhead, so it couldn't be removed with ◆ and a second edit dropped a duplicate — the captured time is now re-snapped after the auto-pause; (2) crop keyframes drew timeline diamonds (added in the audit) but couldn't be deleted or copied — delete/copy/paste now handle crop, with a null-guard so pasting a crop keyframe onto a crop-less layer can't crash; (3) SPLITTING a clip destroyed any looping (cycle/ping-pong) animation on both halves — looping props keep their full keyframe set now; (4) a project with >40 media-fill layers thrashed the fill cache so EVERY fill rendered as the blue placeholder and re-decoded every image every frame — eviction now only reclaims layers that left the scene, never a live one; (5) split's boundary keyframe hardcoded linear easing, flattening the segment it cut — it inherits the real easing now; (6) a params-less Tint rendered at half strength vs. the slider (catalog default is 1, not 0.5); (7) the same missing-param guard was missing on the adjustment-layer path, so a params-less Threshold adjustment layer blew the whole frame solid white — mirrored the fallbacks; (8) paging categories in the effect browser leaked a depth counter that permanently froze the Featured carousel. SECURITY: extended import sanitisation beyond fillImage — labelColor / clipColor / gradient stops are interpolated raw into CSS on render, so a `url(http://evil)` there was the same zero-click beacon as the fillImage hole; imports now validate every CSS-reaching colour field (hex / rgb / hsl / name only). Verified in-browser at desktop-wide + 380px phone; console clean.
- v3.25 — AUDIO TOOLS (the easy, on-device half of Ezra's audio ask). Two new actions on any video/audio clip's ⋯ menu, both pure vanilla Web Audio — no ML, no server, no dependencies. (1) "Save audio as WAV…" extracts the clip's soundtrack and downloads it as a 16-bit WAV file (the video→audio-file / extract-audio ask). (2) "Remove vocals (karaoke)" — a DSP vocal remover: lead vocals are mixed dead-centre (identical L/R), so the side signal (L−R) cancels them, and a low-passed mono sum is added back below ~180Hz so the bass/kick the centre-cancel would have eaten survive. It adds a vocals-removed copy as its own audio layer and mutes the original, so you hear the instrumental. Needs a stereo track (mono is refused with a toast). Crude but instant — not a clean per-instrument split (that needs the ML we deliberately skipped). Verified: WAV encoder round-trips (encode→decode identical), the karaoke DSP knocks a dead-centre tone down to ~15% while off-centre content stays at ~88%, real-file ingestion makes a genuine 2s playable clip, original muted, mono refused cleanly, both menu items present on audio/video layers; console clean.
- v3.26 — the three remaining transport/bookmark asks from Ezra's list. (1) REVIEW PLAY: a new button at the far right of the play row previews from the current frame and jumps the playhead back to where it was when you stop (button, space, or end of timeline) — "play without moving the playhead", so previewing never loses your working position. A balancing hidden spacer on the far left keeps ▶ dead-centre (verified 0px offset at desktop AND 375px phone, no overflow). (2) HOLD PLAY → LOOP: press-and-hold the play button toggles whole-timeline loop mode (playback restarts at the end); a small ↻ badge appears on ▶ while it's armed, and the trailing click is suppressed so it doesn't also start/stop playback. (The loop wrap already existed; this just makes it reachable without digging into a menu.) (3) HOLD THE TIMECODE → SET THUMBNAIL FRAME: long-press the timecode pill (the bookmark control) to pin the current frame as the project's card thumbnail — captured right then while the video is correctly seeked, and marked with a distinct SMALLER accent-coloured pin on the ruler (vs the bigger benchmark markers). The pin is persisted and the periodic autosave thumbnail no longer overwrites it; re-pinning elsewhere moves the single thumbnail marker. Overshoot easing (also on the list) was already an option — it's in every keyframe's easing menu alongside Anticipate. Verified in-browser: review play restores the playhead exactly; hold-play toggles loop + suppresses the click; hold-timecode pins the frame + drops one thumbnail marker that moves on re-pin; play centred and transport un-cramped at 375px; console clean.
- v3.27 — BUG-FIX pass (multi-agent hunt: 8 subsystem finders → adversarial verify → 7 confirmed, all fixed). THREE majors: (1) a speed-RAMPED frame-blend clip (smooth slow-mo with keyframed speed) silently lost its frame cache after reload or a Reverse on/off — four predicates tested `(speed||1)<1`, which is `NaN<1`=false when speed is an animated OBJECT, so the clip was never cached and slow-mo degraded to choppy per-frame seeking (in preview AND export); fixed at all four sites (app.js requestPlay + maybeClearCache, storage.js rehydrate, exporter.js) to be `FM.isAnimated(speed) || (speed||1)<1`. (2) With two browser tabs open on different projects, tab A's autosave wrote its index card + thumbnail to whatever project the OTHER tab last opened (touchCurrent/pinThumbnail read the shared curId() instead of this tab's boundId) — one project's card would silently show another's name/size/thumbnail; now both use boundId. (3) The karaoke "remove vocals" track drifted out of sync with the picture when the source clip was trimmed/sped/reversed — the WAV is the full source but the new layer got default trimStart:0/speed:1/reversed:false; it now inherits the original's trim/speed(deep-cloned ramp)/reverse. FOUR minors: (4) Copy-keyframe silently dropped speed/fill/colour/stroke/shadow keyframes (propKey/resolveSlot didn't cover what animatedProps draws + delete already handled) — copy/paste is now symmetric with the diamonds, with null-guards. (5) Stacking two of the SAME pixel effect (two Tints, two Posterizes/Thresholds/Duotones/RGB-Splits) double-composited anti-aliased edges into a thick/dark fringe (a nested effect's final blit landed on its own still-populated scratch canvas) — each now clears its scratch when it's the nested target. (6) The duration hover-tooltip could read ":60" (e.g. 119.7s → "1:60") — round to whole seconds before splitting. (7) On a slow project open, using a different card's ⋯ "Export project file" could download the WRONG (currently-loading) project — the switch was skipped by the open-in-flight guard but the export ran anyway; openProject now reports success and the export bails if the switch didn't happen. Verified in-browser: tooltip 2:00 not 1:60; karaoke inherits trim/reverse/ramp (deep-cloned); copy→paste round-trips a fill keyframe; ramped frame-blend clip now matches the cache predicate; two stacked tints render clean; console clean across all 7 touched files.
- v3.28 — Ezra's 7-item UI batch. (1) REVIEW PLAY is no longer its own transport button — it's now a press-and-HOLD on the far-right ⛶ view button (tap = the view/zoom popup as before, hold = preview-from-here-and-snap-back). The dedicated button + its balancing spacer are gone, so the play row is back to 7 buttons with ▶ dead-centre. (2) PASTE LAYER is now a split button in the ⧉ layer-actions menu: the label still pastes on top, but the ▸ arrow opens a position picker (On top / Above <each layer> / At the bottom) so you choose exactly which layer it lands above instead of always the top. (3) The FEATURED effects carousel now LOOPS seamlessly instead of hitting a dead wall at the right edge (the cards are duplicated and the scroll wraps invisibly across the identical halves — auto-scroll and manual swipe both). (4) CUSTOM canvas size + CUSTOM fps: the Canvas-size dialog gained a "Custom" aspect chip (reveals Width × Height number inputs, clamped even/16–7680) and a "Custom…" frame-rate option (reveals an fps input, 1–120); presets still work, and opening the dialog on a non-preset size/fps shows it as Custom. (5) The redundant "Preview zoom" row was removed from the Canvas-size dialog — zoom % + Reset view already live in that far-right ⛶ view popup. (6) Removed the "Frame blend interpolates new in-between frames…" explanation line under the Smooth slow-motion checkbox. (7) On PC, clicking OUTSIDE the effect browser's panel now closes it (was staying open); the full-screen backdrop already blocked the clicks from reaching what's behind. Verified in-browser: 7-button transport with ▶ centred + amfit hold→review-restores / tap→popup; paste lands at the chosen z-index (top/middle/bottom); featured carousel renders doubled for the loop; custom 1001→1002-even size + 48fps apply; canvas dialog has no zoom row; fx-browser closes on backdrop click; console clean.
- v3.29 — review-play button now shows its state + stops on a tap. While review play is running the far-right ⛶ view button turns into an accent ■ STOP icon, and a single TAP stops it (returning the playhead to where you started) — before, the icon didn't change and you had to hold again to stop. The icon reverts to the view glyph on any stop (tap, spacebar, reaching the end, switching projects) via a shared FM.syncReviewButton hook in FM.pause; a normal tap when not reviewing still opens the view/zoom popup. Verified in-browser: hold → ■ stop + review runs; tap → stops, icon reverts, playhead restored, popup stays closed; an external stop reverts the icon; plain tap opens the popup; console clean.
- v3.30 — the Paste-Layer position picker (the ▸ arrow on Paste Layer) now shows each layer's THUMBNAIL next to its name — the same little preview as the timeline row's far-left — so you can see exactly which layer you're pasting above, not just read a name. "On top" / "At the bottom" get ↑ / ↓ glyphs in the same slot so everything lines up. Reuses FM.renderThumb (the timeline's own thumbnail renderer) via a new iconEl option on the context menu. Verified in-browser: the picker lists On top ⤒ · Above:<layer> (with a painted thumbnail per layer) · At the bottom ⤓; thumbnails render the real layer with its colour; console clean.
- v3.31 — reverted the featured-effects carousel back to hitting a wall at the end instead of looping (I'd misread the v3.28 request). The duplicated second set of cards is gone (each featured effect shows once again), and the auto-scroll stops at the right edge rather than jumping back to the start. Verified: 12 featured effects render as a single set, scrolling to the end stays put; console clean.
- v3.32 — shapes (and text/images) now get Speed + Volume adjusters too. Speed works the same as on video — it re-times the clip (200% halves its length, etc.), with the ramp keyframe/easing controls; the video-only Frame-blend + Reverse toggles are hidden on a shape's speed panel since it has no frames to blend. Volume shows as a card as well, but it's greyed/DISABLED on anything with no audio (shapes/text/images) — pressing it just says "This layer has no audio" instead of opening a dead panel. Video keeps its Speed/Audio quick-buttons unchanged. Verified in-browser: a shape's inspector lists Speed (enabled) + Volume (disabled); Volume click is blocked with a toast; the Speed panel shows only Speed % (no frame-blend/reverse) and 200% halves the duration; console clean.
- v3.33 — audio-tools home + thumbnail-marker fixes (Ezra). (1) "Save audio as WAV…" and "Remove vocals (karaoke)" now live at the bottom of the VOLUME section (open a video/audio clip → Volume) instead of only in the layer ⋯ menu — which was easy to miss on PC (that's why Save-audio seemed missing). Removed from the ⋯ menu to avoid duplication. (2) The pinned thumbnail-frame marker no longer draws a full-height line down the timeline — it's now a short accent stub (a pin, not a ruler line). (3) The thumbnail marker is no longer a skip target: the prev/next benchmark buttons step over it (real benchmarks still count). Verified in-browser: Volume panel shows both tool buttons (Save-WAV wired to downloadLayerAudio), the ⋯ menu no longer lists them, timelineSnapPoints includes a benchmark but excludes the thumbnail marker; console clean.
- v3.34 — BUG-FIX pass (multi-agent hunt weighted to the recent v3.28–v3.33 code: 8 finders → adversarial verify → 7 confirmed, all fixed). FIVE majors: (1) reopening the Canvas-size dialog on a CUSTOM (or any non-1080p-preset) project and hitting Apply without changing anything silently RESIZED the whole composition — cvDetect always snapped to the nearest preset; it now only picks a preset on an exact W×H match and otherwise opens as Custom, preserving your real size. (2) Removing the thumbnail-frame marker from the timeline (right-click → Remove / Clear all) left the project pinned forever, so the card thumbnail froze and never auto-updated again — removing the pin marker now unpins and resumes auto thumbnails. (3) The loop toggle in the easing/graph editor was silently reverted on the next rebuild for any clip that also had a clip-menu Loop set — the per-prop loop override now survives (newly-keyframed props still inherit the clip loop). (4) Loop ON + deleting the last clip while playing spun a runaway 100%-CPU playback loop with the transport stuck — an empty timeline now stops cleanly. (5) SECURITY: an imported .fmotion.json could smuggle a CSS url() beacon through a gradient's angle/type field (the sanitizer only checked the colours) — angle is now coerced to a number and type whitelisted. TWO minors: (6) duplicating or splitting a Camera made a second camera that hijacked the view — both are now refused for cameras (and split is limited to real clips). (7) a reversed clip inside a HIDDEN group still played its audio in preview (picture + export were correctly silent) — now silent too. Verified in-browser: custom size survives reopen+Apply; thumbnail removal unpins; graph-loop override survives rebuild; loop+empty pauses; gradient angle coerced to 0 / type→linear; camera stays single through duplicate+split; console clean.
- v3.35 — karaoke is now a TOGGLE, not a one-way trip. The instrumental track is tagged with a link back to its source clip (karakeOf), so the button flips it both ways: press "Remove vocals (karaoke)" → the instrumental track appears and the source mutes; press "Restore vocals" → that track is removed and the source unmutes, back exactly where you started. It works pressed from EITHER layer (the source clip or the karaoke track itself), the button label + accent state follow the current state, and the link survives save/reload so it's still a toggle after you reopen the project. Verified in-browser: off→on adds a tagged twin that inherits trim/speed/reverse and mutes the source; on→off from the source restores (twin gone, unmuted); on→off from the karaoke track does the same and re-selects the source; the button reads "Remove vocals (karaoke)" vs "Restore vocals" correctly; console clean.
- v3.36 — Audio Effects: a dedicated effects menu for sound. 27 effects across four categories (EQ & Filter: Bass & Treble, 3-Band EQ, Low/High/Band-pass, Notch, Telephone · Space & Stereo: Reverb, Echo, Ping-Pong Delay, Stereo Width, Pan, Auto-Pan/8D · Dynamics: Gain, Compressor, Limiter, Tremolo · Character: Pitch Shift, Distortion, Bit Crush, Lo-Fi, Chorus, Flanger, Phaser, Vibrato, Ring Mod, Vocal Remove), each a pure Web Audio node graph so the identical chain renders in preview AND in the exported MP4. New "Audio Effects" card on video clips (stack with drag-reorder, swipe-delete, keyframable params + easing curves) and a full-screen Add-Audio-Effect browser mirroring the visual one (featured carousel that hits a wall, recents/favourites, categories, search) with a hand-drawn stroke glyph per effect. Forward clips now route their <video> audio through Web Audio ONLY once they carry an effect — a project with none keeps the exact old path, byte for byte. Import hardening: audioFx is whitelisted against the registry and rebuilt from its schema, never trusted from the file.
- v3.36 — audio-effects hardening found by the build's own audit: Stereo Width and Vocal Remove silently broke on MONO clips (ChannelSplitter is channelInterpretation "discrete", so mono arrives as [L,0] and L−R evaluates to L, not silence — Width killed the right channel at its default and Vocal Remove left the vocal fully intact plus a ~+3.5 dB bass boost); both now force a speakers up-mix and are a true no-op on mono. Two prototype-chain whitelist bypasses closed: a keyframe easing of "toString"/"constructor" resolved through Object.prototype, passed the import sanitiser and poisoned the prop with NaN (fixed at the eval site in scene.js AND in storage.js), and the same shape let a crafted effect type smuggle ghost rows past the registry. LFO phase is now anchored to scene time, so tremolo/auto-pan/flanger/phaser render the same phase regardless of export range (they previously drifted between preview and export). Pitch Shift ships measured, not assumed: +7 semitones off 440 Hz lands at 659.99 Hz (target 659.26). Audio graphs are now torn down on project switch / media replace / reset, so their LFOs no longer run forever on the one shared AudioContext. Category nav buttons in BOTH effect browsers went from ~19px to a proper 40px touch target.
- v3.37 — Audio-reactive animation (AE "Convert Audio to Keyframes"): a new "Audio → keyframes…" tool in the Volume section drives any layer's Scale / Opacity / Rotation / Position from a clip's loudness. Pick a band (Overall / Bass / Mid / Treble — split with offline biquad filtering), sensitivity, smoothing (attack/release envelope follower) and a threshold gate; a live preview traces the envelope; Apply bakes RDP-simplified, hand-editable keyframes onto the target's chosen property over a min→max range. Loudness is measured against the SOURCE audio through the same trim/speed/reverse mapping the exporter uses, so the animation lines up with what's actually heard (incl. sped-up and ramped clips — the RMS window scales with playback rate so transients aren't skipped). Groundwork for audio-driven Behaviors: FM.audioEnvelope() is the reusable primitive. The sheet traps editor shortcuts so Backspace can't delete the layer behind it. Local-only, no schema change (writes ordinary keyframes).
- v3.38 — Trim Paths + dashed strokes + Repeater (After Effects shape-layer parity), for shape/path layers. Trim Path: keyframe Start/End/Offset to draw a stroke on (animate End 0→100% = the outline draws itself), measured against the shape's TRUE outline length via a path-tracing proxy so every kind (incl. ring/arc/pie/semicircle) reveals the right fraction. Dashes: dash length + gap + keyframable offset for marching-ants. Repeater: 1–50 copies each with a cumulative offset X/Y, rotation, scale and opacity falloff — spinners, bursts, ladders. All keyframable and shown for shapes only. Effects/masks/blend wrap the repeated result as one. Imported .fmproj values are clamped/rebuilt from the schema (copies capped at 50, prototype-chain easings rejected, no NaN reaches the canvas).
- v3.39 — Export beyond MP4: Animated GIF, transparent background, and PNG image-sequence export. The Export dialog gains a Format picker (MP4 / Animated GIF / PNG frames) and a "Transparent background" toggle (GIF + PNG only — MP4 can't carry alpha). GIF uses a from-scratch GIF89a encoder (median-cut quantization, per-frame local palette, LZW, Floyd–Steinberg dither, 1-bit transparency, NETSCAPE loop) — no library; it caps the longest side at 640px so files stay sane. PNG frames are zipped with a from-scratch store-only ZIP writer (CRC32, zip-slip-safe names) as name_0000.png, name_0001.png…; a size guard fails fast with a friendly message instead of OOMing on a huge sequence. Everything is local-only and streams per frame. Verified: GIF decodes to the right frames/pixels via ImageDecoder, the zip validates in Python + unzip, transparent export restores the project background.
- v3.40 — Behaviors: After Effects expressions, packaged as tappable property modifiers (no code to write). In Move & Transform, "+ Add behavior" drops a modifier onto a chosen transform prop (X / Y / Scale / Rotation / Opacity): Wiggle (organic random drift), Oscillate (clean sine), Bounce (elastic settle after each keyframe move), Follow (track another layer's prop, with multiplier/offset/delay — a trailing echo), and Audio-drive (react live to a clip's loudness by band, reusing the audio-react envelope). Multiple stack per prop; follow replaces the base, the rest add on top. Loop isn't here because keyframe loop modes (cycle/pingpong/continue) already do it. Diff-free: a project with no behaviors renders and saves byte-identical. Follow/Audio layer refs are remapped on import so they survive a share; imported behaviors are whitelisted + clamped (prototype-chain types rejected, params rebuilt from the schema, capped at 24). Behaviors are hidden on groups (a group's transform reaches children via raw parenting, not the resolver, so the control would be dead there).
- v3.41 — Particles: a real emitter, added as a generative effect (Effects → Procedural → Particles, and it leads the featured carousel). Drop it on any layer (a transparent shape = a pure emitter) and it sprays deterministic particles — confetti, sparks, snow, bokeh, bursts. Controls: rate, lifetime, direction + spread, speed, gravity, size and opacity over life, spin, a start→end colour gradient, five sprite shapes (circle / square / triangle / star / streak) and Normal/Add blend. A moving emitter leaves a trail (each particle is born at the emitter's position at its birth time). Fully deterministic — scrubbing to a time always shows the same frame and it exports frame-exact — and bounded (hard cap on live particles) so heavy settings stay ~a few ms/frame. Reuses the whole effects system (keyframing, thumbnails, copy/paste, import) so there's no new layer type to learn.
- v3.42 — Pen masks: multiple bezier/polygon masks per layer with Add / Subtract / Intersect modes, per-mask feather, opacity and invert — real After Effects-style masking on top of the old single-shape mask. Draw them with a canvas pen tool (tap to drop points, tap the first to close, then drag / insert / delete / smooth), and the whole path is keyframable so you can rotoscope a moving reveal. Masks live in canvas space (a reveal window on the layer's rasterized result). On an adjustment layer a mask becomes a LOCAL grade — the effect only touches the masked region. Fully diff-free (a project with no pen masks renders and saves byte-identical), and imports are hardened (modes whitelisted, points/keyframes clamped and capped). Fixes from the audit: the seed no longer keys off an empty first mask; Done on an untouched animated path no longer injects a stray keyframe; adjustment masks are actually applied.
- v3.43 — 3D layers + camera parallax. Every layer's Rotate mode now has X tilt and Y tilt alongside the normal rotation: tilt a clip/text/shape in real perspective (card flips, leaning panels — keyframe Y tilt 0→180 for a proper flip that shows the mirrored back past 90°, not a blank). Rendered by texture-mapping the layer onto a perspective quad using the same mesh renderer as the 3D solids, so it works in preview AND export, honours effects/masks/opacity/parenting, and costs nothing when the tilts are 0 (byte-identical render — verified by hash). And the camera now does PARALLAX: layers with different Z depths shift by different amounts as the camera pans (a z=800 layer moves exactly its perspective factor less than a z=0 one — measured −83px vs −100px on a 100px pan), so a camera move over a layered scene finally reads as depth instead of a flat slide.
- v3.44 — Editable motion paths (spatial keyframes — the last of the 8 pro-parity features). Position keyframes now carry optional spatial tangents, so movement can CURVE instead of always travelling in straight lines between keyframes. A new Motion path button in Move & Transform (shows once x/y is keyframed, unparented layers) opens an on-canvas editor: the layer's real evaluated trajectory drawn live, a draggable dot at every position keyframe (drag = move the keyframe, playhead untouched), tap a dot for its bezier tangent handles, plus one-tap Smooth path (Catmull-Rom auto-tangents) and Straighten. Under the hood: keyframes gain optional per-axis Hermite tangents evaluated in evalProp — bit-for-bit identical when unused (hashed over 7,000 samples), hostile/NaN tangents ignored, temporal easing still controls speed along the curve, and tangents survive save/undo. Audit fixes: the in-handle now draws at the TRUE incoming control point (v − ti, so a smooth keyframe's handles sit collinear on opposite sides); dragging a dot on a static axis seeds a keyframe instead of shoving the whole path; 40px touch targets; the add-FAB parks while the editor owns the bottom bar; the editor rejects the same malformed tangents the renderer does.
- v3.45 — Post-mission review pass: a cross-feature bug hunt + an editor-minded UX review of all 8 new systems (fleet of hunters that reproduced findings live, plus reviewers driving the app as three editor personas). 18 fixes: parented layers now inherit behavior-driven parent motion (a wiggling null finally moves its rig); opacity behaviors apply exactly once under tilt/masks/motion-blur/effects (they double-applied); Content Motion Blur no longer teleports behavior-driven layers; motion blur no longer whites out borders/strokes (sub-frames render normally, plates composite additively); time-driven effects + particles on a GROUP animate instead of freezing; adjustment layers inside a flattened group grade again; particles spray from a parented/behavior-driven emitter's real position; tilted displacement-map sources no longer ghost into each other; split can no longer corrupt an animated mask path; copy/paste + duplicate remap Follow/Audio-drive layer refs; keyframe copy/paste carries motion-path tangents; karaoke twins inherit volume/fades/audio effects and restore sweeps split/duplicated twins; audio-drive envelopes refresh when a clip is retimed and are pre-warmed before export (no more mid-file pop-in); reversed+ramped clips preview the exact exporter resample; GIF exports no longer stall in a backgrounded tab; the mask pen bar no longer sits under the + FAB. UX: Masks got their own property card (they were buried at the bottom of Blending); the Motion path button is always visible in Move mode (dimmed with a hint until X/Y is keyframed); the audio bake toast says where the keyframes went; the sheet is named "Audio → keyframes" everywhere; tilt scrubs snap to exact 0; a hint links Z + Camera to parallax; behaviors explain they run on top of keyframes; the export dialog is titled "Export" and hides Quality for GIF/PNG; the drop-zone emoji became a stroke icon.
- v3.46 — SHAKES, done properly (Ezra: "strength settings too low by a mile" — fixed by a mile). The Shake effect now runs violent: Amount up to 800px (was 100), Speed to 40Hz, Twist to 180°, plus four new controls — Zoom punch (the scale slams IN on impact), Hardness (blends the old smooth drift into hard stepped jitter, the beat-drop feel), Direction lock (Horizontal/Vertical slam shakes), and Smear (deterministic ghost copies trailing along the shake velocity so violent frames read as motion, not teleporting). Fresh shakes come in hot by default; every EXISTING shake in old projects renders byte-identical (verified sub-pixel against the old formula). The Wiggle behavior got the same treatment: Amount to 1200, Frequency to 40Hz, and a Hardness slider. And the headline: THE CAMERA CAN NOW SHAKE — behaviors unlocked on the camera layer, so one Wiggle on the camera slams the entire scene, and layers at different Z depths lag the shake with true parallax (measured: whole-scene swing 560px, far layer lagging the near). Shake leads the featured effects carousel. All deterministic — scrub and export match preview exactly.
- v3.47 — Effect PRESETS, the Alight Motion way: HOLD any effect in the Add-Effect browser (or right-click on PC) and a preset sheet slides in — pre-made, keyframed uses of that effect, each with a LIVE animated thumbnail rendered by the real engine, a duration badge, and one-tap add. Presets anchor at the playhead: park on the beat, tap "Beat Slam", the hit lands exactly there (falls back to the clip start if the playhead is outside the clip). Ships with a starter pack of 12 — five shakes (Beat Slam, Earthquake, Handheld Cam, Hype Shake X, Rumble Build) plus Zoom Hit, Glitch Pop, Focus Pull, Pixel Reveal, Untwist In, Neon Flicker, Chroma Drift. And it's a SYSTEM, not a list: tune any effect on a layer, ⋯ → "Save as preset…", and it appears under "Your presets" with its own live preview (delete with ✕); "Export all" copies your presets as code — paste them to Claude and they get baked into the app so every install ships with them. Old projects untouched (presets only produce ordinary effect instances with ordinary keyframes — copy/paste, undo, import hardening all just work; custom presets are re-validated from the effect schema on every load, own-properties only). Bonus fix: an effect's ⋯ → Duplicate now copies your ACTUAL settings + keyframes (it silently made a fresh default instance before). A one-time toast teaches the hold gesture. Verified in-browser at 380px: hold opens the sheet (the ending tap is swallowed, no accidental plain add), Beat Slam applied at playhead 2s lands keyframes at 2→2.45s with all params, capture round-trips absolute→relative times cleanly (8.2s → 0, float dirt rounded), hostile localStorage presets (proto-chain keys, Infinity, bad easings) are filtered, preview thumbs scale px params so the motion stays in frame, console clean.
- v3.48 — Shape library quality pass with a fail-proof visual audit: a harness that renders ALL 53 shapes straight through FM.traceShapePath (the exact tracer the canvas, thumbnails and point editor share) into a labeled grid, then each suspect at 860px with its authored points overlaid — nothing was judged from code, only from rendered pixels, and every fix went back through the harness before landing. Findings: FOUR shapes rebuilt. Heart (Ezra: "weird as flip") — the lobes rendered as peaks because auto Catmull-Rom tangents are far too short for a half-circle dome; re-authored with manual tangent handles: fat round lobes, proper dip, clean taper (emoji-grade silhouette). Shield — a real geometry bug, not just taste: the old two-arc build left the arc's bottom-center endpoint in the path, so the right side dropped a vertical cliff onto the tip while the left ran diagonally (asymmetric at any size); re-authored symmetric: peaked top, straight shoulders, sides sweeping into the bottom point. Cloud — was lopsided (no distinct right puff), had a corner notch between puffs and a wavy slanted bottom; now three distinct puffs with crease valleys and a flat, rounded-corner bottom. Flame — was a garlic bulb with a wisp; now a leaning tip with a concave lick-notch, swelling belly, round bottom. Everything else (49 shapes incl. moon, paperplane, person, car, umbrella, thumbsup) verified fine at scale and left byte-identical. The four rebuilt shapes still work in Edit Points (manual [u,v,1,hx,hy] handles are preserved by shapeToPoints and honored by pointCtrl — verified), still fill with healthy coverage at 100px (ink-count sanity), and the full grid re-render after baking came from the shipped file, not the live overrides. Console clean.
- v3.49 — Shape audit round 2 (Ezra's five call-outs, all confirmed and rebuilt through the same render-big-judge-fix harness). Snowflake — the branch stubs were authored for the TOP of an arm and reused unmirrored at the BOTTOM, so half the branches pointed the wrong way and it genuinely read as two different flakes overlaid; regenerated as ONE arm (spoke + two tip-ward branches) rotated 6×60°, symmetric by construction. Car — the wheels were shallow smooth-point lumps in the body outline; now the body is a proper sedan silhouette (rounded nose/tail, flat roof) with the wheels as TRUE circle subpaths, round by definition. Person & Woman — the single-outline figures traced every limb in one wobbly path ("look fucked" — confirmed); rebuilt as restroom-pictogram parts (head circle, rounded-shoulder torso / flared dress, hanging arms with round hands, straight legs with round feet) unioned by the nonzero fill, like the sun/bomb/magnifier already do. Thumbs-up — the thumb spanned nearly half the hand; slimmed to a real thumb's width with a rounded tip, scalloped fingers kept. Pointing hand — the curled fingers were lumpy auto-tangent bumps; rebuilt from parts (rounded fist, upright thumb, long round-capped index, three round-capped curled fingers). Verified: every override was eyeballed at 860px before baking, the full 53-shape grid re-rendered from the shipped file with zero trace errors, all five still fill correctly at icon size, console clean.
- v3.50 — Pointing hand, third time's the charm (Ezra: "an abomination" — he was right). The v3.49 parts build (rounded-rect fist + bars) read as Lego, so it was re-authored the way the accepted thumbs-up is built: ONE continuous silhouette — cuff, palm with a thumb dome on top, a long round-tipped index, three round knuckle scallops cascading below it, rounded palm bottom. Key lesson re-applied: the knuckle caps carry MANUAL bezier tangents ([u,v,1,hx,hy]) because auto Catmull-Rom tangents render sawtooth spikes at that bump size (same root cause as the v3.48 heart lobes). Iterated live in the harness (v1 spiky knuckles → v2 round) before baking; verified from the shipped file at 860px AND at 60px (reads as ☞ at thumbnail size), console clean.
- v3.51 — Masks moved INTO the Effects section (Ezra: masks belong with effects, not as their own card). The standalone Masks property card (added v3.45) is retired; the full masks UI — mask list, Add/Subtract/Intersect, feather, opacity, invert, path keyframing, Edit path, + Add mask — now renders at the bottom of the Effects card, under the effect stack, titled MASKS. Adjustment layers keep their local-grade masks the same way (their Effects card carries the block); nulls/cameras/groups still never see mask UI (nothing rasterizes to reveal). A stale saved view of 'masks' falls back to the category home instead of a dead panel. Pure UI relocation — zero data-model change, projects render byte-identical. Verified at 380px: card list shows no Masks card, the Effects sheet shows the stack + MASKS + "+ Add mask" on a shape AND on an adjustment layer, console clean.
- v3.52 — "Move to playhead" exists now (Ezra: pressing the buttons to jump a clip to the playhead did nothing — because no such action existed; the ‹ › caps are drag-trim grips and the only playhead item was Split). Every clip's menu (right-click on PC, ≡ on the clip on mobile) gains "Move to playhead" right under "Split at playhead": the clip jumps so it STARTS at the playhead, with the same semantics as dragging it there — keyframes ride along (times are absolute project time), a multi-selection moves together keeping relative offsets (the pressed clip lands ON the playhead), a group bar carries its members, locked clips refuse with a toast, and the comp duration re-fits. And the OTHER dead button is dead no more: "Split at playhead" silently did nothing when the playhead was outside the clip (or on a camera/group/null) — both cases now toast exactly what to do ("Park the playhead inside the clip to split it"). Verified in-browser: clip at 8s + playhead at 0 → start 0 with keyframes shifted 9,11→1,3; move to mid-timeline works both directions; already-at-playhead is a friendly no-op; split-outside toasts and never spawns a phantom half; console clean.
- v3.53 — Refresh no longer corrupts smooth slow-motion (Ezra: "it says preparing frames each refresh and the slow motion looked DIFFERENT after"). Root cause: on reload, TWO systems seek the same <video> at once — the timeline filmstrip builder and the frame-cache builder — and seekAndPaint resolved on ANY 'seeked' event (or a 500ms give-up timer), so the rebuilt cache captured whichever frame the OTHER build had just landed on: wrong/duplicated frames = visibly altered slow-mo. (Enabling slow-mo by hand was always clean because the filmstrip already existed — only the reload rebuild raced.) Fix: every seek-consumer of a media element now runs through a per-element seek lock (frame cache and filmstrip serialize), seekAndPaint VERIFIES the element actually arrived at the requested time (stale 'seeked' or timeout → one re-seek), and the cache build waits for the element to be decodable before reading its size/duration. The "Preparing frames…" toast on reload is expected (decoded frames live in RAM and can't survive a refresh) — but the rebuild is now guaranteed to reproduce the same frames. Verified with a synthetic hue-sweep webm: clean build vs race-simulated rebuild (strip + cache concurrently on a fresh element) — all 71 frames identical (max hue diff 0). Console clean.
- v3.54 — Tiles re-thought + the move family unchained. TILES (Ezra: "it just turns the clip into smaller versions of itself"): a new Layout selector — "Extend" (the new default) keeps the clip EXACTLY where it is at full size and generates the tile copies OUTWARD around it, filling the frame like AE's Motion Tile; "Grid" is the old n×n look. Gap now defaults to 0 (seamless), and a Mirror selector (ON by default) flips alternate copies so the edges join seamlessly — the "clip keeps going but it's a reflection" trick. Mirror works in Grid mode too. Every EXISTING tiles instance renders byte-identical (no mode/mirror keys stored → render-side fallbacks pin the classic grid; verified pixel-exact incl. the plate path). Tiles now leads the featured carousel. RANGES (Ezra: "wiggle and probably others still not strong enough"): Wiggle effect Amount 600→2400px, Shake Amount 800→2400px, Swing 90→180°, Drift ±400→±1200 px/s, Orbit radius 400→1200px, and the Wiggle + Oscillate BEHAVIOR amounts both to 2400 — everything can now throw a layer multiple screen-heights. Verified in-browser: fresh tiles = Extend/gap 0/mirror ON, original untouched at center (pixel-identical to the old plate path), copies fill all four corners (14.8× ink), mirror measurably flips copies while the original stays put, old grid instances 9-cell identical, determinism 0-diff across renders, console clean.
- v3.55 — PERFORMANCE: shake + Motion Blur went from unbearable to real-time (Ezra: "everything is now unbearably laggy"). Measured at 1080×1920: the Motion Blur effect alone cost 28.5ms/frame — it was a per-pixel 9-tap JavaScript loop over every frame pixel (~19M samples + a full-frame buffer copy); stacked with Shake the frame hit 44ms (~20fps on a fast Mac, single digits on a phone). Two fixes: (1) Motion Blur re-implemented as GPU compositing — the same 9-tap directional mean as 9 accelerated draws using additive 'lighter' at 1/9 each (a successive-alpha first attempt skewed edge pixels opaque and was caught by an edge-ramp probe; the additive version ramps correctly, verified 20→207 across the blur distance). Bonus: the smear now extends naturally past the silhouette edge instead of stopping at it. (2) The per-frame alpha-bounds scan every canvas effect ran (a full ~8MB getImageData readback) now scans a 4×-downsampled copy — 16× less data, box re-padded outward (≤4px looser ROI, invisible). Results: Motion Blur alone 28.5ms → ~0.5ms, Shake+Motion Blur 44ms → 3.3ms (13×), the new Tiles Extend runs at ~1ms. Note: Motion Blur (Content) — the temporal "motionflow" effect — remains inherently heavier (~21ms with shake); it analyses real inter-frame movement and is best kept for hero moments (a future pass could downscale its analysis too). Routing gotcha for the log: POSTFX (compositor line ~910) is the master gate — an effect removed from it silently renders NOTHING (cost me one round). Console clean.
- v3.56 — Mask is now an ADDABLE entry in the Add-Effect browser (Ezra's clarified ask: "when you press add effect, mask should be an addable effect"). A "Mask" tile leads the Matte / Mask / Key category and shows up when you search "mask" (with a punched-hole thumbnail so it reads instantly). Tapping it doesn't create a fake effect instance — it adds a real pen mask to the layer, closes the browser and drops you straight into the mask editor, pen ready. Camera/null/group get a toast explaining masks need pixels. The registry, storage format and importers stay untouched (pure UI entry — no phantom effect type to sanitize). The masks list itself still lives under the Effects card (v3.51) for editing feather/mode/keyframes afterwards. Verified in-browser: Mask leads the Matte grid, search "mask" finds it, tapping adds exactly one mask + opens the editor ("Tap to add mask points" bar on screen), console clean.
- v3.57 — Mobile/PC PARITY (Ezra: "things are in different places, make sure neither version is missing anything"). A 38-agent audit mapped 177 user-facing actions across every surface on both platforms and adversarially verified 22 real gaps. The core fix is structural — ONE menu source each, so the platforms can't drift again: (1) FM.layerMenuItems is now THE layer menu everywhere — desktop right-click on a clip, the desktop top-bar ⋯ with a clip selected, and the mobile ≡ all show the identical list. The desktop-only extras (Flip H/V, Fit/Fill/Stretch to Composition, Create Clipping Mask, Convert to Outline, Extract Audio, Media Info, layer colour tags) merged in, so phones finally get all of them. (2) NEW mobile top-bar ⋯ button = the same project menu desktop's ⋯ shows: Save frame (PNG) without leaving the editor, guides, Loop, Onion skin, Snapping toggle, Split, Trim project, Mark export start/end/clear, Preview speed, timeline zoom, Open/Save/Reset project — seventeen actions a phone previously could not reach at all ("Keyboard shortcuts" is filtered out on touch). (3) Markers: one shared menu with a NEW "Rename marker…" on BOTH platforms, reachable by right-click on PC and long-press on the ruler on touch (rename used to be a desktop-only inline input; clear-all was right-click-only). (4) "Paste keyframes at playhead" added to the layer menu when the keyframe clipboard has content — previously a layer with NO keyframes had no touch path to receive a cross-layer paste. Deliberately not "fixed": timeline-panel height resize (phone layout is fixed by design) and the keyboard-shortcuts overlay (no keyboard on touch). Verified live at 1240px and 380px: identical 23-item layer menus on every surface, the phone ⋯ shows all 17 project actions, ruler long-press serves Rename/Remove, paste item appears with a loaded clipboard, console clean.
- v3.58 — Ezra's notes batch: square canvas, Apple corners, smart snap, SLIP. (1) The preview canvas no longer has rounded corners — the comp shows its true rectangle (a 4px border-radius was cropping corner pixels). (2) NEW "Rounded Corners" effect (leads the featured carousel): masks any layer's rendered bounds with a corner radius up to 400px, with a Corner selector — "Rounded" (circular) or "Apple (continuous)", the iOS superellipse (|x/r|⁵+|y/r|⁵=1) whose curvature eases out of the straight edge first instead of jumping into an arc. Verified against the math: circular boundary lands at r·(1−0.707), the Apple curve at r·(1−0.87) — measured 76px vs 62px at r=90, pixel-exact both. This effect deliberately uses the EXACT (full-res) alpha-bounds scan — the v3.55 fast scan's ~12px slack made the tight Apple curve cut almost nothing (caught by a corner-tip probe). Stroke Color (auto outline) range also raised 16→60px for sticker-fat outlines. (3) SMART SNAP: clip/trim snapping now remembers what the clip was already sitting on when a drag starts and refuses to re-offer that magnet — so after snapping a clip flush against another, a second drag nudges it freely instead of fighting the magnet; every OTHER target still snaps, and a fresh drag from afar snaps to the same edge again (all verified with synthetic drags: 5px nudge escapes, distant drag re-snaps exactly). The snap radius was already zoom-proportional (7 screen px = 7/pps seconds at any zoom). (4) SLIP (Canva-style): the selected video clip grows a centered ⇄ pill when its source has slack — dragging it slides the MEDIA inside the clip while the clip's position and length stay locked (drag right = earlier content, like Canva). Live canvas preview while sliding, filmstrip refreshes on release, ramp-aware rate, clamped to the source ends, undo-able, and pointercancel restores the original offset. Verified with a real webm: start/duration byte-identical through a slip, source offset 1.0s→0.4s. Already existed (from Ezra's notes, no work needed): head/anything TRACKING (video layer → Move & Transform → target button: tap the subject, template-matched keyframes) and auto OUTLINE (Stroke Color effect), and copy/paste effects between layers (Effects card Copy/Paste + Paste Style…). Console clean.
- v3.59 — Ezra's logo is THE logo. His F ▶ M mark (glitch-edged letters, glowing teal chevron — made in FreeMotion itself) replaces the app icon everywhere: icon-512.png + icon-192.png (PWA manifest, both "any maskable"), apple-touch-icon.png (180px, iOS home screen), and the browser-tab favicon (index.html points at icon-192). Resized from his 1080² master with sips; verified the 192px render still reads (the chevron glow carries it). Note: devices cache home-screen icons — an installed PWA shows the new icon after remove + re-add (or an OS icon refresh); browser tabs pick it up on next load.
- v3.60 — Logo v2: Ezra revised the F ▶ M mark (stronger RGB-glitch outlines on the letters — white/red/green/blue fringes that make the F and M read much better at icon size). Same install as v3.59: icon-512 / icon-192 / apple-touch-icon regenerated from the new 1080² master via sips; 192px render eyeballed — the letters now pop instead of melting into the background. Installed-PWA users still need a remove + re-add to refresh the cached home-screen icon.
- v3.61 — SLIP goes premium (Ezra: "visualise it moving + show the rest of the clip in black and white"). While you drag the ⇄ pill, the clip's row now shows the ENTIRE source as a filmstrip: dimmed black-and-white footage extending past the clip on both sides, with the clip's fixed window in full colour framed in teal — and the film visibly slides under the window as you drag (the pro-NLE slip view). Details: the ghost strip is TIME-ACCURATE (each preview frame sits at its true source-time slot, unlike the decorative in-clip strip), slack readouts on each side show exactly how many seconds of unused footage remain ("◂ 0.7s … 1.3s ▸"), the window frame flashes ORANGE when you hit either end of the source, the ⇄ handle is redrawn riding the window centre, and strip frames stream in mid-drag if they weren't built yet. Fix along the way: timeline rebuilds are now deferred during a slip (selectLayer fired a rebuild that tore down the lane holding the ghost — the ghost never appeared; slipDrag joined the clipMove/trimDrag/kfDrag deferral list). Ghost is pointer-transparent, caps its backing canvas at 8192px (iOS), cleans up on release AND pointercancel, and the release rebuild re-crisps the real filmstrip. Verified live mid-drag in-browser (screenshot: grayscale wings + colour window + labels + handle), ghost removed on release, undo restores the pre-slip offset (0.8 → 0.59 → undo → 0.8), console clean.
- v3.62 — The GLASS logo + a real splash. Ezra's detailed glass render of the F▶M capsule mark is now the app icon everywhere (icon-512 / icon-192 / apple-touch-icon / favicon — converted + resized from his 2048² JPEG; the 192px render was eyeballed and the glass still reads beautifully). And his logo ANIMATION is the launch splash: a full-screen overlay plays the 3s video (downscaled 2048→1280² with macOS avconvert, 4.2MB→2.9MB, shipped as splash.mp4) once per SESSION — a fresh tab or a PWA cold launch gets the boot animation; reloads in the same tab skip it (sessionStorage), so editing/refreshing never re-plays it. Fail-proof by construction: tap to skip, 5s hard cap, prefers-reduced-motion skips entirely, and any video error or autoplay refusal dismisses instantly — the editor is never blocked behind the splash. Verified in-browser: the asset loads (3.0s, 1280², real frames, autoplay allowed), the splash ran and self-dismissed on a fresh session (flag set, element removed from DOM), reloads skip it, and the presentation was screenshot-verified (centred glass mark on black, "tap to skip" hint). Console clean.
- v3.63 — Splash v2: full-bleed + mobile-proof (Ezra: "video isn't playing on mobile and doesn't fill the screen / corners"). The splash is now TWO layers of the same video: a blurred, darkened COVER copy fills every pixel of any screen shape (the YouTube-ambient "fill background" look — no black bars, no empty corners on any aspect), with the sharp video contained on top at the screen's short side. Mobile playback hardened: declarative autoplay/muted/playsinline attributes (more reliably honoured by iOS than a JS play() alone), a poster of the glass logo shows INSTANTLY while the video loads, autoplay refusal (iOS Low Power Mode) no longer nuke-dismisses the splash — the poster stands in and a second play() is retried when data lands; only a genuine video ERROR dismisses early. src is still assigned only when the splash actually shows, so skipped sessions cost zero bytes. Verified in-browser: blurred layer measured covering the full viewport (bgCoversAll true, corners filled), desktop and 380px portrait both screenshot-verified (ambient teal fill above/below the sharp square), the real lifecycle ran clean on a fresh session (splash showed, self-dismissed, flag set, app alive), console clean. NOTE for local dev only: python http.server doesn't serve byte-ranges, which real iOS Safari needs for video — his real host (GitHub Pages) does, so on-device playback goes through the hardened path above.
- v3.64 — Splash poster no longer spoils the ending (Ezra: "it shows the final image instead of the simple one"). The loading placeholder was icon-512 — the GLASS render the animation builds up to. New asset splash-poster.png (512², converted from his flat teal mark) is the poster now: while the video loads you see the SIMPLE logo, the animation plays, and it lands on the glass one — the reveal stays a reveal. Verified in-browser: the poster-only loading state screenshot shows the flat mark centred on the splash backdrop, console clean.
- v3.65 — Splash perfected (Ezra: "use the start of the transition as the poster + workflow it until perfect"). (1) The poster is now the video's OWN first frame — captured from splash.mp4 at native 1280² in the engine's browser and measured pixel-identical to live playback frame 0 (max diff 0 across every sampled pixel): the poster→video hand-off cannot jump. Audit-verified crisp: Laplacian sharpness 4.7–19.7× above any softened round-trip of itself; corners blend into the backdrop within 9/255. (2) The 5-agent audit workflow found real bugs, all fixed: tapping the splash fell through the app's capture-phase empty-tap handler and DESELECTED the restored layer (splash joined the KEEP list; verified: splash tap keeps selection, empty tap still deselects) · the post-touch synthesized click punched through the fading overlay into editor controls (preventDefault + the fading splash now swallows events) · the splash markup sat AFTER ~1.5MB of parser-blocking scripts so it painted late on cold loads (now FIRST in <body> — it covers the load instead of following it) · the blurred backdrop was a SECOND 2.9MB video download that kept looping after dismissal (now a static IMG of the first frame — under 46px of blur the motion was imperceptible; halves the cold-launch cost) · keyboard users couldn't skip (Esc/Enter/Space now do) and desktop says "click to skip" · mobile-landscape 100vh overshot the URL bar and cropped the video (100dvh with vh fallback) · a skipped splash still fetched the 365KB poster (poster is script-assigned now — skips are truly zero-byte) · the video keeps downloading after a tap-skip (paused on dismiss) · Android's OS splash colour now matches the app splash (manifest background_color #04080b). Known, accepted: assets are 1280² — a 4K/5K desktop upscales ~1.7× (phones are native-ish); can re-export at 2048 if it ever bothers. Console clean.
- v3.66 — Home screen, Alight-style (Ezra: "prettier, AM layout inspo, no features added or removed, better thumbnails"). LAYOUT: project cards became AM-style LIST ROWS — square rounded thumbnail on the left with the duration as a timecode badge (0:05) and the OPEN badge on the corner, name + meta (aspect · layers) + edited-ago stacked beside it, the ⋯ actions button vertically centred at the row's end; the Projects/Templates tabs are now filled pills (active = teal); the + button moved to the AM position, centred at the bottom. Every feature is exactly where it was: full ⋯ menu (Open/Rename/Duplicate/Select/Save-as-template/Export/Delete), Select mode + bulk bar, Templates tab, top-bar Select and ⋯ — all verified working in the new layout. THUMBNAILS: the poster pipeline was rendering at 180px and single-step downscaling from 1080+ (which skips most source pixels — the mushy cards); now 360px longest side (2× — retina-crisp at the smaller row thumb), PROGRESSIVE halving with high-quality smoothing so every step averages real pixels, and JPEG q0.8 (was 0.6). Verified: stored thumb measured 203×360 at ~4KB, visibly crisp in the row at 380px. Existing projects re-render their thumbnail the next time each is opened (the generator runs on open/save — no bulk migration needed). Console clean.
- v3.67 — Viewing a project no longer bumps it to the top of the home list (Ezra: "projects only go to the top if you actually make a change, if you just view it nothing happens"). The card's `modified` stamp — which is what the home list sorts by — was being rewritten on every `touchCurrent()` call, and opening the home screen calls that unconditionally just to refresh the card's thumbnail. So merely LOOKING at a project reshuffled the library. Now a module-level dirty flag is set only by `FM.storage.autosave()` — the single choke point every real edit funnels through (history commit/undo/redo, template + element inserts) — plus the import path (which calls `save()` directly) and `rename()` (which writes the index itself). `touchCurrent()` stamps `modified` only when that flag is set, then clears it; `history.reset()` (which runs on every open/load/boot) explicitly clears it so the open-time commit can't masquerade as an edit. Everything else `touchCurrent()` does still happens on a view: name, dimensions, duration, layer count and the THUMBNAIL all refresh, so cards stay current — they just don't jump the queue. Verified in-browser with two projects: creating → newest first; opening the older one (real `home.open()` + `projects.open()` path) → order UNCHANGED; one real edit in it → moves to top; undo → still counts (it changes the doc); rename → counts; pin/unpin thumbnail → counts (both end in a history commit). Also proved the thumbnail still regenerates on a pure view (changed the canvas background without committing, opened home, thumbnail bytes changed while `modified` did not). Console clean, home verified at 380px.
- v3.68 — Every project option up front, and a search that forgives you (Ezra: "when creating a new project show all of the edit options you could ever hope for, like fps" + "a magnify glass at the top of home that searches a title or a date, vague is fine — show the closest thing"). NEW PROJECT DIALOG: was a name box and three aspect buttons; now the full set — SIX aspect tiles with real frame previews (9:16 Phone · 16:9 Wide · 1:1 Square · 4:5 Post · 4:3 Classic · Custom), RESOLUTION (720p / 1080p / 1440p / 2160p, always applied to the short side so 1080p portrait is 1080×1920), a free CUSTOM W×H (even-clamped 16–7680, same clamp Canvas settings uses), FRAME RATE (24 film / 25 PAL / 30 / 50 / 60 smooth / 120 / custom 1–120), BACKGROUND (black / white / charcoal / TRANSPARENT / any colour via the picker — verified: transparent really renders alpha 0, a colour renders exactly), and a live "1080 × 1920 · 30 fps" readout that always shows the CLAMPED truth. Picks are remembered for the next new project (localStorage), Enter creates, Cancel discards and reopens clean at the defaults. The card is a scrolling column capped at 88vh so the whole thing works on a 667px-tall phone with the keyboard up. SEARCH: a magnifier by Select/⋯ opens a name-or-date bar. Names match on substring, word-prefix, INITIALS ("sct" → Spin Cut Transition), typo distance ("ozing" → Oozing Text Morph) and subsequence. Dates parse loosely — today, yesterday, this/last week, this/last month/year, "3 days ago", "2026-08-03", "30/7" (day-first, he's in Perth), "2 aug", "august", "aug 2026", a bare year — and match against BOTH created and edited dates, with the card's sub-line switching to "created 3 Aug 2026" to show why it matched. A date only counts as a real match when the project actually falls in the range; near-misses become the "Nothing matched exactly. Closest projects:" fallback, so asking for "today" can't claim last week's work — but you always get the closest few rather than an empty screen. Also: projects now record a CREATION date (backfilled for existing ones) and their fps, so cards show the AM meta set — aspect · resolution · fps · layers. Select-all now ticks only what's currently listed (with a search active, bulk-deleting invisible projects would have been a nasty surprise). Verified in-browser against 6 seeded projects spanning 200 days: 19 queries returning the right rows, all dialog maths and clamps, transparent-background render, defaults resetting on cancel, and the v3.67 view-doesn't-reorder rule still holding. Console clean; checked at 380×820, 380×667 and desktop.
- v3.69 — Adversarial review of v3.68 (16 agents, 27 claims, 3 confirmed) + the fixes it earned. THE BIG ONE: the frame rate never actually applied. `create()` set fps correctly and then `clampProjectDims()` ran a 24/25/30/50/60 WHITELIST over it, so every other value — 120, and every Custom fps — silently became 30 while the dialog cheerfully re-offered it next time. The whitelist is now a 1–120 bound, which is what the editor's own Canvas settings and the save path already allowed; the untrusted-import guard (an .fmotion.json claiming fps 100000) is unchanged, and a 48fps project exported and reimported now survives instead of snapping to 30. Verified: 120/48/12/60/24 all land in the doc AND on the card. DATA-LOSS PATH CLOSED: ticking projects, then searching, left the hidden ticks armed — the bar still said "5 selected" and Delete removed five projects, four of them off-screen. Selection is now pruned to what's actually listed on every render (the Select-all guard only covered the other direction). Also: impossible dates ("31/6", "29/2/25", "2026-02-31", "feb 30") rolled into the next month and were announced as a confident match — they're now rejected, so you get the honest "closest" list instead of a wrong date presented as fact; the created-date backfill ran AFTER the modified bump, stamping every pre-v3.68 project as "created today" on its first edit (now backfilled before); a card with no creation date on record said "created <date>" using the edit date — it says "edited" now, because the app should never invent a fact it doesn't have; search re-rendered the whole grid per keystroke, re-opening IndexedDB once per card (110ms debounce + an in-memory thumbnail cache — thumbs are now in the DOM within one tick of a re-render, no ▶ strobe); the New project dialog auto-focused the name field on phones, throwing the keyboard up and pushing Create/Cancel off a 667pt screen (desktop-only autofocus now, plus Escape and backdrop-tap to close); the magnifier's focus was deferred in a setTimeout, which loses the iOS gesture token and left the keyboard down; 26px tap targets on the swatches and clear button are 34px; inputs are 16px so iOS stops zooming the page on focus; the hint and field labels moved off --text-faint (~3:1) and collapse once you're typing. AND the gap the review found in the feature itself: background was a ONE-WAY choice — nothing in the app could change it after creation. Canvas settings now has the same swatch row (black / white / charcoal / transparent / any colour), verified applying and rendering both ways. Accessibility: aria-pressed on the aspect and background groups, real labels on the custom W/H/fps inputs, role=search, and a live canvas readout. Console clean; re-verified at 380×820 and 380×667.
- v3.70 — Background lives in the settings cog too (Ezra: "make sure the background editor is also a feature in the actual software in the settings cog section where the other formatting settings are"). It already did — the mobile ⚙ and the desktop ⋯ both open the same canvas dialog the swatches went into — but that dialog was still called "Canvas size", which hid the fact it now also holds frame rate and background. Renamed to "Canvas settings" (dialog title, the ⋯ menu entry, and the button tooltips), so the formatting section says what it actually contains. Verified from the cog itself at 380px: swatches present, applying white renders white, black restores.
- v3.71 — New intro video, floating on pure black (Ezra: "make this the new intro video, and make the background just pure black like the edges of this, so it just seems like a floating logo and not a square video"). The new 2048² clip replaces the old 1280² one — and it's actually SMALLER (2.84MB vs 2.96MB), so the splash got sharper and lighter at once. The square is gone because the thing that drew it is gone: there used to be a blurred, brightness-dimmed cover-copy of the first frame filling the screen behind the sharp video, and that ambient glow was exactly what made the video's boundary readable. Now the splash ground is pure #000 and nothing else. Measured the video's own border pixels first — 0,0,0 on all four edges at the first frame AND mid-animation — so the letterbox is mathematically invisible against the page: on a phone the mark sits in black, on a wide screen it fills the height with black to either side, and there's no seam in either. Dropping the backdrop also drops its download. New poster extracted natively (AVFoundation, exact frame 0, no tolerance) at 1280², 285KB — smaller than the 373KB it replaces; measured against the video's own first frame at display sizes it's a mean ~1 level apart with under 1% of pixels past 16 (a decode colour-management offset on hairline speculars, not a visible pop). PWA launch background is #000000 to match, so the app opens black-to-black. Verified live on the real page: plays 2048² through to t=3.00 and dismisses on its own, ground rgb(0,0,0), no backdrop element, console clean, checked at 380×820 and 1280×760.
- v3.72 — Settings panel + a Media library that means you stop opening the picker (Ezra's two AM screenshots: "Add this settings tab… demo mode makes it so when you're adding photos and files it doesn't show, so if you're screen recording to teach someone it won't show" and "on Alight Motion your photos appear straight away in the media section, in our version you have to press a button which opens up the photos app"). SETTINGS lives behind a cog on the home top bar (left of ⋯), a slide-in panel with only the rows that mean something in a local browser editor — Project sorting (Date | Name, drives the home list), Demo mode, Show touches, Show system fonts, Default layer duration. The rows from his screenshot that have no meaning here (Privacy, About, Get Support, socials, Open Source Libraries — there's no account, no analytics and no backend) are simply absent rather than faked. DEMO MODE blanks the previews AND the filenames of your own media in the Add menu, replacing each tile with a neutral "Photo"/"Video" — because a filename leaks as much as a thumbnail on a recording. SHOW TOUCHES draws a ring where you tap, since screen recordings don't capture taps. DEFAULT LAYER DURATION feeds every new photo/text/shape/drawing through one accessor (video clips keep their own length). MEDIA LIBRARY: the honest version of "photos show up straight away". No browser can read the camera roll — there is no API for it on iOS or anywhere, the picker is the only way in, by design — so instead the app now remembers every file you've ever imported and shows them as a thumbnail grid in Add → Media. After the first import your stuff is just there: one tap drops it on the timeline, no picker, no trip through Photos. Blobs are NOT copied — an entry points at the key the import already wrote, so a 200-clip library costs a few KB of index rather than a second copy of every video; pruneOrphans was taught to keep library-referenced keys (verified: deleted EVERY project, ran the sweep, both files still readable and still addable). Thumbnails are generated lazily on first display and cached in IDB. Same file re-imported = same entry, not a duplicate (name+size+mtime). Long-press/right-click forgets a tile without touching projects that use it. A one-time backfill seeds the library from media already sitting in existing projects, so it isn't empty on day one. Verified live at 380px: 2 imports → 2 tiles with real frames, tap → layer added with no picker, demo mode → "Photo/Photo" with zero thumbnails rendered, sort=name reorders A–Z, system fonts off → the font rail drops from 11 cards to just Import, touch ripple appears and self-cleans, and every setting survives a reload. Console clean.
- v3.73 — Fixes from the v3.72 review (24 agents, 5 confirmed), including one I shipped and should have caught. EVERY VIDEO THUMBNAIL WAS A BLACK SQUARE: loadVideoFile resolves at 'loadedmetadata' (readyState 1), and drawing a video that has no decoded frame paints nothing — so the grid baked a solid black JPEG for every clip and cached it in IDB forever. My own verification only used PNGs, which is exactly why I missed it; the rest of the codebase already guards this (frames.js, compositor.js both check readyState < 2 first). Now getThumb waits for a real frame (seeks ~0.1s in, resolves on seeked/loadeddata, 3s cap so the grid can never hang), makeThumb refuses to draw a video below readyState 2, and the cache namespace moved 'libthumb:' → 'libthumb2:' so the black tiles users already have are abandoned AND collectable — the sweep now protects only the new prefix. Verified with a real mp4: 17.4% lit pixels, mean luma 26.6 (was 0.0). THE HEADLINE CLAIM WAS HALF TRUE: I proved pruneOrphans kept library blobs, but FM.projects.remove() deletes a project's media directly and never goes near that keep-set — so deleting the project you imported a file into DID destroy it. My test passed only because I'd deleted the layers first. remove(), storage.clear() and resetProject() now all share the same keep-set; re-verified the honest way (layer still in the project, delete the whole project → blob survived, tile still adds). Also: Replace media swapped the file under a live library key, leaving a tile with the old name that handed back the new footage — replaceMedia now forgets the stale entry (clearing its cached thumbnail) and registers the replacement; re-importing a known file no longer repoints its anchor onto the newest copy, which could drag an entry into a project about to be deleted; and 'Show touches' drew NOTHING under Reduce Motion (the reduced-motion rule collapsed the animation to 0.01ms, so the ring hit opacity 0 before a frame painted) — it now holds still at full opacity then fades, which is visible in a recording with no movement. Console clean.
- v3.74 — "Liquid Glass" theme, colours sampled from our own logo (Ezra: "copy exactly what the colours in our new glass logo are… premium feel like Apple developed it… subtle Liquid Glass… and the plus button needs work"). THE PALETTE ISN'T INVENTED — icon-512.png was analysed pixel by pixel: the ground is #060c0f (57% of the icon), the glass's dominant chromatic family is hue 195° (54% of all glass pixels, vivid #3585a1), shadows drift to ~210° blue, highlights to mint #b9e8e1, and the top 0.5% of luminance is the specular #dbeeea. The UI accent is that exact 195° hue lifted to #5ac7ed for contrast on near-black — which means the app's accent moved off the old teal-green #29d9bb (168°) onto the logo's own cyan. THE + BUTTON took three passes: a solid gradient bead read as a 2010 gel button; a fully translucent lens then read as the DIMMEST thing on an empty home screen, which is wrong for the primary action; the shipped version is Apple's "prominent glass" middle — a lit cyan lens with a real backdrop refraction, one bright rim where light lands, a thin glass edge all the way round, an inner shadow for thickness, and colour thrown softly onto the ground. Also a slow sheen that drifts across it once every 7s (and holds still under Reduce Motion). PERFORMANCE was the design constraint: backdrop-filter is only on chrome over STATIC content (settings panel, sheets, dialogs, menus, the two FABs) — never over the live preview. Audited it rather than assumed: enumerated every element with a non-none backdrop-filter and checked it against the canvas rect, with the add sheet both open and closed — nothing overlaps, so playback compositing is unchanged. REVERSIBLE BY DESIGN, because he asked for that first: the whole theme is one new file (theme-glass.css) scoped entirely to html[data-theme="glass"], and Settings → Appearance → Classic flips the attribute back, restoring the original stylesheet exactly (verified: --bg returns to #0e1320, --accent to #29d9bb, and the FAB's backdrop-filter reads "none" again). styles.css was not modified. Glass is the default so it can actually be judged.
