The sheet is now DERIVED from the selection on every inspector refresh, which is the one call all of them already make, so the whole class is closed including anything added later. A deliberate swipe-down still stays down until you select something else. Multi-select was its own case of the same bug: the sheet was gated on the single-clip editing mode, so selecting two layers hid the Group, trim-all and align bar behind a closed panel — those are separate states now. And a selectedId whose layer has gone (delete, undo, leaving a group, a project load) is cleared at the top of the refresh rather than leaving the app believing something is selected while the panel falls through to the Add menu. The panel also returns to the top of its scroll when the layer or the category changes, so a short panel can never open already scrolled past its own content. Verified all four routes at 380px: select normally, set the id directly and call refreshAll, multi-select, and pull a layer out from under a live id.
- v4.18 — The Move & Transform scrub strips move, and a flick glides (Ezra: "the sliders for size and stuff in the move and transform section are stiff and don't actually show any movement, they should show it moving and also should glide slightly, like how the timeline does when you do a hard swipe"). The strip was a painted-on ruler that never moved: only the number above it changed, so dragging it felt like pushing against a wall, and a fast drag was worth no more than a slow one, which meant a big size change took several strokes. THE RULER NOW TRAVELS under the fixed centre line as you drag, both rulings together, tiled so it never runs out. AND A FLICK KEEPS GOING: release above 0.6 px/ms and the value carries on and eases to a stop on the same friction curve the timeline's momentum uses, so one hard swipe covers what used to take four. Below that it stops dead where you let go — a deliberate positioning drag runs well under 0.5 px/ms and has to land exactly where you put it. Verified with three gestures at the same 240px distance: 4 px/ms glides from 1.96 to 3.16 and decelerates on the way, 0.3 px/ms stops dead at 1.96, 0.06 px/ms stops dead at 1.96. The whole gesture is ONE undo step, not one per frame. Also caught while testing: a glide outliving its own control. The inspector rebuilds constantly — refresh, category change, deselect — which detaches the strip mid-glide, and its closures happily went on writing to the old layer's property from a control no longer on screen. It dies with the element now.
- v4.19 — Vector drawing gets a precision pad and snaps to the points already down (Ezra: "the vector drawing should have the ability to have the same type of touch pad like move and transform for moving it around, so you can precisely decide where each point is then press add point when it's in the right position, it should also have grid snapping to the other points"). Placing a point used to mean tapping the canvas, and a fingertip on a phone is worth about 3.5 project pixels per screen pixel — you could not put a corner anywhere exact, and you could not see where it was going to land before you committed it. There is a CURSOR now: a crosshair ring that sits where the next point will go, with a dashed rubber band back to the last one so the segment you are about to make is visible before it exists. The TRACKPAD moves it at the same gain as Move & Transform (project width / 640, about 1.7 project px per finger px), which is twice as fine as touching the canvas, and + ADD POINT commits it. SNAPPING is per-axis and independent: the cursor locks to the row of one earlier point and the column of another at the same time, which is exactly what it takes to build a square corner or a level edge, and the alignment is drawn right across the frame in accent green so you can see what you locked onto rather than guessing. Landing on the first point closes the shape, the same as tapping it. The quick route is untouched — tapping the canvas still drops a point immediately, runs through the same snapping, and parks the cursor on what it just placed so you can carry on nudging from there. Verified: the cursor seeds at the frame centre and is drawn before any point exists (874 overlay pixels with an empty shape), 40 finger px moves it 68 project px, a cursor brought within range of one point's X and another point's Y locks to exactly (300, 1200) and lights 2,665 accent pixels of guide, and 60 project px out it stays exactly where it was put.
- v4.20 — The move pad tells you when it has snapped (Ezra: "there should be an indicator of grid snapping when you are using the move and transform move pad"). The pad has snapped to the frame centre, the frame edges and this layer's own earlier keyframe positions since v4.07, but it did it silently — the layer just stopped somewhere and you had to guess whether it had actually caught anything or you had simply stopped moving. Three things happen now, all at once: the axis that locked draws its rule across the pad, the pad picks up an accent ring, and the hint text NAMES what it caught ("Snapped to centre + middle", "Snapped to left edge", "Snapped to a keyframe"). The matching alignment guide also flashes on the CANVAS — that line already existed and the X/Y value boxes already asked for it, but the pad never did, so the one control that snaps the most was the one showing the least. A short haptic tick fires when it catches, once per catch rather than every frame it stays caught. Verified: dragging a layer from (300, 700) onto the centre lands it at exactly (540, 960), the pad carries both snap classes, both rules render at full opacity (2px wide, full 124px height), the hint reads "Snapped to centre + middle", both canvas guides are visible, and moving 120px off any target clears every one of them.
- v4.21 — The heart is redrawn from Ezra's reference. Four things separate that drawing from the old one, and all four are what make it read as a heart rather than a blob: the NOTCH is shallower (0.245 down the box, not 0.31) so the two lobes nearly meet in a small sharp V instead of a deep gouge; the LOBES sit higher and fuller (0.02, and they reach the full width of the box at 0 and 1 rather than stopping at 0.03/0.97); the POINT reaches the very bottom (1.0, not 0.96); and there is a new pair of anchors on the lower flanks. Those last two exist for a real reason — with only six points the widest point's handle had to serve both the dome above it and the long run down to the tip, so a handle long enough to make that run straight also fattened the lobe. Eight points now, mirror-exact about the centre line, and Edit Points gains two more handles on the part of the outline anyone actually reshapes. The Add-menu tile is traced from the same eight anchors, so the icon and the shape it makes cannot drift apart. Measured off the real traced path: the centre notch bites 0.24 down, the widest row is at 0.31 and spans the full box, and the outline tapers 200 → 27 → 0 px through the bottom fifth.
- v4.22 — Drawing takes over the whole screen (Ezra: "when you enter freehand drawing it should make the canvas fill the screen so you can see it larger"). Neither the timeline nor the inspector can be used mid-stroke, and on a phone the two of them were eating about 60% of the height — you were drawing into a postage stamp and then wondering why the line was rough. Entering either drawing mode now hands the window to the stage, the same takeover the text editor already does. MEASURED at 380px: the canvas goes from 175x312 to 365x649, which is 2.1x in each direction and 4.3x the actual drawing area. The stage keeps a margin for the floating bar, deeper in vector mode because that bar carries the trackpad — verified the bar never overlaps the canvas in either mode. Both the canvas backing store and the drawing overlay are re-measured the moment the layout changes, so strokes land exactly where the finger is rather than at the old scale, and leaving the tool puts everything back (312px tall before, 312px after).
- v4.23 — Deselecting a multi-selection no longer strands the app (Ezra: "I selected three layers and un selected them but could[n't] get rid of the group merge options and I couldn't do anything"). The `sel-multi` body class is what shows the Group button — and, less obviously, what HIDES the ⋯ clip menu, the settings button and the project menu, since none of those apply to a set of layers. It was toggled in exactly one place, inside refreshAll. But selectLayer() never calls refreshAll, and selectLayer(null) is what a tap on the background, Escape, and the phone's back arrow all do — so deselecting left the class stuck: the Group button still there with nothing selected, and the three buttons you would use to get out of it still hidden. Which is precisely "I couldn't do anything". The chrome is now derived by ONE function that recounts the live selection rather than trusting its caller, and every path that can change a selection calls it — including the two places in the timeline that were setting the classes by hand for the same reason. Select-mode itself also ends there whenever the selection empties. Verified with three layers selected: Group shown, settings and project menu hidden; then deselecting through selectLayer(null) — Group gone, settings and project menu back, both body classes and select-mode cleared.
- v4.24 — Elements become a real section, and the + button stops lying (Ezra: "elements should be a new thing we have… it will have its own dedicated section like projects and templates in the main menu, and what elements are for is basically let's say you have a watermark or logo, and you want to quickly apply it to an edit and not remake it or go find it and copy paste, you can just save it as an element then find it in the element section" · "the templates and elements sections should actually work when you press the create button, rn if you press the create button it creates a project not a template"). HOME gets a third tab. An element card leads with ADD TO THE OPEN PROJECT, because that is the entire point of the thing — a template's card leads with "new project", and the difference between them is exactly the difference between the two ideas. Cards carry the source project's thumbnail and a layer count, and the same forgiving name search the other tabs have. THE + BUTTON is per-tab now: on Projects it opens the new-project dialog as always, on Templates it asks which project to save as a template, on Elements which to save as an element. Both of those are made FROM a project, so both routes pick one — and a + that quietly made a project while you were looking at a list of templates was the bug. Saving a whole project as an element is new (FM.elements.saveFromProject): media comes out of IndexedDB for a project that isn't open, which the existing selection-based save could never do since it only knew the in-memory map. THE ADD MENU tab is now called "Elements" rather than "Object / Element", and gains two entries: EMPTY GROUP — grouping otherwise needs two layers selected first, so there was no way to make the container and then fill it, which is the order you work in when you know a section is coming — and SAVE SELECTION AS ELEMENT, which was previously buried in a ⋯ menu two levels away from the elements themselves. Verified end to end: the tab renames, both new entries appear, an empty group creates/renders/rebuilds/deletes with no error, all three + buttons do their own thing, saving a project as an element produces a card with its thumbnail and "1 layer", and inserting that element back into a project adds its layer and selects it.
- v4.25 — The camera gets Camera Options: a lens, a focus plane and fog (Ezra, from three Alight Motion screens: "add all of these options to the camera… to access these options there will be a button like the effects button but just for when you have the camera selected"). Select the camera and there is a second card beside Move & Transform, opening onto three sub-screens behind an icon rail. CAMERA VIEW is FIELD OF VIEW and DISTANCE. The focal length the compositor has always used is 2x the project height, which through the ordinary pinhole relation is a 28.1 degree lens — a long one, and the reason Z parallax has always been so subtle. That is exactly what the control reads on a camera nobody has touched, so nothing moves until you drag it; open it toward 90 and the focal length drops to a quarter, and every depth effect firms up together (layer scale with Z, camera parallax, focus falloff) because all of them divide by f. Distance is the camera's own Z rather than a second field holding the same idea — so it was already keyframable and already wired into parallax. FOCUS BLUR defocuses each layer by how far its Z sits from the focus plane, with a depth-of-field width that stays sharp and a strength that ramps past it and caps three widths out. It rides the one filter hook every layer type already passes through, so it costs no buffer, and the radius is divided by the layer's own perspective scale — otherwise a far layer, already shrunk by Z, would blur LESS the further away it got. FOG washes layers toward a colour by their depth between a near and a far plane; it goes on a plate and composites source-atop so the wash clips to the layer's own shape and text stays text rather than getting a tinted rectangle. EVERYTHING IS OFF BY DEFAULT AND PROVABLY SO: an untouched camera resolves to focal length 3840, which is the legacy 2x height to the digit, and 28.0725 degrees round-trips back to 3840 exactly. Measured on a two-depth scene: opening the lens to 90 degrees moves 31,920 bytes and removing the field again is byte-identical; focus blur moves 38,749 and switching it off is byte-identical; fog moves 16,380 and switching it off is byte-identical. A hostile import cannot break it either — NaN, Infinity and 0 degrees all fall back to the legacy lens rather than NaN-ing every transform in the scene, and the whole camera block is rebuilt from a schema on import. Also caught in testing: the new card appeared on shapes, text, media and groups, because every non-camera branch of the category list is built by blacklist and a new entry leaks into all of them; it is taken back out explicitly now.
- v4.26 — The multi-select panel stops repeating the top bar (Ezra: "having multiple layers selected should not look like this, it doesn't need the bin button there as it's at the top, it should display all the options like effects and stuff, and it shouldn't say group three layers there, the group button is at the top right"). A full-width "Group 3 layers" banner and a red bin sat above everything else — and both already appear in the top bar the moment a second layer is selected, so the panel was spending its first two rows saying what the bar had just said, and pushing the actual property cards (Effects, Blending, Move & Transform) down past the fold. Both are gone. What is left is the two things with nowhere else to live: the clip actions for the whole selection, and the timeline alignment. The first property card now sits 148px into the panel instead of below the bottom of the screen, and all nine cards are reachable with three layers selected. Group and delete are unchanged — top right, where he pointed.
- v4.27 — Export settings are remembered (Ezra: "make it so whatever quality settings you last used are remembered for when you make a new project"). Format, resolution, frame rate and quality lived only in the dialog's own DOM, so they survived reopening it and nothing else — a new project, a reload or a second device put you back on Full/30fps/default and you re-picked the same four things every time. They persist now, and apply to any project including a brand-new one. RESOLUTION is stored as the target SHORT SIDE rather than the scale factor the exporter actually uses, because a scale only means anything relative to one comp: 0.5 remembered from a 4K project would quietly mean 540p in a 1080p one. Verified: choosing 720p / 60fps / low quality and exporting stores {short:720, fps:60}, reopening the dialog comes back on 720p 60fps, and a 360x640 project — which cannot reach 720p at all — falls back to Full while still keeping 60fps.
- v4.28 — Duplicate finally means all of them (Ezra: "when I selected multiple stuff I can't duplicate all the stuff I have selected, I have to manually duplicate each thing"). Every duplicate entry point — the layer ⋯ menu, the top-bar menu, the phone's duplicate button and Cmd/Ctrl-D — ran on the primary layer only, so a five-layer selection meant five trips through the menu. They all go through the selection now. A group already brings its whole subtree, so a descendant that is ALSO selected is skipped rather than copied twice; the copies end up selected, so the next thing you do lands on them and not the originals; and the labels count ("Duplicate 3 layers"). It is ONE undo step, not one per layer — duplicateLayer commits history itself, which would have made reversing a single button press take three presses of undo. Verified: three selected layers become six, all three copies are the new selection, and one undo takes it back to three.
- v4.29 — The four shipped effect looks are out of Presets (Ezra: "in the presets menu remove the effects in there, like dreamy and shit"). VHS Glitch, Duotone, Dreamy and Comic were starters from back when there were a few dozen effects and the point was to show what stacking them could do. With 175 effects and a browser that groups and searches them, four fixed looks you could not delete, sitting on top of the presets you actually made, were just clutter. Gone. Saving and applying your OWN effect-stack presets is untouched — verified: the panel lists nothing until you save one, then lists exactly that one, and no built-in chips remain.
- v4.30 — The anchor point shows on the canvas while you place it (Ezra: "when placing the anchor for movement it should show you where it is on the canvas"). Anchor mode moved the pivot with no feedback at all: you nudged the pad, the layer did not move — correctly, since moving the pivot is not moving the layer — and the only way to discover where it had landed was to scale the layer and watch which way it flew. A crosshair-in-a-ring now marks it, in the keyframe amber so it reads over any layer colour, and only while Anchor mode is open. It needs no maths of its own — transform.x/y IS the anchor, so it sits exactly where the selection box's own transform-origin already is and the two can never disagree. Verified: hidden in Move mode, shown in Anchor, dead on the box centre with a centred anchor, and dead on the corner once the anchor is moved there.
- v4.31 — a shape's timeline clip now wears that shape's own colour: red shape, red bar. The hue is kept and only the lightness is re-tuned (measured against the white clip label, so a pale yellow shape gives a readable olive bar rather than a washed-out one, and a black/white shape stays neutral instead of being handed an invented hue). Gradient fills take the first stop, outline-only shapes take their stroke. A clip colour that was chosen deliberately still wins. Also fixed: an outline-only shape's layer thumbnail was drawing the (invisible) fill colour instead of the stroke it actually renders with.
- v4.32 — the Tiles Mirror and Layout buttons work again (and both ways). Their choices were written as a plain label list, which the panel read one CHARACTER at a time: the buttons were labelled "f"/"n" and "x"/"r", and pressing one stored NaN — which matches no option, so mirroring switched off and could never be switched back on. Same bug in Shake's Direction. Fixed once in the schema normalizer, so it can't come back for the next effect written that way. A segment now also highlights the option that is actually rendering, including for effects saved before the option existed.
- v4.33 — the lag fix. The adaptive playback quality was real but disconnected from the thing that actually costs: effect plates were allocated at PROJECT resolution no matter how small the preview canvas was, so shrinking the canvas bought ~7%. Effect plates now follow the preview's scale, and the preview is allowed to render below project resolution when it is DISPLAYED smaller (a 1080x1920 comp on a phone was painting 2.1 megapixels to show 0.1). One layer with six effects: 11 fps -> 39 fps for a still frame on a phone, 10 fps -> 107 fps at the low playback tier. Export and a 1:1 preview are byte-identical — verified across all 175 effects. Effects sized in absolute pixels (Border, Halftone, Dots, Curl, Turbulent Displace, Tile Rotate, Mirror Tile, Wiggle, Shake, Drift, Orbit, Block Dissolve, Iridescence) now carry the scale so the preview still matches the export. Playback quality 'Detail' keeps the old full-resolution preview.
- v4.34 — dragging gets the same treatment as playing. The quality drop only ever applied while the timeline was PLAYING, so scrubbing the playhead or dragging a layer with heavy effects stayed at full resolution and stuttered. Motion is now inferred from the render funnel itself (a burst of frames in one window IS a drag, whatever caused it), so it covers every drag path without a flag being wired into each one. Measured on a six-effect layer: a sustained drag settled at 6.95ms/frame from ~19ms, and the preview repaints sharp the moment you let go. On a machine with headroom the ladder stays at full quality and nothing changes.
- v4.35 — Tiles can repeat the WHOLE clip, not just the part on screen. It sampled the composited frame, so a clip dragged past the edge had already been cropped and the effect faithfully repeated the surviving sliver. A new Repeat option renders the layer into a plate that extends past the frame first (reusing the same origin machinery the zoomed-in viewport crop uses) and tiles that. Measured on a clip with 70px of 300 left on frame: 23 sliver bands before, 6 full-size copies after. Old projects are untouched — Repeat defaults to "On screen" for any Tiles saved before this, byte-identical. Also fixed: bringing a clip to the playhead from the RIGHT threw it forward by its own length; the near edge now meets the playhead, for one clip and for a whole selection.
- v4.36 — the timeline colours stop being ugly. Bars were being darkened until a fixed white label was readable on them, which turns any yellow into olive mud. Now the bar keeps the shape's real hue and saturation, and the LABEL adapts instead — dark ink on bright bars, white on dark ones, picked by measuring both rather than by a threshold, so in-between hues like hot pink and mid orange get the better of the two. Worst label contrast across a 14-hue sweep went from 3.27 to 4.61 while every bar got more colourful, not less. Spawned shapes also pick their colour by walking the hue wheel (golden angle) instead of drawing a fresh random each time, so a stack of shapes reads as one palette: eight in a row now sit at least 32 degrees apart instead of possibly landing on top of each other.
- v4.37 — the + button's glyph is centred, for real this time. The button box was always dead centre (which is why it measured perfect here and looked wrong on Ezra's phone) — it was the + GLYPH inside it that sat low, because it was a text character riding whatever font the device resolved. Measured off his screenshot: circle 186x186, perfectly round, 0px off the screen centre, glyph 9 device pixels below the circle's centre. It is now an SVG path symmetric about its own centre, like the editor's + already was, so no font can move it. Also moved "Save as template…" up under Duplicate in a project's ⋯ menu — the two both make a new thing out of the project, and Ezra asked for a feature that was already there, which is a findability problem.
- v4.38 — the ⋯ menu works again on a selected layer. It has been dead since v4.28: that release added "Duplicate N layers" labels that read selCount at the top of the menu, but selCount was declared 36 lines further down, and reading a const before its declaration throws. FM.layerMenuItems threw on every call, so the ⋯ button did nothing on mobile AND right-clicking a clip or a track head did nothing on desktop. Declaration moved to the top of the function.
- v4.39 — freehand and vector drawing land where you actually draw. The draw tool converted touches using the canvas's raw backing size, which is only the same as the project when the render scale happens to be exactly 1. It is not: the preview is supersampled on a high-DPR phone and reduced since the preview-resolution work. Measured, tapping the dead centre of the preview: at scale 1.19 the point was recorded 103x183px away, at 1.5 it was 270x480px away, at 0.49 it was 275x489px away. Now converted through __fmRS and the crop origin, the same maths canvas-edit already used — exact at every scale, and correct on a zoomed, cropped preview too. The overlay also re-syncs whenever the canvas is re-allocated, which the adaptive quality tier now does the moment a drag starts — i.e. mid-stroke.
- v4.40 — the same coordinate bug swept out of five more on-canvas tools. Free crop, the point editor, the mask tool, motion paths and the tracker each rolled their own (canvas.width / rect.width) conversion, which is only right when the preview happens to be exactly 1:1 with the comp. All five now go through one shared FM.eventToProject / FM.previewDispScale that honours the render scale and the crop origin — verified exact at scale 1, 1.19, 0.49, 2 and on a cropped preview, and the point editor's hit test now lands on the corner it is told to. The eyedropper and the touch-up tool were already correct (one samples canvas pixels on purpose, the other works in percentages) and are untouched.
- v4.41 — the two motion blurs are finally two different, findable things. The effect called "Motion Blur" never read motion at all — nine copies smeared along an angle you set by hand — so it is now called Directional Blur, and the footage one is Motion Blur (Footage). Both carry a plain-English line saying what they do, shown above their controls and in the effect browser; any effect can now declare one. And the REAL transform motion blur, which has been unreachable since v2.66 (its controls were pulled out of Edit Shape and never re-homed, so nothing in the app could switch it on), now lives in Move & Transform with Shutter and Samples. It also stops charging for nothing: a clip that is not moving is detected exactly, from the transform stack itself, and renders byte-identically to blur-off instead of averaging eight identical copies (0.025ms instead of eight full renders). A keyframed Shutter or Samples used to evaluate to NaN and make the layer vanish; fixed.
- v4.42 — the numbered 1-9 cards are gone from a multi-selection. Every one of them is a single-layer editor: it builds its panel from the primary clip and writes to that one alone, so with three clips selected they promised to edit the selection and quietly edited one. A multi-selection now shows only what genuinely applies to all of it — Edit N clips (move / extend / trim / split) and Align on timeline. Opening a category and THEN selecting more clips also drops back, instead of leaving a single-layer panel open over a multi-selection.
- v4.43 — every scrubber glides, not just the Move & Transform pad. The momentum written for that pad in v4.18 is now a shared helper attached to all three scrub controls: the effect/keyframe ruler (so every effect parameter has it), the draggable number boxes, and the pad itself. Flick it and it keeps travelling and eases out like the timeline; drag it slowly and it stops dead where you let go, because a control that drifts after release cannot set an exact number. Measured on a blur radius: a 3px/ms flick moved 10.5 during the drag and 25.5 more on the glide, a 6px/ms flick ran to the parameter's ceiling and stopped there rather than spinning, and a 0.125px/ms positioning drag glided zero. Tapping a number still opens the type-in editor without any drift.
- v4.44 — the top-bar button next to the bin sets PARENTING, which is what the Alight Motion screenshot it came from was actually showing. It had been read as a duplicate button; duplicate already lives on the transport row and in the layer ⋯ menu, so nothing is lost. The picker also got the thing that made AM's version usable: every candidate shows its own thumbnail, so you attach to the layer you can see rather than to one of three things called "Copy Copy 2". Verified by measurement — after parenting, moving the parent 400px moves the child 400px. (One picker, not two: an FM.openParentPicker already existed in app.js, so the new one was deleted rather than left to shadow it.)
- v4.45 — four real defects out of Motion Blur (Footage). Switching Style to Echo while paused made the layer VANISH — the accumulator is created empty and the same-frame shortcut handed that blank canvas straight out; measured 0 visible pixels before, 19,600 after. With onion skin on it never blurred at all, because the ±0.2s ghost renders walked the per-layer time record back and forth until it reset on every paint; the ghosts now pass through untouched. The default Pixel style softened parts of the picture that never moved — it does its work on a 480px canvas and stretched the WHOLE thing back over the frame, so "static pixels stay razor sharp" was simply untrue; static pixels are now punched out and the sharp full-resolution frame shows through, measured 0.997 → 1.000 of the un-blurred edge contrast. Moving edges also stop picking up a dark fringe (taps are weighted by their own alpha instead of averaging transparent black into the colour). Plus: the Smear style stopped freeing and reallocating an 8MB canvas every single frame, and the frame cache is cleared when you switch project instead of growing for the whole session.
- v4.46 — the AI demo stops being about real estate. The "Preview (no key)" run was hardwired to EXAMPLES[1], which was the real-estate prompt, so the demo everyone sees generated an address-and-price scene. That example is now an event intro, the demo runs the FIRST example rather than a hardcoded index (so reordering the list can't silently change what it shows), and the property wording is out of the schema hint too. Checked across all four AI files: no property vocabulary left.
- v4.47 — Captions moved onto the add menu's quick rail, directly under Text. It was buried in the Media tab next to Import and Sample clip, which is the wrong shelf: it spawns instantly like everything on the rail, and it is a text tool. Rail is now Text · Captions · Freehand Drawing · Vector Drawing, and Shift+1..4 reaches all four (the shortcut was capped at three).
- v4.48 — the Elements tab is a searchable, scrolling list instead of a sideways pager. Elements were never lost: they were on page 2, 3, 4 of a horizontal pager you had to know to swipe, nine at a time, behind four built-in entries — so with a handful saved you'd see one of yours and assume the rest hadn't saved. That tab now has a search box and shows every element in one scrolling grid. The pager stays for Shapes and Media, where a fixed catalogue you learn by sight is exactly what it's good at. Verified with 14 elements: all 19 entries render, search filters, nothing hidden.
- v4.49 — Motion Blur (Footage) is no longer offered on a group, and is ignored on any project that already had one there. A group reaches the effect stack already flattened, with its own transform baked into the pixels, so there is nothing left for the content blur to neutralise — dragging the group smeared it, which is precisely the opposite of what the effect promises. Put it on the clips inside the group instead. Verified: dragging a blurred group now changes zero pixels versus the same drag with the effect off.
- v4.50 — every effect now has a description and tags, shown when you HOLD its tile, and search reads both. Holding an effect leads with a sentence saying what it does and a row of keyword chips, then its presets — the panel Ezra asked for. 68 of the 175 have a hand-written line; the rest state their family and controls truthfully rather than inventing prose, because a wrong description is worse than a plain one. Tags are derived from the label, category and every control the effect exposes, so searching "shutter" finds Motion Blur (Footage) and "smear" finds Directional Blur, Shake and the footage blur — none of which carry those words in their names.
- v4.51 — you can now CREATE an element, not only turn existing work into one. The Elements + offers "Build a new one…", which makes a square, transparent canvas — the shape a watermark, logo or sticker actually wants — and drops you into it; and every project's ⋯ gained "Save as element…" beside "Save as template…", so the round trip is one tap at each end. Verified: building one creates a 1080×1080 project with a transparent background and opens it.
- v4.52 — transform motion blur rebuilt as re-projection. It used to render the layer N times, once per shutter slice; it now rasterises the layer ONCE and pushes that plate through the matrix delta D = M(tau)·M(t)⁻¹ for each slice — one drawLayer and N cheap blits. That also makes "transform blur ignores the footage" true by construction rather than by comment: the content is decoded once, so a reversed or frame-blended clip can no longer hand a different frame to each sub-sample. Slice count follows the actual travel (1.5 device px per slice) instead of being a flat 8. Verified: an 80px shape whipping across at 75px/frame measures 106px wide at half shutter and 132px at full — the smear scales with the shutter exactly as it physically should — a static layer stays byte-identical to blur-off, and a moving one costs 1.6x the un-blurred draw instead of 8x.
- v4.53 — effect thumbnails now show the effect, not the same ball 175 times. Every tile used to apply its effect to one flat teal circle, which has no tonal range, no straight lines and no texture — so Brightness, Contrast and Saturation rendered pixel-similar tiles, every warp was a slightly different ball, and all eighteen 3D solids were one teal blob. Each effect now names the subject that demonstrates IT: a mini landscape with a full 0-255 luma range for anything tonal, a 64px card of it where the effect works on the layer's EDGE (stroke, shadow, corners, repeat, 3D), a lattice of straight rules and rings for the warps, letters for the six text effects, the green-screen shot for keying, and the ball only where a clean silhouette really is the point. That also exposed ~40 tiles that were rendering nothing at all: Tile Shift and Tile Rotate default to a 120px tile on a 96px thumbnail (one cell — an exact no-op), Wave's 38px wavelength is a 239px period (under half a hump), Mirror Tile's 140px cell is bigger than the whole frame, Displacement Map had no map to displace by, Matte Choker's 4px choke and Timecode's eight glyphs were invisible or overflowing, and Shake's 120px throw hurled the subject off the tile. All fixed per effect. Verified against a 175-tile contact sheet and in the real browser at 375px.
- v4.54 — two effects that were genuinely missing: **Levels** and **Halation**. Levels sets where black and white actually fall — input black/white, gamma, output black/white, per channel or all three — which is the first move on any flat or washed-out clip and the only way to kill a colour cast at its source. It runs on an adjustment layer too, so it can grade everything below it. Halation is the warm red bleed film gets around blown highlights, because light scatters off the back of the base and re-exposes the emulsion: two radii at once, a tight core hugging the highlight and a wide low wash. That second radius is exactly what Glow, Light Glow and Soft Glow structurally can't do, which is why none of them read as film. Measured at 1080×1920: Levels 7.98ms and Halation 4.9-7.4ms, against Solarize 8.69, Gamma 9.5 and Vignette 10.3 — both sit at or below the cost every per-pixel effect already pays. Verified byte-identical to the previous build on the adjustment path and on a mixed pixel/warp/canvas stack, so nothing existing moved.
- v4.55 — **Frame Stutter** and **Shockwave**. Frame Stutter holds each frame for 1/rate of a second instead of playing every one: the stop-motion / anime step, with Strobe (blank the gap) and Hold + Trail (a ghost of the frame before) as well as plain Hold. The rate is keyframable, so 24 → 6 → 24 is two keyframes. Shockwave is one expanding pressure ring that travels out and leaves frame — keyframe the Radius and that is the entire move. It is not Circular Ripple, which is an infinite centre-locked standing sine; this is a single band, with the wake behind the crest stronger than the front (that asymmetry is what reads as pressure rather than as a bulge) and a per-channel chroma split through the front. Cost tracks the ring, not the frame: only the annulus is walked, so once the wave has passed the corners it costs nothing. Verified: at 4fps on a 30fps timeline a drifting square holds 8 frames per step and shows 2 positions where smooth motion shows 12; Strobe blanks exactly the back half of each step; the ring lands at 71-131px for a radius of 102 and a band of ±30, and nothing outside it moves; Frame Stutter costs 0.03ms at 1080×1920. Byte-identical to the previous build across ten effect stacks × five times each.
- v4.56 — **Speed Lines** and **HSL Bands**. Speed Lines draws tapered manga impact strokes in from the frame edge and leaves a soft clear disc around your subject, so the face still reads: count, weight, length, scatter, spin, focus point, ink colour, and an Add mode for white light-streaks. Radial Rays is a per-pixel cosine wash — this is drawn geometry, and the taper is the whole difference between ink and light. HSL Bands is the panel every other editor has and this one didn't: pick a colour band (or set your own centre and width) and retune just its hue, saturation and brightness. The trick that makes it usable is weighting by each pixel's own saturation, so neutrals and skin stay put while the sky moves — verified: pushing the Blue band +45° took the sky from cornflower to indigo and left the green field, the skin tone and the red jacket byte-identical. Speed Lines costs 1.27ms (against 1.32ms with no effect at all — it's free), HSL Bands 6.8ms, and its early-out means most of the frame never reaches the HSL round trip. Byte-identical to the previous build across thirteen effect stacks.
- v4.57 — **Time Warp Scan** and **Chroma Key Pro**. Time Warp Scan sweeps a bar across the frame and everything behind it stays frozen at the instant it was crossed — the TikTok scan. Reveal flips it: the picture is held still and the bar wipes the live frame in. Direction, sweep time, bar width and glow, and a Loop so it repeats. Verified with a bar drifting sideways: at the halfway point row 20 shows it at x 26-61, row 60 at 50-84, row 110 at 78-112, and every row the scan bar has not reached yet shows it live at 82-117 — a clean staircase of held moments, one bar per row. Chroma Key Pro is the green screen key that survives lighting you did not control: it measures colour only and throws brightness away, so a shadowed corner of the screen keys exactly like a lit one. On a screen lit 6:1 across the frame it removed 100% of both halves and kept 100% of the subject; the existing Chroma Key removed the lit half and none of the dark half at its default, and pushing its tolerance up to 0.75 finally cleared the dark half but took the whole subject with it. It also despills the green rim, desaturates what is left of the fringe, has a Matte view for checking your edges, and works on any layer rather than media only.
- v4.58 — **Light Wrap**, the shot that makes a key look composited instead of pasted on. Every glow in the app blooms the layer's own pixels; none of them can see what is underneath. This one reads the layers below and bleeds their light around your subject's edge, which is what a real lens does and what your eye is unconsciously checking when a composite looks wrong. Put it above Chroma Key Pro on a keyed subject and the background's colour creeps around the hair and shoulders. Reach sets how far inside the edge it comes, Softness how blurred the borrowed light is, and Screen/Add how it lands. Verified on a dark subject over a warm backdrop: the rim gained 56.8 luma, the deep interior gained 2.7, and nothing outside the subject changed at all — the band is doing exactly what it should. Costs 1.0ms against 0.55ms for the same frame with no effect.
- v4.59 — you can leave an Add-Effect sub-menu by tapping anywhere empty. Open a category, or hold a tile for its preset sheet, and any tap that does not land on a tile or a button backs you out one level — the ‹ Back button is a long reach with one thumb and it used to be the only way out. It only fires on a real TAP: a drag is a scroll and leaves the list alone, and a hold is a hold. Verified all five cases — tapping empty space closes, dragging 90px does not, holding 800ms does not, tapping a tile or a category arrow does not, and the preset sheet behaves the same as the category grid.
- v4.60 — **Dispersion** and **VHS Tape**. Dispersion is the disintegrate transition: the layer tears apart along a noise front and blows away in one direction, glowing at the leading edge. Keyframe Progress 0 → 1 and that is the whole move. Dissolve and Block Dissolve are alpha-only — they punch holes and the content never MOVES, which is exactly why neither of them reads as disintegration. Verified across the whole sweep: progress 0 is byte-for-byte the untouched frame, 0.35 has debris landing 55px past the layer's original edge, 0.6 is a third gone, 1.0 is empty. VHS Tape is real tape damage rather than a screen artifact — the two tells nothing else in the app can make are colour smearing sideways while the luma edge stays sharp, and a tracking band rolling through the picture. Verified: red bleeds into the neighbouring blue block while the white-to-blue luma step gets SHARPER (131 → 161, the tape's own ringing), the tracking band sits at row 45 at one moment and row 172 at another, and Amount 0 is an exact no-op. Both run one pass with O(1) running sums — the colour smear costs the same at 90px as at 1px. Byte-identical to the previous build across nineteen effect stacks.
- v4.61 — **Compression Crunch** and **Temporal Denoise**, the last two on the build list. Compression Crunch is what over-compression actually does: colour collapses into blocks and flat areas band, while edges stay razor sharp. Mosaic and Pixelate destroy the luma, which is the exact opposite, and is why neither of them ever reads as a re-uploaded video. Verified: a smooth gradient goes from 172 distinct brightness levels to 43 — real banding — while a razor edge in the same frame gets SHARPER, not softer, because the ringing overshoots it. Fry pushes the whole thing into meme territory. Temporal Denoise melts grain out of the parts of a shot that are holding still and leaves anything moving sharp — for low-light footage where a plain blur would just soften everything. Verified on a grainy static field: noise down 25%, while a bar moving 3.7px a frame starts at exactly the same pixel with the effect on as with it off, with two blended pixels at its antialiased edge and no trail. Byte-identical to the previous build across twenty-one effect stacks. That closes the build table: all thirteen effects on it are shipped.
- v4.62 — round 11 opens with **Lens Distortion** and **Pixel Sort**. Lens Distortion is real lens geometry — a k1/k2 radial polynomial — which means a NEGATIVE Barrel straightens wide-angle footage rather than just bending it the other way. Fisheye and Pinch/Bulge are ad-hoc curves that happen to look bendy; this one is the maths a lens profile is written in. It also carries the colour fringing real glass has: measured on concentric rings, the fringe is three times stronger at the corners than near the centre and vanishes at the middle, which is what the existing Chromatic Aberration (uniform across the whole frame) cannot do. Zoom defaults to 118% because bending a rectangle outward pulls in content from beyond the frame, which does not exist — every tool that does this has a resize for the same reason, and without it the effect lands looking broken. Pixel Sort re-ORDERS runs of pixels by brightness, which nothing else in the app does; every other effect changes pixels in place. Verified that a sorted row contains exactly the same colours it started with, just rearranged — nothing invented, nothing lost — and that Density scales the frame's involvement from 591 pixels to 18,682. The sort skips the comparator so the engine stays on its fast integer path. 2.5ms each. Byte-identical to the previous build across twenty-three effect stacks.
- v4.63 — **Luma Matte**, and a real bug fixed underneath it. Luma Matte cuts a layer out using another layer's brightness: white keeps, black hides, and the matte can be anywhere in the stack rather than having to sit directly above. Alpha mattes already existed as masking groups and the Cutout blend modes; what did not was reading LUMA, from a non-adjacent layer, with Invert, Feather and a Black/White point to set how hard the cut is. Verified across a black-to-white ramp: visibility follows the matte exactly ([31, 72, 121, 172, 212] across the frame), Invert gives the precise mirror ([212, 171, 122, 71, 31]), and tightening the points to 120/130 turns it into a hard edge at exactly the pixel where the ramp crosses that value. The bug: every effect that references ANOTHER layer shared three module-level scratch canvases, so stacking two of them made the inner one overwrite the outer one's workspace mid-render — measured, adding a ZERO-STRENGTH second picker effect changed 5,106 bytes of the frame when it should have changed nothing. They now take depth-indexed slots, the same way the blur and warp paths already did. Luma Matte is built on the fixed pool, and stacking it with a displacement map now changes nothing at all.
- v4.64 — **Compound Blur** and **Match Grade**, both riding the scratch pool that v4.63 fixed. Compound Blur blurs a layer by how bright ANOTHER layer is: white blurs most, black stays sharp. A depth map gives you a real rack focus, a soft shape gives a vignette blur, and a gradient gives a tilt-shift along whatever line you draw — none of which any other blur here can do, because they all apply one radius to the whole layer. Verified with a half-black half-white map on a striped clip: the black side keeps its full sharpness (local contrast 54.5, exactly the untouched value) while the white side goes to 0.2, and Invert swaps them precisely. Match Grade pushes one clip's colour toward another's — it matches the average tone and the contrast, which is the quickest way to make two cameras agree. Verified: a cool low-contrast clip (mean 107/124/157) matched against a warm one (mean 149/112/83) landed on 148/111/82. Colour-only moves the tone and leaves contrast alone; contrast-only does the reverse; Amount 0.5 lands exactly halfway. Contrast gain is capped at 4x so a nearly flat source cannot explode. Finish by hand with Levels or HSL Bands — auto-matching disappoints the moment two shots contain different things, which is why it shipped after those two and not before. 2.9ms and 2.5ms. Byte-identical to the previous build across twenty-eight effect stacks.
- v4.65 — every one of the 193 effects now has a hand-written description; none fall back to the generated line any more. That line was truthful but plain ("Distortion & Warp. Controls: Amount, Detail.") and 107 effects were still using it. Each new one says what the effect DOES in the words someone would use before they knew its name, and where two effects are easy to confuse it says what separates them — Mosaic is "coarser and blockier than Pixelate", Dark Glow is "the inverse bloom", Block Dissolve is "the same, in blocks rather than single pixels". Because search reads descriptions, this also makes the browser findable by plain language: "boom mic" now finds Remove Object, "doughnut" finds Torus, "whirlpool" finds Curl, "pendulum" finds Swing, "topographic" finds Contour Strips and "colour cast" finds Color Balance — none of which contain those words in their names. Verified: 193 written, 0 generated, 0 duplicates, 0 stubs.
- v4.66 — the two bugs Ezra reported, both fixed at the cause. (1) **The preview no longer shrinks when the quality tier drops.** A canvas is a replaced element with an intrinsic size, and #canvas-wrap was content-sized — so when adaptive playback quality shrank the backing store, the wrap shrank with it and the picture physically got smaller on screen (measured: 508px → 302px, a 40% collapse, on the bottom tier). A constant-width spacer inside the wrap now provides the sizing, and the canvas is positioned out of flow, so the on-screen size depends only on the stage and the comp aspect. Verified stable across all six tiers × four comp aspects (1:1, 9:16, 16:9, 21:9) × mobile and desktop × normal and drawing mode, with the crop path (zoom 3 + pan) still cropping and restoring correctly. It also fixes a shrink in drawing mode that was there before this bug existed. (2) **One plain video no longer drags the quality down.** The adaptive ladder measured a frame taking longer than 12ms and shed resolution — but decoding a video frame and handing it to the GPU costs the same however small the destination canvas is: measured on one 2048×2048 clip with no effects, thirteen times fewer pixels bought only 32% less time (12.2ms → 8.3ms), with tier-to-tier steps in the noise. So the ladder read "still slow", shed again, and walked to the bottom tier for nothing — 28% resolution, no gain. A drop now has to pay for itself: if it doesn't cut the average by 15%+ it is undone and probing is locked out, unless the cost later climbs 35% above where it locked (a blur added, a second clip), which earns a fresh probe. Verified end-to-end on the real playback tick: decode-bound cost settles at tier 0 (full resolution) where it used to walk 0→1→2→3→4→5; genuinely pixel-bound cost still drops 0→1→2 and settles just inside budget; a cheap scene still climbs back to full detail.
- v4.67 — **fixing v4.66's own regressions**, found by an adversarial review of the commit rather than by use. The payoff test I added judged a tier drop by whether the frame cost fell, which is wrong whenever a drop changes no pixels at all — and there are two separate ways that happens. 'Smooth' mode floors the factor at tier 2, so stepping 0→1→2 renders identically; every probe read "no gain", was undone, and the ladder dead-ended before it could ever reach tier 3 where the factor actually moves. The mode meant for slow devices was left unable to shed a single pixel — strictly worse than before v4.66. Separately, `previewScale()`'s 0.25 floor and pixel budget can clamp two different factors to the same backing store (a 2048² comp clamps every tier below 0.735), with the same effect. Both are now caught by asking the CANVAS whether the probe changed anything, rather than asking the tier. Three more: the lock is now a latch rather than a 600-frame timer, so it stops re-probing and softening the preview for a moment every ten seconds on exactly the plain video v4.66 was written to fix; the payoff bookkeeping resets when the cost regime changes between dragging and playing, since a cost-to-beat learned from a drag (no video decode, much cheaper) made playback undo a drop that was helping and then lock adaptation out while it stuttered; and the lock's baseline is now the cost at the tier returned TO rather than the one abandoned, so the escape threshold measures from the right place. 'Detail' mode no longer runs futile probes at all, `_lastPlayTier` (dead since it was written) is gone, and `FM.playbackQualityInfo()` now reports `effective` alongside `factor` — they differ in smooth and detail mode, and reading the wrong one is what hid the dead-end. Verified two ways: nine scenarios against the real function bodies extracted verbatim from js/app.js, and the same scenarios end-to-end on the live playback tick.
- v4.68 — **a bug that wasn't, withdrawn before it got "fixed".** BACKLOG.md has carried a high-severity entry since July saying the per-layer Solo 'S' button had regressed out of the UI and should be restored — "almost certainly lost in the mobile timeline rebuild" — and a docs audit repeated it as the top piece of outstanding work. Both were wrong. `git log -S` finds `69563ae` (v1.75): *"Removed the per-layer Solo ('S') button from each track head (Ezra didn't want the 'isolate one layer' toggle)"*, and js/timeline.js still carries a comment saying so at the spot where it used to be built. Nor was `layer.solo` orphaned engine code: it drives the **"Hide other layers"** checkbox in the export dialog, which solos the selected clip for the duration of an export and restores it in a `finally` — which is why the compositor, exporter and audio-play gates all exist. The only real defect was four dead `.th-solo` CSS rules that outlived the button by a year and made it look half-built; they are gone, along with the mobile rule that hid them. Nothing else changed: verified at 380px that the track head is still 72px wide with stripe/eye/thumb/name and no console errors. BACKLOG and NEXT-SESSION now say plainly not to rebuild it, and both carry the rule the mistake came from — **an absent feature is not automatically a regression; run `git log -S'<thing>'` before calling it one.**
- v4.69 — **blurs, glows and drop shadows now look in the preview like they will in the export.** Every length inside `ctx.filter` and `ctx.shadow*` is in DEVICE pixels — the canvas transform does not touch it. Measured: `blur(10px)` leaves the same 26-device-pixel edge ramp whether the context is scaled 1, 0.5 or 0.25. So on a preview rendered at quarter scale that blur covered 104 project pixels instead of 26 and the picture looked four times blurrier than the finished video, and `shadowOffsetX/Y` (device-space by spec) put a 40px shadow 156 project pixels from its layer instead of 39 — the shadow visibly detached. v4.66/v4.67 made this far more noticeable, because the adaptive quality tier now changes the preview scale during playback: press play and a blur would deepen and a shadow slide away, then both snap back on pause. Radii and offsets are now multiplied by `plateScale(ctx)` at all five sites (layer filter, glow's stacked drop-shadows, camera defocus, layer shadow, adjustment layers). At scale 1 the arithmetic is a no-op, so **exports cannot change** — verified byte-for-byte identical against the previous compositor across six scenes × two times, on 1,037k-pixel non-empty renders. A 0.4-scale preview is now 96–97% closer to the export it produces (glow, the worst case because it stacks drop-shadows, went from a mean error of 74.2 per channel to 3.2). A control scene with no length-valued filters is unchanged to two decimal places.
- v4.70 — **the reverse-playback frame cache stops being able to OOM-kill a phone.** The cache decodes a clip into ImageBitmaps so reverse and slow-mo scrub smoothly, capped at a flat `maxBytes: 384MB` on every device — with a comment saying the cap existed so a long reversed clip "can't OOM-kill mobile Safari". But 384MB of ImageBitmaps *is* what kills mobile Safari; the number was a desktop number wearing a mobile justification. The budget now comes from the device: `navigator.deviceMemory` at 48MB per GB, clamped to 64–384MB, and because that API is Chromium-only — Safari, the browser that actually does the killing, never reports it — it falls back to the same fine-pointer test home.js already uses for "this machine has a keyboard". A touch OS also gets a hard 160MB ceiling regardless of reported RAM, since `deviceMemory` describes the device class rather than what is free, and a phone reclaims background tabs far more readily than a desktop. Cached frames drop to 640px on the small-budget devices, which sounds like a loss and isn't: the preview canvas there is a few hundred device pixels wide, and smaller frames buy ~2.25x more of them for the same bytes — the frame COUNT is what decides how much of a clip reverses smoothly. Measured across the range: **desktops are completely unchanged** (384MB/960px), an 8GB tablet gives up 58% of the memory for only 6% fewer frames, an iPhone 67% for 25% fewer, and a 1GB phone 83% for 63% fewer while still holding 3 seconds. Exercised for real, not just in the arithmetic: a 2048×2048 clip cached 72 frames at 640×640 for 112.5MB inside a 160MB budget — the same three seconds cost 265MB before — with reverse playback still drawing every frame and the `scaled` flag set so the exporter force-clears it and exports at full source resolution.
- v4.71 — NEW desktop **Studio layout** (Settings → Layout → Classic / Studio, persisted). Ezra's design: the editing panel drops down NEXT TO THE TIMELINE instead of living in the far right column, so adding and editing is a short trip from the clips rather than a reach to the top corner; the top bar stands up as a rail on the far left, handing its 50px row back to the canvas (measured: stage 75px → 125px in the same window); and the existing drag-resizer now grabs the whole bottom band, so you trade canvas height for editing room in one gesture. It is a pure GRID RE-PLACEMENT — the same four regions, one #inspector-panel node, zero duplicated panels — which is why every existing handler still works. To make that possible #inspector-panel moved out of #main to sit as a direct child of #app, and classic is now placed explicitly (it renders identically: verified column/row tracks and every panel box before and after). The phone is deliberately untouched: the whole Studio block is behind (min-width: 701px) and the phone's inspector is a position:fixed sheet, so with layout:studio saved a 375px viewport measures byte-identical to before (same 52/324.797/435.203 rows, same fixed sheet at y=812, project name still static). New regression test `studio-layout` guards the two things that would rot — a duplicated inspector, and the grid template silently not applying — both mutation-checked (a "narrow top bar" assertion passed while the layout was broken, so it now asserts the 2-row template instead). Also nudges BEFORE-PUBLISHING items 2 and 6: this is our own composition, not AM's.
- v4.72 — Studio: the left rail now stops at the canvas instead of running the full height of the window. The bottom band (editing panel + timeline) reaches the window edge and gains the rail's 60px, so the half that needs room to breathe gets it — the editing panel went 300px → 360px wide, and the drag-resizer now spans the whole band from x=0. The rail is chrome for the canvas, so it ends where the canvas does, exactly like the floating view-bar. Shortening it exposed a real bug on short windows: Export was pinned to the rail's bottom with margin-top:auto and sat 20px PAST the end of the rail, painting over the panel below — it now stacks normally and the rail clips/scrolls, so nothing can spill. (At a normal window the rail has ~848px for 157px of content; this only bites below roughly 400px of stage height.) Test `studio-layout` gained three guards, all mutation-checked: the rail must stop at the canvas, the band must reach the window edge, and the rail must clip. Classic and phone untouched — verified identical boxes in both.
- v4.73 — FIX: importing audio on a phone did nothing. Two separate causes, both real. (1) handleFiles classified files with a bare `file.type.startsWith()` chain, and a song picked out of the iOS/Android Files app very often arrives with an EMPTY file.type — .m4a, .flac and .opus especially — so it matched none of the three branches and was dropped with no error whatsoever. It now falls back to the extension (the drop handler and the font importer already did this; handleFiles was the odd one out), and anything genuinely unusable now says so instead of vanishing. (2) "Import audio…" shared the one file input with Import media, whose accept carried image/* and video/* too — iOS greys audio out in Files when the accept list is mixed like that. The audio entry now narrows accept to audio plus explicit extensions (mobile pickers match extensions far more reliably than they map audio/* to a UTI) and restores the full list straight after, so Import media is untouched. New regression test `typeless-import` covers the classifier.
- v4.74 — the car shape has tyres. The traced body already had wheel ARCHES cut up from the baseline but nothing in them, so it sat on air. Each wheel is now a disc wound clockwise like the body outline (shapes fill nonzero, so it unions into the sill harmlessly) plus a hub wound anticlockwise to punch a hole — which is what makes a tyre read as a tyre when the whole shape is one flat colour. Sized r=0.135 against a 0.106 arch half-width: big enough to fill the arch and sit on the ground line, small enough to leave the arch crescent visible. Checked the alternatives side by side first — r=0.106 looks like castors, r=0.168 swallows the arch and the car goes blobby.
- v4.75 — FIX: tapping the canvas while editing a shape's points closed the edit panel. Two halves. The hit radius was a flat 14 display px — mouse-sized — so a fingertip missed a point far more often than it hit one; touch and pen now get 26px while a mouse keeps the tight 14 so dense points stay individually selectable. And a MISS used to return out of onDown without stopping the event, so it fell through the overlay to the canvas handler underneath, which reads a tap on empty space as "deselect" and shut the whole panel. While Edit Points is open the canvas belongs to it, so a miss is now swallowed — verified by dispatching a touch pointerdown at the overlay centre: defaultPrevented true, ZERO events reaching #stage, editor still active, layer still selected. New regression test `point-hit-radius` (mutation-checked); the swallow half was verified by running it rather than by a test, because a test for it would have to create and delete a layer in the live project and autosave could persist that.
- v4.76 — the tick shape is smooth. Not a single point moved (bounding box identical to the decimal) — the only change is which points are marked smooth. The trace had left the three ENDS as hard corners, and that is exactly where the faceting was: a chopped flat elbow at the bottom of the tick, an angular nub on the left tail, and a cut-off top-right tip. Those are round in any drawn tick, so they're smooth now. The inner V stays a corner deliberately — it's meant to be sharp, and rounding it is the one change that would actually alter the shape.
- v4.76a — backfilled the Edit Points regression test now that wrecking a test project is acceptable: `point-miss-swallow` drives the real overlay with a real touch PointerEvent, asserts the miss is consumed AND that zero events reach #stage, then removes its throwaway layer and restores the selection in a finally. Checked the escape detector itself actually fires on a genuine bubble, so its green means something.
- v4.77 — Motion Blur (Object) is now in the Effects list, leading the Blur category right beside Motion Blur (Footage), and it turns up first when you search "motion". This is the blur that smears the LAYER'S OWN movement — position, scale, rotation — as opposed to Motion Blur (Footage), which blurs movement inside the clip, and Directional Blur, which ignores movement altogether. It is not new code: `layer.motionBlur` already existed and already worked (measured — a 60px square keyframed x 40→280 over 2s renders a 60→64px lit span at t=1, which is the correct physics for shutter 0.9 at 30fps). The problem was purely that it lived as a checkbox buried in Move & Transform while Ezra was looking in the effects list. Same pseudo-entry technique as Mask: the tile drives `layer.motionBlur` rather than pushing an effect instance, so every existing project renders and exports byte-identically. Motion Blur (Footage)'s description no longer points at "Move & Transform" for the object blur.
- v4.78 — every Move & Transform slider now carries its OWN keyframe diamond, so X and Y are genuinely independent tracks (Ezra: "Every single Individual slider needs to have its own key frames … moving the clip around and zooming in need to be seperate", and he chose full X/Y separation explicitly over Position-as-one). Keying X leaves Y a plain number — verified live: x becomes {kf:[{t:1,v:160}]} while y stays 120. This needed NO change to the scene format, the renderer, save/load or export: FM.setTransform and toggleKeyframe always worked on a single property, and transform.x/y/scaleX/scaleY were already independent — the coupling was purely the inspector keying every channel in MT_PRIMARY at once. So old projects keep playing untouched and there is no migration. The mode-level ◆ stays as a "key this whole mode" convenience. Diamonds are on X/Y/Z, Rotation/X tilt/Y tilt and X/Y Skew; the anchor sliders deliberately get none, because a keyframed anchor is read as a raw number by the compositor and NaNs the layer out of existence (confirmed in BUG-HUNT).
- v4.79 — the Scale rows got their keyframe diamonds too, completing the per-slider set. Which channel each one keys follows the LINK state, because that is what the row actually writes: linked, Width and Height both drive the uniform `scale`, so both diamonds key `scale` (pressing either is the same keyframe — right, there is only one channel); unlinked, Width owns scaleX and Height owns scaleY and they become independent tracks like X and Y. Verified both ways live: unlinked, pressing Width animates scaleX and leaves scale and scaleY alone; linked, it animates scale and neither scaleX nor scaleY.
- v4.79b — FIX: keyframes you could see but never delete. FM.animatedProps (which decides what draws a diamond on the clip) covered five containers that deleteKeyframesAt (which decides what a delete can reach) did not: trimPath start/end/offset, stroke.dash.offset, the whole shape repeater, pen-mask paths and audioFx params. So those diamonds were undeletable — measured: 12 of 28 keyframed properties on a fully-loaded layer survived a delete at their own time. The two lists now cover the same containers. This mattered more the moment every slider got its own track: a track you can create has to be a track you can clear. New regression test `delete-parity` asserts animatedProps comes back EMPTY after a delete, and is mutation-checked against the old slot list.
- v4.80 — the object motion blur has finished moving. Its controls (Shutter, Samples) now live in EFFECTS, appearing as a "Motion Blur (Object)" card once it's switched on from + Add Effect, with an × to remove it — the same add-and-remove gesture as every other effect. The old checkbox in Move & Transform is gone, not hidden. Ezra asked for that toggle to be deleted because he thought it did nothing; it actually worked (measured in v4.77), so it was MOVED rather than dropped: switch on in the effects browser, tune and remove in the effects panel. Verified end to end — Move & Transform no longer mentions motion blur, the Effects panel shows nothing until it's on, then shows Shutter + Samples + ×, and the × really clears the flag.
- v4.81 — the Elements tab has a real browser. Saved elements used to be pushed onto the SAME list as Camera / Null / Adjustment / Empty group, so structural layer types and everything you had ever saved sat loose in one flat grid (Ezra: "make sure all the elements are grouped together and not siting loose in the same menu that holds camera and all that"). Those four stay inline; your saved elements now sit behind one "Browse elements (n)" button that opens a full-screen browser with search, built on the effect browser's own chrome so it reads as the same kind of place — grid, search field, tap to insert, long-press or right-click to delete. Tiles show the element's real saved thumbnail (the same one the home screen uses) and fall back to an initial when there isn't one. An insert whose IndexedDB pack was evicted now says so instead of silently doing nothing. New regression test `elements-browser` covers the filter, mutation-checked.
- v4.82 — FIX (third attempt, this one measured): Tiles repeating blank once the clip left the canvas. Ezra: "if I move the clip off screen the tiles only duplicate what's on the actual screen/canvas." Measured before: with Tiles at its defaults a centred clip tiles 100% of the frame, half off-canvas 100%, fully off-canvas 0% — nothing at all. The "Whole clip" mode that exists precisely for this was ON by default and still failed, because the effect call is gated on the CANVAS bbox: a clip fully off-frame leaves no on-screen alpha, so the tiles function never ran, and the expanded-plate path inside it — which reaches past the frame and replaces the tile rect wholesale — was unreachable. Both earlier fixes were downstream of a call that never happened, which is why they looked right and changed nothing. Tiles in Whole-clip mode now gets a placeholder rect so it runs and can build its own plate. After: fully off-canvas 0% → 100%, with centred and half-off unchanged at 100%, and "On screen" mode still correctly showing nothing off-frame — the two modes stay distinct. New regression test `tiles-offcanvas` asserts all three.
- v4.83 — FIX: freehand/vector drawing threw the canvas into a weird spot in the Studio layout. Drawing mode forces `#app` to two columns — a rule written for classic, whose grid IS two columns (stage | inspector). Studio has three and places its regions by explicit column lines, so the override collided: measured, the left rail swelled from 60px to 967px while the stage collapsed from 1140px to 233px and jumped from x=60 to x=967, taking the canvas with it. Studio now gets its own drawing rule — rail keeps its width, the inspector column collapses, the stage takes everything else — so drawing does what it is for and hands the canvas the whole window. After: stage stays at x=60 and full 1140px width, and the layout restores exactly on exit. Classic is untouched (verified identical before/during/after). New regression test `drawing-grid` asserts drawing never shrinks or moves the stage in EITHER layout; mutation-checked (removing the rule fails it with "SHRANK the stage 1140px -> 233px").
- v4.84 — undo and redo grey out when there is nothing behind or ahead of them (Ezra). The state always existed — `index > 0` and `index < stack.length - 1` are the exact guards undo() and redo() already used — it was just never shown, so both buttons looked live at the ends of the stack and pressing them did nothing. FM.history gains canUndo()/canRedo(), and one syncButtons() runs from every path that moves the stack (commit, undo, redo, reset) so the two can't drift; commit matters as much as the rest, because a new edit drops the redo tail. Greyed to 30% rather than hidden or disabled: the control stays where your eye expects it, and a tap still lands harmlessly (undo/redo already no-op at the ends) instead of feeling broken. New regression test `undo-affordance` checks the button class agrees with the history state at four points, and is sensitivity-checked by inverting canUndo/canRedo — both inversions fail it.
- v4.85 — the love heart, re-cut and judged by an outside agent with no stake in it. The lobes are now near-circles centred at x=0.30/0.70 (were 0.255/0.745, which splayed the cleft into a wedge), the widest point lifted 0.35 → 0.297 (it sat low enough to read as a shield), the crowns reach y=0 so the tops are domes rather than peaks, and the cleft went 0.245 → 0.18 — its flanks, not its depth, were what read wrong. Measured off the app's own rendered pixels: mirror mismatch 0.04% of area, IoU 0.944 against the Apple Color Emoji heart, legible down to 20px. Also fixed a curvature-sign flip on each lower flank (two vertical tangents forced an S-bend, showing as a faint flat patch at large sizes) by tilting those handles to -0.02 — the RIGHT one is also -0.02, not +0.02, because traversal reverses on the mirror side. And the Add-menu tile's SVG, whose comment claimed it was "traced from the same eight anchors", had drifted to IoU 0.92 (squatter, deeper V); it is now generated from the shipped geometry rather than redrawn.
- v4.86 — the home top row's buttons sit on the centre line now (Ezra: "the buttons on the top row aren't centred"). `.hm-top` had asymmetric vertical padding — 16px top against 8px bottom — so `align-items: center` dutifully centred everything in the CONTENT box while that box itself sat low in the bar: measured, all five children (brand, search, Select, ⚙, ⋯) were 3.5px below the row's true middle. Worth saying what was NOT wrong, since it narrows what to look at next time: the buttons were perfectly centred within themselves (glyph offsets 0.0 on both axes), mutually aligned with each other, and the row's left and right margins already matched at 18px. Padding is 12/12 now, which keeps the row's 63px height and puts the contents on the centre line (residual 0.5px is the odd pixel of an odd-numbered height).
- v4.87 — the home header's "FreeMotion" text is now Ezra's own glass wordmark. The source render sits on pure black, so the background came off with a soft luminance key rather than a hard threshold — a ramp from 10 to 46 with a smoothstep, keyed on the MAX channel so coloured glass edges stay bright, then unpremultiplied so partial-alpha edges keep their true colour instead of going muddy. Contrast and saturation are lifted a little because the render was lit for pure black and goes grey against the app's #12151b chrome. Shipped as a 421x64 RGBA PNG (52KB) at ~3x its display size. The ▶ mark stays beside it on desktop and is dropped on phone, where the four action buttons and their gaps eat ~242px of 375 and the wordmark needs the rest; it scales to fit rather than being clipped by .hm-brand's overflow.
- v4.88 — the home screen has a backdrop instead of flat near-black (Ezra: "a bit boring… stylise it a bit with fitting colour gradients, try to make it not feel like a stale image as it will be very seeable on pc"). Three very low-opacity radial pools rather than one linear wash — a linear gradient on a tall PC window reads as a flat band, which is the stale feel to avoid, whereas offset pools weight the corners differently and give the eye somewhere to travel. Colours are the app's own: the teal accent, plus the violet and blue already in the glass wordmark. A fourth pool drifts on a 48s cycle, travelling a few percent, so it is never a fixed image but never something you would catch moving; it is disabled under prefers-reduced-motion. It lives in ::before/::after under the content, pointer-events:none, and never touches the cards, so nothing behind the project rows loses contrast. Sized in vmax so a wide desktop gets pools in proportion rather than four small dots.
- v4.89 — the + FAB is Ezra's glass orb render. The + is genuinely CUT THROUGH: the source has the plus in the same black as its backdrop, so a single soft luminance key punched out the background and the plus together and left the bevel around it — whatever sits behind the button now shows through the middle (verified: alpha 0 at the centre of the plus, 255 on the glass ring). The AI sparkle watermark in the corner was cropped off by column profile, not by eye. Padded to square before resizing so the circle can't be squashed; 216px for a 62px button. The painted "bead" the glass theme drew on this button is gone — it assumed a solid fill and drew an inner edge across a deliberately see-through middle — but the SHINE stays, as its own sweeping highlight layer clipped to the button by the existing overflow:hidden, so light still crosses the glass and vanishes at the rim.
- v4.90 — the OPEN badge on the current project's thumbnail is Ezra's glass pill render, same construction as the + FAB: the word is cut in the same black as the backdrop, so one soft luminance key punched out the background AND the letters, and the thumbnail behind shows through them (alpha 5 inside the O's counter, 254 on the glass rim). The AI sparkle watermark was cropped off by column profile again — the artwork is a single content run, the watermark a separate one, so the cut is exact rather than eyeballed. Sized off the render's own 1.495 aspect (33x22) so the pill can never stretch. The word OPEN stays in the DOM and is hidden with font-size:0 rather than display:none, so the badge keeps its accessible name.
- v4.91 — the + FAB grew 62px → 74px and its asset was re-rendered at 256px with more contrast and saturation. The artwork was already correct in v4.89 (verified: background is fab-plus.png, the old SVG plus is display:none, and the asset's centre alpha is 0 so the plus really is cut through) — but at ~60px the render's facets, concentric rings and iridescent rim all average out into a plain glossy disc, so it did not look like the source. Bigger and punchier helps; it still will not show the faceting the way the full-size render does, because that detail needs well over 100px to survive.
- v4.92 — the home screen's arrival, and the glass buttons finished. (1) The HOME CREATE BUTTON is the glass orb now — it was still the flat accent disc while the editor's + FAB had been swapped over two versions ago. (2) The SQUARE BOX around the FAB is gone: the travelling sheen is a child layer clipped by overflow:hidden, and with no border-radius that clip was a square, so it painted a faint panel around a round orb; the sheen is now masked by the artwork itself (which also stops it pouring through the + cut-out and lighting the plus up like a solid glyph for half of every cycle) and the button is a circle. (3) Both orbs dropped 74px → 58px. (4) LIQUID GLASS EDGE on the create button and the OPEN badge — layered drop-shadows, which follow the artwork's alpha and so light the outer rim AND the inside of every cut-out; the wide ambient glow is a box-shadow instead, because a third drop-shadow pass filled the 6px plus solid (measured at 3.2x zoom). (5) SPLASH → HOME is a dissolve, not a cut: the fade now starts 0.9s BEFORE the video's last frame and takes ~900ms while the video plays on underneath, and the home backdrop's gradient pools bloom up through the clearing black (verified: fade begins at t=2281ms with the video at 2.21s of 3.00s, opacity 1 → 0 over 950ms). A tap to skip still cuts in 280ms. (6) EVERY CARD AND CONTROL FADES IN INDIVIDUALLY from below — top-bar buttons, then tabs, then each project, then the + — on a 55ms beat, and it now waits for the splash's dismiss event so it plays into the dissolve instead of finishing 200ms before it started (which is what it did on the first attempt: measured intro complete at t=2101ms, fade begins at t=2281ms). (7) The HOME TOP ROW is translucent — a thin darkening wash over a real backdrop blur, so the background design runs the full height of the screen and the separation comes from the blur and a hairline rather than from an opaque bar. (8) The FreeMotion WORDMARK has a glint that crosses it every 6.4s, masked by the wordmark's own alpha so the highlight travels through the letters instead of a rectangle sweeping over the logo. Also: the test runner's iframe was display:none, which gave every element a 0x0 box and left two geometry tests permanently unpassable — parked off-screen at a real size instead, and the suite is 21/21 for the first time.
- v4.93 — the three mobile issues.
