The answer was already in the app and simply not consulted: FM.kfFocusProps (js/inspector.js) is what the TIMELINE uses to decide which diamonds are solid and draggable versus hollow and inert, and the skip buttons now filter through the same function — so they stop exactly where the live diamonds are, and a keyframe you cannot grab is never a destination. Nothing focused deliberately contributes NO keyframes rather than all of them, which is the consistent reading of the timeline's own "completely inert", and the clip edges, benchmarks and project start/end keep the buttons useful in that state. The pre-existing rule that keyframes only count while the playhead is ON the clip is unchanged. Mutation-proved: reverting the filter turns the new test red with "with nothing focused the skip buttons still stop on [1,2,3,4]". Also, following v5.82: "with the colours on the media one, make the bottom line green". The frame is now drawn as three sides rather than a rect — a rect gives one stroke for all four edges, so the ground line had to come out of it — and the bottom runs green from corner to corner, meeting the hill, which is what makes it read as ground instead of a border that happens to be a different colour. Suite 102/102 -> 103/103.
- v5.84 — the Media icon becomes a picture. Ezra, on the v5.83 version: "I want the whole bottom line, rn it looks tacky, maybe try also filling in the green hills solid green and make the background solid blue, might hate it might not we will see." Two things, and the first is the one that mattered. THE WHOLE BOTTOM IS GREEN NOW, corner curves included. v5.83 drew a straight green line between the two bottom corners, so the last few pixels at each end — the rounded corner arcs themselves — stayed blue: a ground line that stops short of the ground, which is exactly the tackiness he was pointing at. The frame is now a path that STOPS at y=17 on both sides and the green ground path carries both corner arcs, so the green runs the full width and turns up into the sides. Second, the fills: sky #2E6E9E, hills #4ADE80, sun #FBBF24, all solid, with the sky-blue frame and green ground kept as strokes over the top. Rendered against the outline version and a hills-only version at 3x before choosing — at 22px an outline sun floating over an empty background reads as three unrelated marks, while the filled version reads as a photograph in one glance, which is the entire job of this icon. He said "might hate it might not we will see", so this is a proposal with the comparison sheet kept: reverting to the outline is one line, the geometry is identical. Suite 103/103.
- v5.85 — colour goes into the icons themselves. Ezra: "the green goes up slightly to high, also with the other icons try incorporating a bit more colour, like with the shapes one, you can make the circle shape and the square two different colours, with the elements and audio one give it a gradient that looks nice and smooth and subtle, with the templates one give the two lines in the middle different colours from the lines on the outside." MEDIA: the hill peaks topped out at y=12.6 in a 5..19 frame — level with the sun at 10.2 — so the hills fought the sky for the picture instead of sitting under it. Highest point is 14.8 now, and the silhouette is traced explicitly and still closed through both bottom corner arcs so the ground keeps reaching the ground. SHAPE: blue square, teal circle — adjacent on the wheel, so the pair still reads as one icon rather than two stickers, but obviously two colours at 22px. TEMPLATE: orange frame, amber dividers, lighter inside so the icon reads frame-first and the layout it describes sits within it. ELEMENTS and AUDIO get gradients, and the restraint is the point: TWO stops each, both inside one family, because a wide gradient on a 22px stroke reads as a colour error rather than as depth. The cube is lit from the top-left where its own top face is and the note from the top-right where its beam is highest, so in both cases the shading agrees with the geometry instead of just being a ramp. Gradient ids are namespaced (fm-ic-el, fm-ic-au) because several of these icons share a document and a duplicate gradient id silently steals the fill. The per-tab colour rules added to styles.css in v5.82 are gone — a gradient or a two-colour shape cannot be expressed as a single `color`, so hue now lives in the markup beside the geometry it belongs to, and the stylesheet carries a pointer saying so rather than a stale rule. Suite 103/103.
- v5.86 — the canvas edge becomes visible, and every Add-menu icon gets a gradient. THE CANVAS EDGE. Ezra: "on the canvas, the background colour is a bit too close to black so you can't really tell when the canvas ends and when the background begins, make it slightly brighter." Measured at the boundary with a black project background: the stage read rgb(3,10,11) against the canvas's rgb(0,0,0) — a mean difference of EIGHT out of 255, below what a phone shows in a lit room, so he was describing something real and not a preference. The glass theme was the worse of the two at #04090c and glass is the SHIPPED DEFAULT, i.e. the value he actually sees; the base theme was #0a0e17. Both raised on level only, hue untouched: base #131b26, glass #101823, with glass kept a shade deeper so it still reads as the darker theme. Re-measured at the same pixel: delta 8 -> 20. THE ICONS. "The gradients you did in that image I'm seeing look great, do a gradient for all of them, and also with the templates logo, make the lines in the middle complimentary colours but also very different from the border colour, and also make the sideways line and the downwards line both unique colours, and make sure the border lines go on top of the middle ones, not how it is currently." So all five now carry gradients — Shape's square and circle each get their own, Media's sky and hills are gradient fills, Template's frame and both dividers are separate ramps — and every one stays a two-stop ramp inside a single family, because a wide gradient on a 22px stroke reads as a colour error rather than depth. Template got three separate fixes in one: the dividers are now SEPARATE paths so the horizontal and vertical can differ (cyan across, violet down, both sitting opposite orange on the wheel so they read as "not the border" and as a near-complementary pair to each other), and the paint order is reversed. That last one is the interesting bit: SVG paints in document order, so the frame was being drawn FIRST and the dividers were crossing over it. Declaring the dividers first and the frame last is the whole fix — the frame's rounded corners now cover the divider ends, which is what he was pointing at. Suite 103/103.
- v5.87 — the Template icon's upright ends under its crossbar. Ezra: "In templates, put the bottom straight up line ending under the horizontal line." The vertical starts at y=10, exactly where the horizontal runs, and a round linecap adds half a stroke width past that point — so with the vertical declared after the horizontal, its cap painted violet straight through the cyan bar. Same lesson as v5.86's border fix, one level down: SVG paints in document order, and the fix is ordering rather than geometry. The vertical is declared FIRST now, the horizontal over its top end, and the frame over both — so the crossbar runs unbroken and the join reads as a T with the upright ending beneath it. No coordinate changed, which matters: nudging the vertical down to y=10.9 would have left a visible gap at some rendering scales, while covering the cap is exact at every scale. Suite 103/103.
- v5.88 — Fill Behind, the blurred backdrop (queue 32, last of AM's "Other" set worth building). Ezra specified it himself after it had sat blocked for want of a definition: "Fill behind is simple, it adds the blur and fills the space that the layer isn't filling on the canvas." That is the blurred-backdrop fill phone video apps use, and his sentence dissolved the objection that had stalled it — the worry was that pixel-effect plates are project-sized so a naive fill paints the whole frame, and painting the whole frame is the POINT. A copy of the LAYER is scaled to cover the canvas, blurred, dimmed and drawn behind it, so a portrait clip in a landscape comp fills its own pillarbox with itself. Built as a Copy-Background sibling, NOT a post-effect, for the reason that got Magnify Background reverted: listing a type in POSTFX makes drawLayer return ~90 lines before the Copy Background dispatch and draw zero pixels. Verified in PIXELS rather than by reading: in a 640x360 comp with a 200x356 portrait clip over a striped backdrop, switching it on changes 477,580 bytes and the pillarbox reads rgb(0,150,0) left and rgb(0,0,150) right — the layer's own green and blue halves at exactly 192*(1-0.22 dim), so it is neither a solid (left != right) nor the scene behind (the backdrop's stripes are gone). The subject's interior is byte-for-byte untouched, 0 of 542,800 bytes, and its edge energy is identical on and off (74.7486 both) — the fill is genuinely behind, not over. It tracks the layer through move, scale and rotate, and it burns into the EXPORT, confirmed by decoding the output rather than trusting the preview. The blur radius is multiplied by plateScale, which is the single most common bug in this codebase — an unscaled length is stronger in the preview than in the export. One defect caught at landing and worth recording: the diff moved js/compositor.js's cache-buster to ?v=252, which is exactly what HEAD already shipped, so the file's CONTENT changed while its URL did not and every returning user would have been served the old compositor from cache with the new registry alongside it. Bumped to 253. Suite 103/103 -> 109/109.
- v5.89 — a canvas tool now owns every tap, and no future tool can be forgotten. Ezra: "Still an issue where when I am editing a shape and tap on the canvas to select an edit point it just closes the editing window." The shape point editor's overlay (#pe-overlay) was not named in deselectOnEmptyTap's KEEP list, so a stationary tap anywhere on the comp — including the tap that PLACES a point, which is the whole gesture the tool exists for — read as "empty background", deselected the layer and tore the editor down with it. That listener is on DOCUMENT in the CAPTURE phase, so an overlay's own stopPropagation() cannot reach it; being named in that list was the only thing that spared a surface. THIS IS THE FIFTH TOOL TO SHIP MISSING FROM IT — the eyedropper and crop were the first two, touch-up the third, fill-drag the fourth (fixed in v5.67 after a verifier measured the identical bug returning), and the point editor now. A list that has gone stale five times is not a mechanism, it is a recurring outage, so this fixes the class rather than the instance. The same knowledge already existed one screen up: the Escape handler enumerates exactly which tools can be dismissed, because a tool that Escape closes is by definition a tool driving the canvas. FM.toolOwnsCanvas() is that set, asked once and consulted by the tap guard, so any future tool wired into Escape gets tap-protection for free and CANNOT be forgotten here. The ids stay in KEEP as belt-and-braces and to document the surfaces. The new test pins the CONTRACT rather than the list — it forces each tool's own isActive/isOpen/isPicking true in turn and requires FM.toolOwnsCanvas() to agree — so the next tool added to Escape and not to the guard fails here instead of in Ezra's hands. Mutation-proved: dropping the point editor back out of the set reproduces his exact bug and turns the test red naming it. Suite 109/109 -> 110/110.
- v5.90 — the invisible clip, explained and fixed. Ezra: "I just tried importing a 14 second long screen recording and it worked at first but the clip just doesn't show up, it's like invisible… feels like currently the mobile version is broken", and when asked which it was: "it showed up on the timeline but didn't have any actual video." A REGRESSION FROM TONIGHT WAS RULED OUT FIRST, with numbers rather than reasoning: the same 14s 1170x2532 clip imported into v5.72 and v5.79 behaves identically — rec 1170x2532, duration 12.828, readyState 1, preview ink 0.00% at import on BOTH, settling to 99.94% on BOTH. v5.74's clearClipStrip only runs on a project SWITCH and v5.77's mask/motion-blur changes need a mask or motion blur on the layer; neither is on the import path, and the measurements agree. THE REAL BUG IS OLDER AND IT IS A GENUINE HOLE. FM.loadVideoFile resolves on `loadedmetadata`, which promises DIMENSIONS and says nothing about a decoded frame. The compositor skips any video below readyState 2 (compositor.js:6764). The preview renders ON DEMAND — there is no idle loop — and addMediaLayer wired only `seeked`, which never fires when you seek an element to the time it is already at, which is exactly the case for a clip that starts at 0 with the playhead at 0. Measured with decode held at HAVE_METADATA: ink 0.00% at import, 0.00% after three seconds, and STILL 0.00% five seconds after readyState reached 4, with renderScene frozen at 14 calls. Only a manual scrub brought the picture back. On a fast desktop the filmstrip build happens to seek the element and its `seeked` events hide the hole entirely — a phone decoding a 14 second 1170x2532 screen recording is precisely where it does not. So the bug was invisible to every previous test and reliable on his device. FM.wireVideoRepaint now repaints on `loadeddata`/`canplay`, added ALONGSIDE every existing `seeked` listener so nothing that works today changes, and it is wired at import, at the three storage restore sites and at replaceMediaWith. After: ink 99.94% within 0.4s of the frame landing, with no interaction. Three more silent-invisible routes are closed with it: a 0x0 video now says "No picture in X — audio only" instead of painting a zero-width box in silence (gated on FM.mediaKind so songs stay quiet); a file that fires neither `loadedmetadata` nor `error` used to leave the import promise pending FOREVER, and now times out with a named error; and an import past the end of the comp is clamped with the playhead following, instead of landing in silent black four seconds beyond the work. Verified independently: suite 102/102 pristine -> 108/108 patched, all seven mutations killed, and the ordinary path untouched (import, scrub, second import, undo/redo, save-reload-reopen all identical). NOT FIXED AND SAID PLAINLY: a video whose header is intact but whose frame payload cannot be decoded is STILL a silent invisible layer, because loadVideoFile has already resolved by the time the element errors. That is the same class and it is going back for another round rather than being quietly left. Suite 110/110 -> 116/116.
- v5.91 — six duplicate entries leave the layer ⋯ menu. Ezra circled them and said "Remove the circled options in this menu": Duplicate, Duplicate in place, Copy, Paste Style…, Split at playhead, Move to playhead. Every one already has a door where the hand actually is — ⧉ in the top bar carries Duplicate / Copy / Paste Style, which is the surface Alight Motion uses for them, and Split sits beside the playhead in the transport row where it was deliberately moved in v5.x. This menu had quietly become the place a second copy of everything accumulated, which is the same complaint as queue 35 about the project ⋯ menu. The ACTIONS are untouched: FM.duplicateSelection, FM.copySelection, FM.openPasteStyle, FM.splitLayer and FM.moveLayerToPlayhead all still exist and are still reached from their real homes and from the keyboard — only the duplicate listing goes. One trap avoided on the way out: the `selCount` const at the top of FM.layerMenuItems carries a comment saying it must be DECLARED FIRST, because it once sat 36 lines lower and a const read before its declaration threw ReferenceError and killed the entire menu — nothing on right-click and nothing from ⋯ with a layer selected (Ezra at the time: "the three dots don't work when a layer selected"). The Duplicate labels that first exposed that are the ones just deleted, so the comment no longer described anything real; the grouping entries below still read selCount, so the const stays and the comment now says why the hazard is still live rather than pointing at code that is gone. Measured after: the menu is Lock / Reset transform / Save as preset / Save selection as element / the flip-and-fit block / Media Info / Delete, with none of the six present and the rest in their original order. Suite 116/116.
- v5.92 — the clapperboard icon stops stabbing itself. Ezra: "This shape looks a little shitty, fix it up so the lines aren't going through." Measured rather than eyeballed: the clapstick was an OPEN path forming a WEDGE 4.5 units thick at the hinge and only 1.5 at the tip, while the three stripes were fixed-length diagonals of identical length that fitted neither end of it. Against the wedge's top edge (y = 4.5 + 0.1818(x-4.5)) all three overshot by about 1.0; against the board's top edge at y=9, stripe two ended at 8.8 and its 0.8 round cap carried it to 9.6, and stripe three simply STARTED at 9.6 — entirely inside the board. That is the "lines going through": a stripe drawn across a bar that is not there any more. The clapstick is now a CLOSED bar of constant 3.6 thickness and the stripes are inset 0.8 from both of its edges, so a round cap cannot protrude at either end no matter the render scale — the same reasoning as v5.87, where nudging a line was rejected in favour of geometry that is exact at every scale. Declaration order is load-bearing again, exactly as it was for the Template icon two releases ago: stripes first, the bar over their ends, the board last. Three candidate redraws were rendered at 150px and compared side by side before choosing, rather than reasoned about in coordinates. Suite 116/116.
- v5.93 — dragging a clip stops throwing the panel up, and the open animation slows down. THE DRAG (queue 54, and he has now asked twice: "I still need it so I can drag clips on the timeline without it opening up the editing panel"). What shipped in v5.66 under this heading was the EFFECT ROWS in the inspector — a different code path that sounded the same. The timeline clip path was untouched: on touch, the hold-to-drag handler calls FM.selectLayer before starting the drag, deliberately, so you can see which clip you grabbed — but on a phone the inspector sheet is DERIVED from the selection (js/mobile.js syncSheet, which is itself a deliberate design: twenty-odd paths set selectedId directly and deriving the sheet from the selection is what stopped "you have a layer selected but the edit options don't show up"). So showing you the grab also threw the panel over the timeline you were dragging on. Grabbing a clip now STAMPS THE LAYER ID and the sheet leaves that one selection alone. TWO EARLIER SHAPES OF THIS FIX WERE CAUGHT BY THE SUITE and are worth recording, because both looked correct. First, a "drag in progress" predicate reading the live drag state: a clip drag can end by a path that leaves that state set, and the sheet then never opened again for the rest of the session — the suite went red immediately. Second, a bare boolean one-shot: syncSheet returns early when the viewport is not a phone, so a flag set on desktop survived until some later phone-sized sync consumed it and suppressed an unrelated panel — red again, same test. Stamping the id bounds the blast radius to the one selection it was meant for, and any other selection clears it on sight. THE ANIMATION: PUSH_MS 280 -> 380, as asked. It is now published to CSS as --fm-push-ms and every duration in the stylesheet reads it, because the number lived in five CSS rules plus a JS const — six places to keep in step, and this constant also drives the backstop timer, so a drift means a backstop firing before the animation it guards and a stranded transform on #app. NOT SHIPPED, deliberately: the reverse animation on the way back to home. The keyframes are written and correct, but wiring them made the v5.78 test go red for a real reason — it asserts #app never keeps a transform after a push, and a return animation legitimately puts one there. Weakening a guard that exists to prevent a permanent bug, at the end of a long session, to land a cosmetic nicety, is the wrong trade; it goes to a workflow with its own tests. Suite 116/116.
- v5.94 — benchmarks light up: hovering a benchmark on the ruler makes it glow and turns the timecode chip marker-yellow, and parking the playhead on one turns the chip yellow too (the half that works on a phone, where there is no hover). Tolerance is half a frame, so a chip that is yellow means you are exactly on it. Verified 8/8 with a real cursor: parked/one-frame-off/hover/leave/rebuild-while-hovering/rebuild-after-leaving/benchmark-deleted.
- v5.95 — text editing on a real device: with the keyboard up, iOS shifts the VISUAL viewport (visualViewport.offsetTop) but #stage is a normal-flow box in the LAYOUT viewport, and only its padding-bottom was ever recomputed — so the canvas was carried up by exactly offsetTop/2 and the text you were editing sat behind the toolbar. New js/screen.js (FM.screen) owns the viewport maths; text-edit.js now computes padding-TOP from it too, and teardown clears both paddings so a stale inline padding can't strand the canvas for the rest of the session. Reproduced at 390x844 with safe-area insets before fixing.
- v5.96 — you can favourite every effect now. The ★ only ever existed in tile(), so the Featured carousel in BOTH browsers built its cards without one — and since FX_FEATURED leads with the newest effects, the newest effects were exactly the ones you couldn't fave. Mask and Motion Blur (Object) had no star either, and readList() filtered any non-registry id straight back out, so a pseudo-entry could never have stuck even with one. One shared starFor() helper now serves every tile builder, the fav list tolerates the two pseudo-ids and renders them, and an applied effect's ⋯ menu (visual and audio) offers Favourite / Remove from favourites — you usually decide an effect is a keeper while using it, not while browsing.
- v5.97 — the effect thumbnails now use Ezra's own photographs. Fourteen shots (fx-art/, 320px squares, 243 KB the lot) replace the drawn art on five sections — color=golden CBD sunset, blur=dusk cityscape, distort=aerial tower grid, stylize=the chocolate Lab, other=the cleanest colour ramp — and nine more are wired to the individual effects they demonstrate better: the blazing sun for the glows and flares, the hyper-saturated pink beach for the grades, the cat for pixelate/mosaic/halftone, the silhouetted pair for the luma matte, and so on. The drawn art is NOT removed: it is what a tile paints until the JPEG decodes and what it keeps painting offline, so the browser never depends on an asset. Sharpen, Unsharp Mask, Temporal Denoise and Pixel Sort deliberately keep drawn art — a photo resampled to a 96px tile has no pixel-level detail left for them to act on, and the suite proved it (sharpen scored 7.80 against a threshold of 9).
- v5.98 — the playhead is no longer missing (or off-centre) when you open a project. recomputePad() published #timeline-panel's PAINTED left edge into --tl-panel-left, and the project-open push translates #app across the viewport for 380ms — so any rebuild landing inside that window stored an offset a whole viewport out, parking the centreline at x=-195 until the app was restarted. A partial read mid-slide is the same bug with a smaller number: the line lands slightly off and the track padding disagrees with it, which is the 'off-centred to the right' version. The trigger is the deferred filmstrip callback, which rebuilds 1.9ms after the push starts — which is why it hit projects with media (20/20 cold opens) and never shapes-only projects (0/8). panelLeft() now subtracts any ancestor transform, so it publishes the LAYOUT offset at every instant of the animation rather than being timed against it. Repro: 20/20 broken before, 0/20 after.
- v5.99 — scrubbing a heavy project is ~4x faster. The adaptive quality ladder already had a drag path, but its gate was a RATE test in disguise: noteMotion wanted 5 renders inside 250ms, i.e. a frame gap under 62.5ms, which is precisely what a scene heavy enough to need relief cannot reach — so the window reset before the 5th frame every time and the relief was available exactly when it wasn't needed. It is now a count of CONSECUTIVE renders each within 500ms of the last, and the idle timeout went 200ms -> 700ms because a single 216ms frame was firing it mid-drag and snapping back to full resolution. Measured on 4 layers x 4 effects at 390x844 dpr3, same probe both trees: before, the backing store never moved off 810x1440 and the median scrub gap was 329.5ms (3 fps); after, it drops to 270 wide and the median gap is 79.6ms (12.5 fps).
- v6.00 — stopped allocating a canvas every frame. FM.layerSize()'s text branch built a fresh canvas AND a fresh 2D context on every call, just to measureText; canvas-edit's update() calls it from render(), so with a text layer selected that ran 60 times a second — measured at exactly 1.00 canvases per rendered frame with text selected, 0.00 with a shape. One module-scope measuring context now, the same singleton pattern the rest of the file already uses.
- v6.01 — Edit Shape's Width and Height each got their own slider, matching Move & Transform's Size so the gesture is learned once: aspect LOCKED is one strip (both edges, ratio kept), Resize Freely opens a second strip and the two axes go independent. The rate is derived from the source size rather than fixed — a crop is measured in source pixels, so a constant rate would crawl on a 4K frame and bolt on a small one. Verified by dragging the real strips with a real cursor: 1920x1080 -> 1716x960 aspect kept when locked, then width-only and height-only when unlocked.
- v6.02 — Fill Behind's blur slider now blurs instead of zooming. The cover scale included the blur margin (s = max((W+2m)/sw, (H+2m)/sh) * zoom), so raising Blur enlarged the fill: measured 2.96x -> 14.07x cover scale across blur 0->200 on a 320x240 frame, and the fill's own colour edge moved 488 -> 906 px on 1080x1920. The margin is now paid for with surface instead of scale — the copy's outermost row/column are CLAMPED outward into an overscanned working plate, so there is nothing at the join for a seam to form from. Blur 200 also got ~1.6x FASTER, because the old code was paying for a 14x cover. Scale is now flat at every blur, zoom still zooms exactly, no vignette, and preview-vs-export scale went from 1.0385 to 1.0000.
- v6.03 — the person and woman shapes redrawn from one shared proportion table. Measured first, and it was NOT the car bug: neither had a SHAPE_ASPECT entry, so both spawned square and the head rendered 211x212 px — a true circle, nothing stretched. The defects were in the art: the man's torso was a literal rectangle (382px wide, zero taper, for a third of his height), his legs were only 31.9% of his height against a pictogram's ~57-60%, the legs merged into one ink run at 24px, and a hand arc undercut the hip by 13px a side. The woman had no shoulders at all — 1.04 head-widths across, against his 1.58 — and matched him nowhere below the neck despite a comment claiming otherwise.
- v6.04 — export got faster, cancellable, and safe to background. Five 'seeked' handlers repaint the PREVIEW, and the exporter seeks every video for every frame, so each export frame was also driving a full preview render nobody looks at: one handler had no guard at all and three guarded only playback. Measured A/B, video + 4 effects: 125.8s -> 84.6s, 1.49x faster, and the per-frame seek wait fell 487ms -> 285ms because the thread was free to service seek completions. Separately, run()'s frame loop had NO unconditional yield (runGif has had one for ages): on a machine whose encoder keeps up, a 360-frame export was ONE unbroken 763ms task with four progress updates painted and Cancel never honoured — the export ran to completion regardless. One await per frame drops the longest task to 54ms and lands Cancel in 148ms. It uses nextTick rather than setTimeout because a backgrounded tab clamps setTimeout ~84x, which turned a 60-frame MP4 export from 239ms into 3900ms.
- v6.05 — the audio no longer shreds when the picture struggles. It was never 'lag': once a frame takes longer than ~150ms, the >150ms drift check hard-seeks the playing media element, the seek stalls it, the stall guarantees the next frame is late too, and it fires again — on 100% of frames. Between seeks the element advanced 0.309s across 13.1s of wall clock, i.e. 2.4% of real time; it was being teleported, not played. Only 223 of 409 tone bursts survived, so nearly half the audio never played, with 31-49% of the timeline digital silence and dozens of hard clicks. Picture and sound stayed aligned on average precisely BECAUSE every frame re-pinned it — the cost was paid in missing material. Now the transport reads AudioContext.currentTime instead of accumulating rAF deltas, frames DROP instead of dragging the clock, and drift is corrected with a +/-10% playbackRate trim inside a 45ms deadband, hard-seeking only past 350ms. Measured at 300ms/frame: seeks 59 -> 1 (that one is play's own), silence 8971ms -> 1.2ms, clicks 34 -> 0, and every 50ms burst emitted in order. The picture paid instead: 555 frames dropped.
- v6.06 — Edge Glow rebuilt. It had one control and, whatever you set, it produced 0 pixels outside the layer — measured 0 of 76800 changed on a flat shape filling the frame. Now a Layer / Media / Both source toggle (Both by default), white instead of teal, and a Radius that reaches: 2px -> 4px of visible bloom, 60px -> 42px. Three blur passes, chosen by measuring the falloff — one pass gives a slab and a cliff, two a linear tent, three a real curve, four buys nothing. Radius is free (sliding-window box blur, O(N): radius 2, 8 and 60 are indistinguishable in cost). Old projects are byte-identical: CRC32 of the rendered frame matches HEAD for five saved instances, because an absent 'source' key can only mean 'saved before the control existed' and selects the entire old path. Verified by decoding the exported MP4 back — the bloom is in the file, within 3/255 of the preview.
- v6.07 — the project ⋯ menu is gone from the PC top bar (queue 35). It was audited entry by entry first, by driving every alternative door rather than reading the code: Canvas settings, guides, split, export marks, preview speed, timeline zoom, Open project and Keyboard shortcuts all already had a working control on screen, so they were deleted outright. Exactly three had NO other door anywhere — Trim to last clip, Save a project file and Reset project — and those moved into the settings cog first, so nothing was lost. The layer half of the menu was a pure duplicate of the right-click menu on both the clip and the row head (verified identical across group, text and shape). Note the phone's ⋯ is a different element and is untouched; it is still the only door to loop/onion-skin/snapping there, because the phone view bar's lower half is dead — logged separately.
- v6.08 — the gaps in a long audio clip's waveform are gone, and they were only ever in the DRAWING. Measured first, at 10/25/30/32/60/180/300s in WAV and AAC: the decoded buffer is sample-exact (max difference 4.6e-05, i.e. 16-bit quantisation), no zeroed runs, no dropped spans, and the export mix correlates 1.00000 — his song always rendered intact. The holes came from js/media.js:214, where the peak scan used stride = floor(duration/15): 1 up to 29s, 4 at a minute, 20 at five minutes, so at 5 minutes it judged each bar from 5% of the samples. A fixed stride aliases — every inspected sample lands at nearly the same phase near a multiple of the decimated rate, and when that phase is a zero crossing the bar reads as silence. On a deliberately FLAT constant-amplitude source it produced 6 deep notches and a ripple every 10s. Now a full scan, with the peak count following duration instead of a fixed 600. Second defect: the strip cache key was width + peak COUNT and never the peak VALUES, so a rebuild could never redraw a waveform whose peaks had changed.
- v6.09 — the crystal renders come off the four controls Ezra had given specific artwork to: export (desktop pill and phone square), the editor's + FAB and the home +. Each goes back to its OWN stroked glyph, which was never deleted — the PNGs were painted over the top, which is why this was CSS and not another artwork round. What replaces them is a real glass MATERIAL rather than a photograph of one: backdrop-filter refraction, a rim bright along the top-left and dark along the bottom-right so the pane has a light direction, one small off-centre specular catch, and a contact + ambient shadow. Export keeps an accent tint because it is the one primary action. The wordmark keeps its render — that is the logo, not a button. theme-glass.css was patched too: it loads later and was repainting all three PNGs, which would have left the shipped default theme unchanged.
- v6.10 — the open/close control on an effect row is findable now. It was an 11px '▸' text triangle in a 20px box, in dimmed grey — Ezra: "way too small, and it's kinda hard to someone who doesn't know what to do to figure out that ur supposed to tap on the effect to open and close." Tapping the row already worked; nothing about it LOOKED like it did. Now a stroked chevron at 17px in a 30px target, at full text contrast rather than dimmed, which rotates 90° when the row opens so it ends up pointing down into the controls it just revealed — movement reads as state far better than swapping one glyph for another. The row header gained a hover/press background so it looks pressable, and the chevron picks up the accent colour on hover. Plus a one-time line of text, the first time you ever see an effect row: "Tap an effect to open its controls" — the same one-shot pattern the effects browser already uses for its hold-for-presets gesture.
- v6.11 — Paste Style covers the categories the inspector actually has. Ezra: "there's 9 categories now not 6." The table was written when there were six; Speed, Volume, Element and Presets arrived later. Speed (rate + frame blend) and Volume (level, mute, both fades) are real style aspects and are in — both cloned and keyframe-re-anchored like every other animated value, and mute travels with the level so pasting onto a muted layer doesn't read as 'the paste did nothing'. PRESETS is deliberately NOT added: it is a browser of saved looks, not a property the layer carries, so there is nothing on the source to copy and a toggle would be a dead switch. Edit Points is left out for now as a real design question, not an oversight — pasting a 12-point path onto a rectangle has no obvious right answer.
- v6.12 — the nine inspector category icons are coloured, using the add-menu's gradient mechanism (no blanket stroke=currentColor, which would flatten every per-path paint back to one colour; ids namespaced fm-ci-* so they can't collide with the add menu's fm-ic-*). To Ezra's specifics: SPEED is a real speedometer — dial arc, ticks, needle and hub, with the REDLINE painted as its own band over the top-right third, because at 22px a gradient that merely ends in red doesn't read as one. BLENDING has two filled discs with the right one compositing via 'screen', so the overlap is a third colour the blend actually computes rather than a flat shape painted to imitate it. EFFECTS uses ONE gradient over the whole icon box referenced by every spike, so the rainbow blends continuously across them — per-path gradients would restart the ramp inside each spike, which is exactly what he said not to do. VOLUME has two states: a layer that cannot carry audio at all, or is muted, or is at zero, gets a single flat line instead of three waves. COLOR & FILL was changed rather than tinted. Edit Shape keeps a shape; Edit Points shows the same outline with its vertices as handles, switching on the same condition the label already uses.
- v6.13 — six from Ezra. (1) SETTINGS → clear the import history: the Add menu remembers every file you have ever imported (a browser can't read the camera roll, so that shortcut list is the whole feature) and there was no way to empty it except one long-press per tile. Two rows in Settings, Songs and Photos & videos, each with a live count and its own Clear; the panel STAYS OPEN so the count is the receipt, and clearing forgets the shortcut only — a project already using a file keeps it. (2) Home backdrop moves faster again (17s/12s, from 26s/19s — same travel in two thirds of the time); the intro rule carries the same pair, which is the line that re-pinned the old speed last time. (3) OPEN badge is real type on real glass instead of a PNG — no more upscaled 33px bitmap, no more risk of the word reading as a hole over a pale thumbnail — and the open project now wears a travelling glint around its border (a conic gradient masked down to a 1.5px ring, so it is bright without touching the contrast of anything you read). (4) The glass shine on the export button and the + buttons is properly blurred: it was three stops spending three quarters of its brightness in the first 46% of the radius, which is a step, and a step in a gradient reads as an EDGE — six stops on a Gaussian tail, dimmer at the core, no radius where it stops. (5) PC navigation: a real Back button (chevron + label) replaces the ▶ play triangle that used to be the only way home from the Studio rail; the project name now sits in the panel header where Ezra marked it; and the settings cog inside a project opens Canvas settings on desktop exactly as it does on the phone (app preferences are one labelled button away inside it) — the same gear meaning two different things by device was the "wrong settings menu" report. (6) "Color & Fill" is "Colouring", and its droplet runs violet→magenta→pink: pink-to-orange-to-yellow on a pointed teardrop was, unmistakably, a flame. Suite 156/156.
- v6.14 — the effect colours, and the last ⋯ menu. (1) Ezra: "make the colours for all of the effects brighter and more pretty." The hues were never wrong — the ALPHA was. Every category banner was rgba at .16–.32 over a near-black panel, and translucent paint on black is just a dark version of itself: Drawing & Edge composited to rgb(88,78,45), which is olive, and Stylize to rgb(88,52,63), a dull maroon. Doubling the alphas to ~.62/.40 brings all thirteen visual categories and all four audio ones up to the colours they were actually chosen as. Text stays neutral on purpose — it is the one category with no colour of its own. (2) The phone's project ⋯ is DELETED, which finishes queue 35 (the PC one went earlier). What unblocked it was v6.13's "App settings…" button in the canvas dialog: FM.settings used to be reachable from the home screen only, so on a phone that menu really had been the single door to snapping, onion skin, guides, trim and save/reset. Guides gained a settings row of its own — the last entry with nowhere else to be — and everything else already had a control: loop, preview speed, export marks and timeline zoom on the ⛶ view bar, split beside the playhead, canvas on the cog. FM.projectMoreItems is gone too, not just its buttons; the suite now asserts it CANNOT come back, and walks the phone's whole replacement path (cog → canvas → App settings → press Save for real). (3) Export sits above the cog in the Studio rail, by CSS order rather than by moving the node — the markup order is the classic layout's left-to-right reading order, where Export is rightmost because it is the last thing you do. Suite 156/156.
- v6.15 — the layer-disappearing bug, root-caused and fixed. Ezra: "I'm having a glitch where adding an effect to this layer for some reason makes the layer behind it invisible, really odd." It was ONE effect, and it is the first card in the Featured row: **Fill Behind**. Adding it to any layer — a 40x40 speck in the corner is enough — erased every layer beneath it, completely, in the exported file as well as the preview. Not a regression: it shipped like that in v5.88 and three checkouts (v5.88, v6.01, v6.11) all measure 100% loss, so it has been there the whole time. The mechanism: Fill Behind cover-scales the layer's own alpha until it contains the frame, then makes the plate opaque edge to edge on purpose and blits that frame-sized plate over the composite. Painting the whole frame IS the effect — but only when the frame around the layer is empty, and in a real project it is every layer below. Fixed by ORDER rather than by clipping: the fill is now laid onto the backdrop before any layer draws, and the subject draws for itself at its own z through the ordinary path. 194 of 195 effects render byte-identical to before, a 20-scene battery (groups plain/unit/mask/opacity/blend, adjustment, camera, 3D, text, solo, hidden) is 20/20 identical, and Fill Behind stacked with each of the other 194 in both orders adds zero occlusion. One honest cost: with layer opacity below 1 or a non-normal blend the fill now shows THROUGH a semi-transparent subject, because the two composite separately — inherent to "the fill is a backdrop". Two same-class bugs found and deliberately NOT fixed yet, because the right fix needs bounds plumbing rather than a one-line removal: Letterbox and Border Frame both manufacture opaque coverage where the layer has none. Suite 163/163 (7 new).
- v6.16 — sharper effect previews, and a preview/export mismatch found on the way. Ezra: "the quality of the effect previews need to be better, rn it looks shitty." They were rendered at 96² and then blown up: measured live, a tile's thumbnail box is 77 CSS px on a 375px phone and 154 on PC, and a featured card's is 148 — so on a DPR-2 screen a 96px bitmap was being upscaled between 1.6× and 3.2×. The tiles now render at 2× (192²) while the SCENE stays 96 units, so none of the 195 per-thumbnail demo values had to be re-tuned. About 3% more CPU (1.72 → 1.78 ms/effect) and a lazy cache that costs ~23MB after a realistic browse. Doing it required fixing a real bug first: `plateScale` is capped at 1 on purpose (a supersampled plate is a CPU multiplier for detail nobody sees) but it was also being used for `ctx.filter` and `ctx.shadow*` lengths, which are DEVICE pixels the transform never touches. So on any canvas rendering above 1:1 — a zoomed-in preview, not just a thumbnail — blur came out 38% weaker and glow 33% weaker than the same frame at 1:1, and than the export. A new uncapped `renderScale` is used at those five call sites only; exports are project-sized so they cannot change. Honest cost, measured rather than assumed: three effects whose signature IS the pixel grid read softer on their tiles, because their kernel still runs on a 96-unit plate that then gets scaled up — dither −31%, noise −10%, halftone −16%. Dither and noise got a coarser demo cell to claw most of it back; halftone did NOT, because bigger dots measured worse (−46%), and a tile that lies about the effect is the same failure as a soft one. Everything else — blur, glow, scanlines, pixelate, mosaic — is unchanged to within 2%. Suite 163/163.
- v6.17 — Select works on Templates and Elements, not just Projects. Ezra: "Selecting templates and elements doesnt work properly." It half-worked, which is the worse kind: tapping a template card in Select mode DID add it to the selection and the bar counted it — but every routine that updates a card in place (toggleSel, paintClasses, cardEls) finds its card with `.hm-card[data-pid]`, and only project cards ever carried that attribute. So the bar read "1 selected" while the card showed no tick and no outline. With Delete sitting on that same bar, a selection you cannot see is a genuinely dangerous kind of wrong. Two other things were projects-only for the same reason: holding a card to enter Select, and dragging across cards to take a run of them — on the other two tabs picking five things meant five taps after finding the header button. All of it now lives in `selectify()`, the helper whose entire stated purpose was that the three tabs "behave identically instead of approximately"; projectCard's private copy is deleted rather than left to drift again. Proved by mutation, not by reading: stripping data-pid back off the cards reproduces the exact symptom (bar says 1, card shows nothing), restoring it fixes it. The new test asserts the SCREEN — the tick on the card — because asserting the selection set is what would have passed on the broken build. Suite 164/164.
- v6.18 — the PC back button loses its label. Ezra: "the back button on pc doesnt need to say back, just make the arrow look nice." The word was there for a reason at v6.13 — an unlabelled arrow among a bar of unlabelled arrows reads as nothing — but that reason stopped applying the moment the ▶ play-triangle beside it was removed: it is alone in the corner every app puts back in. So the text node goes (aria-label carries the name, so nothing is lost to a screen reader) and the chevron is drawn as a control instead of an icon: a 34px round button, 38px in the Studio rail, a 2.4-weight stroke on a 20-22px glyph, and a hairline ring that only appears under the pointer. The chevron sits 1px right of geometric centre, because a left-pointing V carries its visual mass at the point and centring it by the box looks a pixel too far left — the same optical correction a play triangle needs the other way. Suite 164/164.
- v6.19 — a finger on a slider no longer eats your scroll (queue 65). Ezra: "sometimes when scrolling through an effect with lots of sliders it doesn't let me scroll up because I placed my finger on the slider, which is annoying." Two things killed the scroll before the finger had moved a single pixel: `.fx-scrub` carried `touch-action: none`, which tells the browser that element owns EVERY direction, and pointerdown called setPointerCapture plus preventDefault immediately. On an effect panel most of the panel IS slider, so most of the panel could not be scrolled. Now the CSS says `pan-y` — the browser keeps the vertical axis, the JS keeps the horizontal — and a touch stays PENDING until it proves which way it is going: past a 6px slop the winning axis takes the gesture, and a vertical one is released entirely (including attachGlide's velocity tracker, or a scroll-flick would release into a momentum glide and move a value you never touched). Scrubbing measures from where the finger LANDED, not from where the lock resolved, so nothing jumps by the slop. A mouse keeps the old immediate behaviour — you cannot scroll a panel by dragging with a mouse, so there is nothing to disambiguate. Worth recording what the mutation check showed, because it is worse than "it wouldn't scroll": with only the JS half reverted, a swipe 90px up with 8px of sideways drift — which is what a real thumb does — took Blur's radius from 6 to 0. Scrolling past a slider was silently destroying its value. Suite 165/165.
- v6.20 — every numeric inspector control is a real slider now (queue 67 + 31). Two of Ezra's reports were the same complaint: "the width and height in here actually need their own sliders" and "some effects use raw sliders". They already HAD width and height rows — but like 37 other controls across the inspector they were a browser `<input type="range">`, and in an app where every other number is an AM-style notched ruler with momentum, a range input is not a slider, it is the absence of one. Fixed in `rangeRow` itself rather than at 37 call sites, so Edit Shape's Width, mask Feather, gradient Angle, fill Opacity, stroke Line width, text Spacing / Line height / Curve, the audio fades, the camera's FOV / Focus / Depth of field and every plain numeric effect param all became the same control at once, and cannot drift apart again. Each caller's signature is untouched. Two decisions worth recording: no ◆ keyframe diamond, because most of these are read by the compositor as RAW numbers (layer.shapeW is read at four places and never through evalProp) and a diamond would have written keyframes the renderer ignores — a control that looks like it works and does nothing; and the row WRAPS like .fx-scrub-row does, because .fx-scrub is `flex: 1 1 100%` and would otherwise have crushed the label and the number into a sliver. Measured at 390px: strip 366px, sitting 12px inside the panel, no overflow. Suite 165/165.
- v6.21 — clip names stay at the clip's start (queue 73). Ezra: "currently the names of layers follow and stay on screen, I want them to just stay at the start of the layer and not move along with you." The label was tracking the clip's VISIBLE left edge as you scrolled, so the name slid along the bar and never left the screen. There was a real argument for that — you could always read a long clip's name — but it costs the thing a name is most useful for: a fixed mark showing where the clip actually begins. On a timeline of several long clips every label ends up crowded against the same left edge, where they all look alike. Now it sits at the start and scrolls away with it: measured at 1280x860, the label holds left:17px at scrolls of 0 / 600 / 1400 while its screen position travels 213 → -387 → -1187. The RIGHT-hand cap stays — that half fixed a different real defect (a name on a clip wider than the screen never reached its own ellipsis and ran out past the ≡ reorder handle), and it still needs a viewport-relative edge to bite on. Suite 166/166.
- v6.22 — the multi-select bar's Group and bin move to the right (queue 71). Ezra circled them and drew an arrow across the bar. They had been anchored LEFT, tucked against the back arrow, which put the most destructive button in the app about 90px from the control that means "leave" — two taps meaning opposite things, side by side, on a bar you use while dragging a selection. They are the rightmost controls now. The bin is NOT flush in the corner, and that is deliberate: measured at 390px, project mode packs the right-hand side solid — the cog holds 298..340 and Export 344..382 — and a thumb learns both positions and goes there without looking. Packing the two buttons to the edge put the bin at 330..372, directly over Export's 334..372, which the phone-bar test has guarded against since v5.71 and which it caught immediately. So Group, which is harmless, takes the outer slot at 340..382, and a 52px margin pushes the bin back to 242..284 — clear of both, and separated from its neighbour, which a destructive control should be anyway. Suite 166/166.
- v6.23 — moving film-grain static over every project card (queue 76). Ezra sent a reference photo: "a static effect to each project that is subtle and moves like how the static looks in this image", clarifying he meant the cards on the home screen. The noise tile is GENERATED at runtime rather than shipped: a 64px grain PNG is ~9KB of base64 that would otherwise have to live in the CSS or as an asset, and building it on a throwaway canvas costs about a millisecond, once, with the result cached and handed to CSS as --hm-static so all the cards share one decoded image. It is white grain with only the ALPHA varying — coloured noise would tint the user's own thumbnails — at .055 opacity, sitting UNDER the thumb and the text (z-index 1, against the glint's 3) rather than over them. It steps() between five unrelated offsets rather than sliding, because a smoothly-translating noise field reads as fabric moving past rather than as static; real static re-randomises every frame. 8 jumps a second is enough to read as alive at a fifth of the repaints of a per-frame animation, and it costs nothing off this screen — #home-screen is display:none inside a project and browsers do not animate what is not displayed. Reduced motion keeps the texture and drops the flicker. Suite 166/166.
- v6.24 — Onion skin, Snapping and Guides are one tap away again, on the ⛶ view bar. Ezra: "idk if the onion skin and snapping magnets are not where we discussed coz u havent done it yet or coz you forgot." Neither: they DID move into the settings panel as agreed, and then two later changes buried them. v6.13 pointed the cog at Canvas settings so PC and phone would finally agree, and v6.14 deleted the ⋯ that had been the other door — between them the route became cog → Canvas settings → App settings… → panel. Three taps deep for a toggle you flick on and off while working is functionally the same as gone, which is a good lesson about "moved somewhere sensible" not being the same as "still reachable". The view bar is their real home: its own note says every control there "is about HOW you're watching rather than what the project contains", which is exactly these three — they change nothing in the project and nothing in the export, and Loop was already sitting there. Each button PRESSES the control that owns the toggle rather than flipping a flag of its own, and reads its lit state back from FM.onionSkin / FM.timeline.isSnapping() / FM.showGuides, so the bar and the panel cannot disagree — a second copy of "is snapping on" is exactly how the old ⋯ menu used to show the wrong tick. They stay in the settings panel too: that is where you go to read the state of things, this is where you flick it. Suite 166/166.
- v6.25 — an Easter egg: pull the home list down past the top, keep pulling, and it slams back up and shakes the screen. Ezra asked for exactly this. Two things make it safe to add: the gesture only exists while the list is ALREADY at the top and you keep pulling — a dead gesture otherwise, so nothing is taken away to pay for it — and nothing fires below a 64px damped pull, so an ordinary overscroll stays silent. The pull follows `Math.pow(dy, .78)` rather than the finger 1:1, which is why every rubber-band scroll does the same: linear tracking feels like the list has come loose, a damped curve feels attached to something. It takes about a 207px drag to arm, which is the "keep swiping" he described. The return is deliberately NOT a spring — a spring says "gently corrected" — so it is a hard cubic that arrives fast and stops dead, and the SHAKE is on #home-screen rather than the list, because a slam that only moved the list would read as a scroll animation instead of an impact. Decaying amplitude with a slight rotation on the big beats: a purely vertical shake reads as a glitch, a twist reads as force. 420ms, a short haptic, and it is skipped entirely under prefers-reduced-motion — where a screen shake is not a joke but a problem, so the egg simply does not exist rather than firing a silent version of itself. Verified by driving real pointer events: a 220px pull damps to 67px and fires; a 28px pull moves 13px and does not. Suite 166/166.
- v6.26 — the overpull Easter egg works on a PC too. Ezra: "for the easter egg to work you should make it on pc when scrolling and you reach the top you can keep scrolling." He was right that v6.25 was unreachable on a desktop: it listened for a pointer DRAG, and nobody scrolls a list by dragging it with a mouse. The wheel path is the same gesture in the other input's language — deltaY < 0 at the top is the wheel equivalent of pulling the list down — feeding the identical accumulator, so both paths hit one shared threshold at the same felt effort. A wheel has no release event, so the burst is timed out at 130ms of silence: long enough to bridge the gaps inside one trackpad flick, short enough that two deliberate pushes count as two attempts rather than one. preventDefault (hence passive:false) keeps the browser's own overscroll bounce, and on some setups its back-navigation gesture, from running underneath ours. Measured: four wheel notches build a 107px pull and fire the slam; a single notch builds 36px and does not; scrolling DOWN at the top does nothing at all. Suite 166/166.
- v6.27 — returning to home now reverses the open animation (queue 60). Opening a project has pushed since v5.x — the card leaves left, the editor arrives from the right — but coming back was instant, so the two directions disagreed and the app felt like it only had a forward gear. The CSS for the reverse already existed and had simply never been wired: fm-pop-out (the editor leaving right), fm-pop-in (home returning from the left), the #add-fab viewport variant and the reduced-motion guard were all sitting there unused. Only RETURNING plays it — the first open of a session is the app arriving from the splash, and sliding home in from the left there would look like it came back from somewhere it has never been. That gate is its own flag rather than reusing introShown, which never flips under reduced motion and would have fired this on boot for exactly the people who least want it. The suite caught the one real hazard immediately: fm-pop-out is `animation-fill-mode: both`, so a pop still classed on when a push begins leaves its final frame applied to #app — and even at the identity matrix that is fatal, because ANY transform on #app makes it the containing block for every position:fixed panel in the editor. endPush now cancels a pop first. Suite 166/166.
- v6.28 — Fractal Ridges and Iridescence, both reworked (queue 63 + 64). Ezra asked for "more colour options and overlay options and animation options so it actually moves" on one, and a blur plus "you should be able to decide how much it actually moves" on the other. Measurement first, and it was worse than either of us thought: BOTH effects were completely static. Fractal Ridges at t=0 vs t=0.5 vs t=1.667 differed by exactly 0.000000 — `t` appeared in the schema twice but never reached the pixel loop — and Iridescence's Drift is a RATE defaulting to 0, so its phase never advanced either, which is also why both thumbnails cached as stills. Fractal Ridges gains Sharpness, Seed, a four-way Colour mode (Mono / Tint / Gradient / Spectrum) with two swatches, Bands, a four-way Overlay blend, Speed and Drift X/Y — and it now moves (t=0 vs t=0.5 measures 10.83, with the same-t control still at 0). Overlay was the point of his "overlay options": at Amount 1 the old Normal mode left the picture's luma correlated at −0.026, i.e. gone; Multiply/Screen/Overlay give +0.92/+0.88/+0.97. It also violated the plate-scale rule (roughness ratio 0.97 → 1.93 after threading `ps`), and a per-octave corner cache made it 60.3 → 35.6 ms/frame while doing strictly more. Iridescence gains Blur — which blurs only the hue triangle and re-multiplies by each pixel's own SHARP luma, so you get a soft oil-slick over sharp footage rather than blurred footage — and Motion, a bounded phase sweep in hue cycles rather than pixels, so it reads the same at any Scale. Every new key carries `legacy:`, so an instance saved before today reproduces its old picture exactly: 194 of 195 effects byte-identical, and a legacy Fractal Ridges instance identical in 14 of 14 cases. Verified independently after landing: fractal moves 10.83 (control 0), iridescence moves 34.8 and its Blur at 20 shifts the frame by 16.2. Suite 169/169.
- v6.29 — text adding on PC (queue 88). Ezra sent a screenshot: the toolbar stretched across a 2000px window with a huge empty gap, and "Type your text…" stranded a full viewport below it. It was never a regression — a desktop layout had simply never been written. `.te-bar` and `.te-dock` are base rules with NO media query at all, so the phone bottom-sheet WAS the desktop layout: measured, the toolbar's bottom edge and the field's top edge were 1114.8px apart at 2000x1250. The "empty gap" is not empty either — it is the font button stretched to 1686px by `flex: 1 1 auto` on a window-wide bar. A second, independent fault explains the tiny canvas in his shot: a `grid-template-columns: … !important` override written for the classic 2-column grid, applied to Studio's 3-column one, blew the 60px rail out to 1596px. And a third, which nobody had reported: on desktop, clicking the canvas committed and closed the editor, focus fell to BODY, and one Backspace then ran deleteSelected — a text layer destroyed by a click and a keystroke. Desktop is modeless now: a compact card docked to the stage, timeline and inspector still visible, the grid untouched, so the whole !important-override failure mode is gone rather than patched a third time. Gap 1114.8px → 0. Rail holds at 60px. Canvas 397x397 instead of a 404px sliver. Backspace after a canvas click leaves the layer alone. The phone is byte-identical across 16 measured properties at 390x844 and 375x812, with and without a 336px keyboard. The best finding is why this had been "fixed" three times already: the two existing mobile text tests assert PHONE geometry but were running inside run.html's 900x760 desktop iframe — they only ever passed because there was no desktop layout for them to be measuring instead. They run at a real phone width now. Suite 172/172. Three things left alone and written down: captions never open the editor at all, six other effects' option rows overflow a phone, and landscape phone is still cramped.
- v6.30 — preset tiles preview YOUR layer (queue 37, concrete version). Ezra: "the presets menu should show a preview of what the layer will look like when you add the effects." mountPreset took no layer at all, so every tile was a pure function of the preset — proved on the shipping DOM path: with a magenta shape selected versus a green text layer, two presets gave byte-identical frame multisets. Now each tile renders the live scene with the target layer's effect stack extended by exactly the instance addEffect would push, anchored at the playhead if it is inside the clip and at the clip start otherwise. The whole scene is rendered, not the layer alone, for measured reasons: a group renders nothing by itself (its members are found through scene.layers) and an adjustment layer only changes what is under it. The cache key carries the layer id AND a content hash over the layer's JSON, its media identity and the playhead — because undo/redo rebuilds layers with the same ids, so an id alone would serve a stale tile. A 10MB byte-capped LRU replaces a cache that never evicted anything. The queue renders under an 8ms slice and shortens the strip when the first frame is expensive. A second, worse bug fell out of building the instrument: when the fx-art photos finished decoding, the re-mount dropped the preset, generation threw, and the FALLBACK BALL was cached under the preset's key for the rest of the session — reproduced on the old build, fixed here. Measured on a phone viewport: 6 rows ready in 73-161ms; 0 of 6 rows identical between two layers; editing the layer changes every row and reverting restores it. Honestly reported rather than buried: at 4K with RGB Split one frame takes a 116ms slice and cannot be split, because nine kernels in compositor.js loop at full project size regardless of tile size. Suite 179/179. TWO THINGS FOR EZRA: the inspector card literally named "Presets" is a different, older system and is untouched — if that was the menu he meant, it moves; and NEXT-SESSION.md explicitly says not to build preset thumbnails and to build a full-screen preview player instead, which is a documented conflict with tonight's request.
- v6.31 — the playhead guard now covers the way BACK, not just the way in. The project-open push has had a test since v5.93: a timeline rebuild landing mid-animation must not store the TRANSLATED edge of #timeline-panel, because --tl-panel-left is then wrong for the rest of the session and the playhead parks off screen — that is the "playhead sometimes isn't there when a project opens" bug that needed an app restart to clear. v6.27 added two MORE animations that move #app (fm-pop-out / fm-pop-in, the return to home), so the identical hazard existed on the way back and nothing covered it. panelLeft() walks ancestors subtracting translateX and its comment already named all four keyframes, so this should have held — this test is what turns "should" into "does", and what stops the next animation being added without one. Measured: the stored var drifts under 1px across 14 frames of a real pop. The test also asserts the RAW rect drifted, as a control: if the panel never moved, no animation ran and a green result would be meaningless. Suite 180/180. The underlying report stays OPEN in REQUESTS.md rather than ticked, because Ezra hit it again after the first fix — so if it recurs it is a third cause and these two are now ruled out.
- v6.32 — one effect clipboard instead of two (queue 59). Ezra: "copy/paste button in the effects menu, and paste ONE effect." The buttons existed; the problem was that there were TWO clipboards and they could not see each other. FM.fxClipboard held a single effect in localStorage behind each row's ⋯ menu; FM.effectClipboard held the whole stack in memory behind the panel's Copy/Paste buttons. So "Copy effect" from a row's ⋯ followed by the panel's Paste did not paste that effect — the panel button was reading the other clipboard and was usually disabled, which reads as broken. There is one clipboard now, storing an ARRAY either way, so a single effect and a whole stack are the same shape and every Copy feeds every Paste. It also gains the localStorage backing the panel buttons never had — the in-memory one died on every reload, and copying a look from one project into another is exactly the case where you close and reopen something. Two smaller things fell out: the panel's Paste now says WHAT it will paste ("Paste 3") rather than leaving you to guess whether you are about to land one effect or somebody's whole stack; and the pre-v6.32 single-object format still reads, so a clipboard written by an older build pastes instead of silently reading as empty. The existing copy/paste test was updated to the array API rather than worked around — its assertions are unchanged, it just unwraps. Suite 181/181.
- v6.33 — the editing lag, root-caused and fixed. Ezra: "the lag is very bad very quickly" and "I don't think the software is rendering the canvas in a Lower quality, might be the issue". He was right, and the truth is worse than the guess: the preview DOES try to drop quality, and then gives up. Nine draw paths allocated their working plate at full project size and ran their per-pixel loop over it no matter how small the preview was — measured at a 0.35 preview on a 1080x1920 comp, compoundblur cost 154.6ms at full res and 143.3ms at reduced (ratio 1.08: the cost simply did not follow the preview down), against boxblur's 12.4 which did. So notePlaybackCost dropped a tier, measured no payoff because the dominant cost was resolution-independent, undid the drop and set _locked = 1. On a real 6-layer comp the old build ended at FULL resolution, locked, at 95.09 ms/frame with 191 of 295 frames dropped. Now those nine paths size their plate at plateScale and multiply the lengths that index it — same pattern drawPixelEffect already used. Playback: 95.09 → 17.43 ms/frame (5.5x) with 3 dropped frames instead of 191; a heavy 14-layer comp 396.75 → 54.81 (7.2x). Separately on the UI thread, FM.layersPanel.refresh() is a shim whose entire body is FM.timeline.rebuild(), and selectLayer called both — so every tap rebuilt the timeline TWICE — while inside each rebuild pxPerSec() re-read clientWidth once per clip mid-DOM-build, forcing a synchronous layout each time. At 80 layers a tap cost 32.8ms and 211 forced layouts; it is now 13.7ms and 5, and the layout count is FLAT with layer count rather than linear, which is precisely the "gets bad fast" as a project grows. Exports are untouched: 832 of 832 scenarios byte-identical across 208 setups x 4 project resolutions, rendered through the export path. The PREVIEW does change by 0.086% of sampled pixels — soft edges on feathered masks, 3D-tilt seams and pen-mask outlines, resampled at plate scale, the same documented trade drawPixelEffect already makes. Verified independently after landing: rgbsplit reads 8.2x fewer pixels at a 0.35 preview (was 1.21), with boxblur at 8.2 as an untouched control. Suite 185/185. Deliberately NOT fixed and written down: FM.media never releases a deleted clip's record, so memory grows with every discarded import — that needs undo-stack surgery.
- v6.34 — the voice recorder finally lands. Add menu → Audio → "Record voice…": record from the mic, stop, and it drops on the timeline as an audio layer beside Import audio rather than replacing it. This feature was built and verified weeks ago and then a laptop reboot wiped the staging directory before it was ever committed — it survived only because the agent that built it worked in a git worktree INSIDE the repo. It was cut against v5.87 and HEAD is v6.33, roughly forty releases later, so landing it was a rebase rather than an apply. Both predicted traps showed up exactly as expected and were handled: every cache-buster in the old diff was stale (taken from HEAD and re-bumped for the three files actually changed), and tests/tests.js had HEAD and the diff both ending MID-TEST sharing a single trailing "});" — keeping both naively gives two opens and one close, an unbalanced brace that takes the whole app down, which has happened on this file before. Verified after resolution: zero conflict markers, `git diff --diff-filter=U` empty (a --3way apply can leave a file UNMERGED while the suite still reports green), all 185 existing tests present in the same order, and styles.css a pure append with no selector collisions. The feature needed NO changes to work on today's code. End-to-end proof at 390x844 with touch emulation: the mic goes live then releases, a 2.16s 48kHz layer lands with peak 1.02 and RMS 0.092, the timeline draws a real waveform, and every control was hit-tested with elementFromPoint before clicking rather than click()ed blind. The negative control matters — the same measurement on a genuinely silent buffer returns peak 0, so "non-silent" means something. Caveat stated plainly: the audio path was exercised with Chrome's fake media device, not real hardware. Suite 190/190.
- v6.35 — Letterbox and Border Frame stop painting over the layers below (queue 89), on the FOURTH attempt. Both effects DRAW a frame instead of grading one, and both drew it against the effect plate's edges — and that plate is the size of the PROJECT, not of the layer. A Letterbox on a 40x40 speck laid its bars across the whole frame; Border Frame hugged the frame's border wherever the layer actually was. Each kernel then forced alpha to 255 over that region, manufacturing opaque pixels the layer never drew, and the plate composites at the layer's own z with source-over — so those pixels ERASED what was underneath. Measured on v6.34: Letterbox size 14 left 71.1% of the layer below alive, Border width 10 left 85.6%. Same mechanism as the Fill Behind bug fixed in v6.15, one level up. Both are bounded to the layer's own alpha box now, so they frame the thing they are attached to, and as a side effect cannot reach anything else. Three earlier passes were REFUSED, and the reasons are the useful part. (1) The first no longer APPLIED to current code, and `git apply --3way` exited 0 while writing conflict markers into the file — plain `--check` is the real bar. (2) The second snapped a layer to "full-frame" on a 4px tolerance measured in PLATE pixels, but a layer's margin shrinks with the plate: a 10px margin became 3.5px at preview scale, slipped under the tolerance, and erased the layer below while scale 1 read a clean 100%. (3) The third used `bb.x === 0`, exact about the wrong quantity — alphaBBox pads by 2 and samples on a stride of 2, so content 1-2.8 plate px inside the frame still reads as 0; scale a video slightly inside the frame, which is an ordinary thing to do, and the rim erased the layer below anyway. The signal is now taken from the pixels the question is actually about: fxTouchesEdge reads the plate's four edge LINES directly at alphaBBox's own threshold, under 1.2% of a scan already being done, exact by construction rather than by tolerance. That third pass also shipped an undisclosed regression of its own, fixed here: when the strided scan found nothing the code `return`ed out of drawPixelEffect BEFORE the drawImage that puts the layer on screen — which did not leave a thin layer unframed, it DELETED it. A 1-4px hairline divider or underline vanished from the composite in 39 of 240 thin-layer configurations at preview scales, and a 1px layer on an odd plate row vanished at scale 1 too, i.e. in the export. Two changes: the strided scan falls back to an exact one instead of giving up, and a missing box now skips the kernel only, never the blit. Verified with a 288-configuration byte-identity sweep against v6.34 — 0 full-frame configs changed (a saved project with a full-frame Letterbox renders identically), 0 control effects changed, 0 plain renders changed, and all 128 differences are the two effects on a non-full-frame layer, which IS the fix. Suite 198/198 at desktop and at 380px. Both new tests were mutation-checked: restoring either old behaviour turns exactly one test red while the other seven framing tests stay green — which is the proof those seven could never have caught this, and the reason the bar for this item was a wide sweep rather than the suite. My own first draft of the hairline test was decorative and I caught it the same way: it swept y values that straddle two rows, one of them even, so the strided scan always found the layer, the null branch never ran, and it passed against the broken build. tests/_hairline.html measures the real parities; the test now fails loudly if it stops reaching them. Left alone and written down: five sweep configurations are no-ops on a 200x6 layer at reduced plate scale, where the box rounds to 1px tall and a bar or a two-sided ring cannot be drawn inside it — pre-existing, unaffected at scale 1 so exports are fine, and fixing it means changing the kernel guards, which is a different job.
- v6.36 — adding a caption track now lands you in the editor. FM.addTextLayer ends by opening the text editor with the placeholder pre-selected, so adding text goes tap → keyboard up → type. FM.addCaptionLayer did everything else the same way and then simply stopped: it unshifted a track carrying two placeholder cues, selected it, and left you looking at them. A way in did exist — the cue buttons inside the Aa sheet — but that is several taps down a 46vh scroller, so in practice a caption track was a thing you could add and not type into. Found while fixing 88 and deliberately left as its own bug; this is it. The editor itself needed nothing: text-edit.js has been caption-aware since v5.99, binding to the CUE at the playhead rather than to layer.text, which the compositor never reads once captions exist. Two lines: scrub to the first cue, then start the editor with it selected — the same pair captions.js's own cue button already used, so there is one way to type a cue rather than two that behave differently. The scrub is not decoration: text-edit binds via captions.indexAt(layer, FM.time), so adding a track at t=4.5s bound the editor to the SECOND cue, and adding one past the seeded cues entirely made captions.addCue manufacture a blank third. Both halves are mutation-checked separately — remove the editor call and the test says the track lands with no way in; remove only the scrub and it says the playhead sits on cue 1, not cue 0. Suite 199/199 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.37 — the red delete bar stops flashing during a fast scroll (queue 58). The panel was in the DOM and PAINTED behind every effect row at all times, covered only by an opaque wrapper carrying a permanent `will-change: transform`. That promotes every row to its own compositor layer for the whole life of the list, and during a fast scroll the browser is free to present the scrolled parent before those layers have re-rastered — and what sits behind them is red. Worth being clear about what was NOT wrong, because it is where I would have looked first: the gesture code is fine and never entered swipe mode on a vertical drag (it requires |dx| > 8 AND |dx| > |dy| + 2), so no threshold or scroll guard would have helped. The fix is that there is nothing red left to reveal — `visibility: hidden` until a swipe is genuinely under way, with the layer promotion scoped to the same window, both driven by an `fx-swiping` class added the moment the gesture is confirmed horizontal and removed after the spring-back. It stays on through a committed delete on purpose, since the red is what the row animates out over. Verified in the real app at a phone width on the shipped glass theme, not just in the suite: four rows, red panel `hidden` on every one, `will-change: auto` on every one, no console errors. Both halves mutation-checked — put the old CSS back and the test reports the panel visible at rest; stop adding the class and it reports the swipe revealing nothing, which is the failure mode a naive "just delete the panel" fix would have shipped. Suite 200/200 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.38 — Group and Masking Group reach the PC (queue 53). Ezra: "PC is missing the Group and Mask options." The ACTIONS were never missing — the ⧉ layer-actions menu and the timeline right-click have both offered Group Selection / Masking Group all along — but the BUTTON was phone-only. #m-group lives inside `@media (max-width: 700px)`, and measured in a real 1440x900 window with two layers selected it reports zero-size, while the phone puts Group in the top bar the instant a second layer is selected. So on a PC the only route was an unlabelled icon partway down the screen, which is missing in the sense that counts. This is the phone's button in the desktop bar, opening the identical two-item menu and calling the identical FM.groupSelection — not a third implementation of grouping. It appears at 2+ selected and not at 1, because groupSelection needs two members and a button that does nothing when pressed is how a control stops being believed. (The phone holds its slot with `visibility` rather than `display` for a reason that does not apply here: there the bin sits beside it and must not move under a thumb mid-gesture.) Verified by driving the real app at 1440x900 rather than only in the suite: hidden at one selection, 31x31 and hit-tested REACHABLE at two, a real click opening a menu reading "Group" and "Masking Group — top layer clips the rest", and choosing Group turning 2 layers into 3 with both inside the new group. No console errors. Mutation-checked in both directions — never show it and the test reports the original queue-53 state; always show it and the test reports the dead button at one selection. Suite 201/201 at desktop and at 380px, phone unchanged.
- v6.39 — Speed re-times the keyframes, and works on every layer type (queue 68). Ezra: "if you add a bunch of effects with key frames you may want to make it go faster or slower, changing all the key frames automatically to slow or speed with the layer instead of manually doing it." Changing Speed already re-timed the CLIP — the source span is invariant, so the bar grows or shrinks — but every keyframe stayed at its absolute project time. Double the speed and the bar halved while the animation carried on running past the end of it, and you dragged every diamond back by hand. New FM.scaleLayerKeyframes stretches a layer's whole animation about its own start, and the Speed control calls it with the ratio of the durations that ACTUALLY resulted rather than the speed ratio — because duration is clamped, by a 0.1s floor and on a video by the source that is really left, and deriving the factor from speed would stretch the keyframes past a bar that had stopped growing.
