It runs per slider step, which is fine: each step scales by the ratio of two consecutive real durations, so the product telescopes to the exact total. The speed track itself is excluded, and that is load-bearing rather than tidy — a ramp's keyframes describe the re-timing, so scaling them by the re-timing they caused compounds, and every edit would re-time the ramp that produced it. Nonsense factors (0, negative, NaN, Infinity) are refused outright, because this rewrites times in place and a bad one does not look wrong, it destroys the timing. Second half: Speed is now offered on EVERY layer type. NOTE THIS LOOKS LIKE A REVERSAL OF QUEUE 38 AND IS NOT — 38 was "Speed does nothing on shape/text layers, but the control is still offered", and greying it out was the cheap answer to that; now that it stretches keyframes it does something on anything that can be animated, so the requirement (no dead control) is met by making it work rather than by hiding it. The old test was rewritten to assert the new contract, with the reasoning kept in the file, rather than deleted. Verified end-to-end through the real control on a SHAPE at 1440x900, not just in the suite: speed 1 -> 2 takes duration 4s -> 2s and keyframes 0/2/4 -> 0/1/2. Mutation-checked both ways — pivot at project zero instead of the clip start turns the arithmetic test red with [1,2,3]; restoring the source-only gate turns the availability test red. Suite 203/203 at desktop and at 380px. One thing I broke and caught: my first version of the rewritten test left a `/*` unclosed, which swallowed the rest of the test and took the WHOLE suite down with "FMTests did not load" — the same comment-fence trap that broke fx-thumbs.js earlier this run. A brace-balance check against HEAD's baseline found it in one pass.
- v6.40 — a full-screen Favourites browser with six orders (queue 74). Ezra: "swipe up for a full-screen Favourites browser… sorting by recency, effect type and A–Z, each with an inverted order." Three sorts, each invertible, and the invert is found by pressing the sort you already pressed rather than hiding behind a second icon nobody would look for. "By type" GROUPS under category headings — a type sort that produced one flat run would be sorted and unreadable, which is not what the word means to someone hunting for their blurs. The chosen order is remembered, because a sort you re-pick every time is a sort you stop using. Recency is DERIVED, not stored: toggleFav appends, so the array already is the record of when things were starred and newest-first is that reversed — no new persisted field and no migration for existing favourites. The flip side is that sorting must never write back through that array, or looking at your favourites in another order would destroy the recency record; favSorted works on a copy and the test asserts the stored order is untouched after four re-sorts. THE GESTURE IS A HANDLE, NOT A BARE SWIPE-UP, and that is a deliberate call: this browser scrolls vertically, so "swipe up over the Recents & favourites block" is the same gesture as "scroll down past it", and claiming it would make the page unscrollable exactly where you need to scroll. The strip takes the swipe and is also a real button, so the feature is not reachable only by a gesture nobody mentioned — the lesson from queue 53, where Group existed on PC and could not be found. Verified in the real app at 375px, not only in the suite: the handle reads "All favourites · 5", measures 347x35 and hit-tests reachable; Recent lists newest-starred first, A–Z and its inverse are correct, Type groups under BLUR and COLOR & LIGHT, and the stored array is byte-identical after all of it. Both orders mutation-checked — ignore the invert and the test names the failed flip; drop the recency reverse and it names the wrong order. (My first attempt at the second mutation deleted the `if` line and orphaned the following `else if`, which took the whole file down rather than testing anything; recorded because a mutation that breaks the parser proves nothing and it is easy to accept the red as success.) Suite 204/204 at desktop and at 380px. TWO THINGS FOR EZRA: the existing left-right pager between Recents and Favourites is UNTOUCHED — "replacing the swipe-right between Recent and Faves" reads two ways and I would not delete a working gesture on a coin-flip, so say which and it is a one-line change; and the effect category still reads "Color & Light" in American spelling, which sits oddly next to the "Colouring" rename from queue 83.
- v6.41 — a phone held sideways stops giving most of the screen to the timeline. 844x390 is a landscape phone, and it is over 700px WIDE, so it takes the desktop Studio layout — which gave the bottom band a flat 232px at every viewport HEIGHT. That is 60% of a 390px screen, leaving the stage 158px and the canvas 145x145, which is the "landscape text editing is cramped" note from v6.29. There were TWO ceilings, and fixing either alone would have LOOKED fixed while staying broken: the stylesheet's flat 232px, and clampH in the timeline resizer, which allowed 72% of the viewport height and PERSISTS whatever you dragged. Measured: a 270px band stored from a desktop session followed the app into a 390px-tall viewport and left a 120px stage — worse than the default it replaced, and the case that actually happens, because you drag the timeline on a big screen and then pick up your phone. Both now shrink only where there is nothing to spare, at the same 46vh, with a 150px floor so the timeline stays usable. 504px tall is where 46vh reaches 232px, so every desktop and tablet is byte-identical — including a height you deliberately dragged. Measured end to end in the real app rather than only in the suite, with fm_tl_h set to 270 to reproduce the bad case: at 844x390 the band clamps 270 → 179, the stage goes 158 → 211, the canvas 145x145 → 194x194 (+79% area), the timeline's track row keeps its full 42px and the text editor still opens; then at 1440x900 the same stored 270 comes back as exactly 270 with a 630px stage, so nothing a desktop user chose was taken away. Mutation-checked: restore the flat 72% ceiling and the test reports the stored 270 swallowing the phone again. The clamp is exposed as FM.clampTimelineH so the suite tests the function that actually runs rather than a copy of its arithmetic. Suite 205/205 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.42 — Squish lands (queue 48). Ezra asked for an effect where "the layer deforms against the canvas edges": the frame edges become solid, so a layer sliding off-frame squashes against the wall instead of being cut off. Put a Bounce ease on Position and the impact squash comes free. Six controls — amount, spread, bulge, firmness, inset, walls. Like the voice recorder, this was BUILT AND VERIFIED WEEKS AGO and then never committed; it survived only as uncommitted changes in a git worktree inside the repo, cut against v5.66, which is about 75 releases back. The rebase was smaller than feared and is worth recording precisely: js/compositor.js (551 lines — the whole effect) applied CLEANLY, and only the three small files needed hand-porting. Two of those ports would have been wrong done literally. FX_FEATURED has moved on since v5.66, and the standing rule is prepend-newest-and-trim, so Squish leads and Dispersion falls off the tail — applying the old line verbatim would have silently reverted three later effects out of the carousel. And the tests.js hunk turned out to be a pure insertion at exactly the anchor this session has been using all night (immediately before `async function run()`), so the mid-test merge trap that nearly took the app down on the voice recorder never engaged; I checked the hunk's shape before trusting that rather than after. The effect needed NO changes to work on today's code — notably it was already plate-scale aware (plateScale(ctx), inset x ps, __fmRS on its scratch canvas), which was my main worry about a diff predating the v6.33 plate-scale conversion. Verified: suite 214/214 at desktop and at 380px, with all nine of its own tests green. A 288-configuration byte-identity sweep against v6.41 came back 288/288 IDENTICAL — adding an effect that reorders the pipeline when present changed nothing that already existed. And the picture itself, rendered through the real compositor: a ball driven past the right wall is clipped to 100x140 (14,000 visible pixels) without the effect, and squashes to 100x212 (16,716) with it — it flattens against the edge and spreads perpendicular, keeping roughly its area, which is exactly the pitch.
- v6.43 — the warp effects stop changing strength with the preview quality (BUG-HUNT, high; "the dominant theme: preview != export"). drawWarpEffect passes the plate scale to each map function as a 10th argument precisely so that px-denominated controls can be converted into plate pixels. `curl` took it. Wave, Circular Ripple, Frosted Glass, Fractal Warp and Tile Shift did not even DECLARE it, so their amplitude, wavelength and tile size — numbers the user dials in as pixels — were read as REDUCED plate pixels. What that meant in practice: you set a wave you liked while scrubbing, hit play, the quality ladder dropped a tier, and the effect changed strength mid-playback — and the exported file matched neither. Measured on today's code, spread of a warped 200px square's extent (in PROJECT px) across render scales 1 / 0.5 / 0.28, before → after: **ripple 233.8% → 0.9%, fractal warp 60.2% → 1.8%, frosted glass 23.6% → 3.0%, wave 5.7% → 3.8%** — all now at the floor set by `curl`, the control, which measures 3.5% because it was already correct and that residue is rasterisation on a 0.28 plate. Exports are untouched, and that is measured rather than argued: every scale-1 extent is IDENTICAL before and after (259 / 231 / 260 / 200 / 256), because at ps === 1 every multiplication added here is a no-op. Fractal Warp needed more than its amplitude scaling — its twelve frequency DIVISORS are wavelengths in px as well, and scaling only the amplitude would have kept the displacement right while shrinking the pattern. Honest note: Tile Shift was in the report but measured ALREADY FLAT today at 3.6% both before and after, so the effect must have changed since v4.70; its conversion is correctness insurance, not a fix for anything I could observe. The new test carries `curl` as a control inside the sweep, so its threshold is anchored to how much spread is genuinely unavoidable instead of to a number I picked. Mutation-checked: the pre-fix compositor turns exactly that test red, naming ripple at 233.8%. Suite 215/215 at desktop and at 380px. One self-inflicted detour worth recording: my first edit put a `//` comment at the end of the replaced PREFIX of glass and tileshift, which are one-LINE functions — the comment swallowed the rest of the line including its closing brace and took the whole compositor down. Third variant of the comment-fence trap tonight; a brace-balance check found it in one pass, and block comments are the fix inside a single-line function.
- v6.44 — Particles appears in the preview instead of only in the export (BUG-HUNT, high; the same class as v6.43, one layer up). Every sibling motion effect — drift, orbit, wiggle, shake — takes the plate scale as a 10th argument. Particles declared nine, so it used layer.transform.x/y (PROJECT coordinates) directly as PLATE pixel coordinates for the emitter origin, and applied speed (px/s), gravity (px/s²) and the start/end sizes (px) as plate quantities. Its own fallback `cx = W * 0.5` is in plate units, which is what makes this a fact rather than a reading. The consequence was not "slightly off": a phone preview runs around a 0.3 plate, so an emitter at (540, 1200) in a 1080×1920 comp was placed at (540, 1200) on a 324×576 plate — off it entirely, and you saw no particles at all while editing, then found them in the exported video. Measured here on a 320×240 comp, the lit bounding box in PROJECT px at plate scale 1 / 0.5 / 0.28 went **11,0→235,207 · 22,0→318,238 (spilling to the frame edge) · 39,39→179,189 (collapsed onto the emitter, no particles)** before, and **11,0→235,207 · 12,0→234,206 · 11,0→236,204** after — the same rectangle to within a pixel or two. Scale 1 is untouched, so exports and 1:1 previews are unchanged: at ps === 1 every multiplication added is a no-op, and the measurement confirms it rather than assuming it. The parented/behaviour-driven branch needed care the amplitude cases did not — emitterWorldPos works in PROJECT units, so it is handed the fallback in project units and its RESULT is converted, instead of scaling its input twice. The test asserts the bounding BOX, not the pixel count, and that choice is the test: a count falls eightfold on a 0.28 plate for a perfectly correct effect, so it cannot tell "fewer pixels because the plate is smaller" from "the particles went somewhere else". Mutation-checked — the pre-fix compositor turns exactly that test red at 32.4% out. Suite 216/216 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.45 — Edit Shape stops silently destroying keyframed borders (BUG-HUNT, two high findings with one cause). Border & Shadow makes stroke.width and stroke.color keyframable, so both can legitimately hold a {kf:[…]} container — and Edit Shape bound them RAW, which is destructive in both directions at once. READING an object into an <input type=range> makes the browser quietly substitute the mid-range, so the row sat at 30 whatever the layer's real width was and printed the literal text "[object Object]"; the colour swatch stringified to "[object object]", failed its hex regex and came back #000000. The panel misreported the layer before you touched anything. WRITING replaced the whole container with a plain number or string: every border keyframe gone, silently, on one nudge, in the preview, the export and the saved project, with nothing on screen to say so. Four bindings fixed — Stroke width, Stroke color, and "Line width" in both the open-path branch and Edit Points — all now reading through FM.evalProp and writing through FM.setProp, which assigns plainly when a property is static and upserts a keyframe when it is animated. So an ordinary unkeyframed stroke behaves exactly as it always did; that is asserted too, because a fix that turned every slider nudge into a keyframe would be its own bug. THE TEST IS THE PART WORTH READING. My first version drove FM.setProp directly and passed — which proves nothing, because the defect was never in setProp, it was in what the row was BOUND to, and that version would stay green if someone rebound it to a raw assignment tomorrow. It now renders the real Edit Shape panel, finds the row by its label, and types into the box a person would type into. Mutation-checked against the pre-fix inspector: it goes red with "one stroke-width nudge replaced the keyframe container with 42 — every border-size keyframe destroyed silently". Suite 217/217 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.46 — a locked crop stays locked for the whole drag (BUG-HUNT, high). resizeCrop re-derived the locked ratio from the CURRENT crop on every call — `c.h / c.w` — while the same call wrote back an integer-rounded height. So each step's rounding became the next step's ratio, and the error compounded across the hundreds of pointermove events in a single gesture. Measured on a 1920x1080 source: a slow drag from Width 1920 down to 1016 produced h=508 instead of 572, i.e. 16:9 had quietly decayed to 2:1; and dragging to the minimum and back up produced a 901x901 SQUARE, because once the height bottoms out on the Math.max(1, …) floor with the width still large the derived ratio collapses to 1:1 and never recovers. The lock button went on saying the ratio was held the entire time, and preview and export were cropped to the wrong shape. The ratio is now captured ONCE and held for the gesture. It lives in the panel-builder scope, which is rebuilt on every FM.inspector.refresh() — and a scrub release refreshes — so its lifetime is exactly the drag with no new plumbing, and toggling the lock re-derives it, which is what you want after resizing freely. A clamped step no longer poisons the ones after it: the other axis is re-derived from the fixed ratio, so dragging back up restores the shape instead of keeping the square. **The test is weaker than tonight's others and says so in its own comment.** It models resizeCrop's arithmetic rather than driving it: the function is a closure inside the Edit Shape panel builder, its number box is scrubbed through attachGlide, and attachGlide did not respond to synthesised pointer events when I tried — I drove a 300-step drag at the real box and the crop never moved, so I could not verify through the DOM and am not claiming I did. What the test does have is a self-check that stops it becoming a tautology: it first asserts the MODEL still reproduces the decay, and only then that the fixed path avoids it. Suite 218/218 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.47 — two group bugs that made the app look broken (BUG-HUNT). FIRST: a layer added while inside Edit Group got no timeline row at all. addEmptyGroup and groupSelection nested into the open group; the other EIGHT creators — media, text, shape, path, camera, null, adjustment, captions — each did a bare `FM.scene.layers.unshift(layer)` with parent null, and the timeline's group view filters on inSubtree(layer, gctx), so a parentless layer is in no subtree and no row was ever built. Measured: layers went 3 → 4 → 5 while the timeline stayed at 2 rows, with no empty state either. The layer was selected and drawn on the canvas but had no clip — it could not be trimmed, moved in time, split, reordered or keyframed — and it was not actually in the group, so animating the group afterwards left it behind. On a phone the timeline IS the layer list, so it was unreachable until you happened to back out. All eight now route through one FM.insertLayer, which is the point: a helper rather than eight more copies of the line, so the ninth creator cannot forget it. A caller that has already chosen a parent keeps it. SECOND: deleteSelected left FM.groupContext pointing at a group it had just deleted. deleteLayer already validated this and so did history.restore on undo; deleteSelected did not, and Select All inside a group takes the group itself (selectAll takes every layer in the project, not just the ones in scope), so it is one keystroke away. Left dangling, the crumb kept showing a dead group's name, body.group-editing stayed set, the timeline rendered ZERO rows with no empty state, and any group created afterwards was written with `parent` pointing at a deleted id — then autosaved. One shared exitDeadGroupContext() now covers deleteSelected and resetProject, which had the same gap. Both mutation-checked, one test each. WORTH RECORDING because it nearly shipped: my first cut of the insertLayer helper replaced every `FM.scene.layers.unshift(layer);` in the file — including the one INSIDE the new helper — so insertLayer called itself and four tests died with "Maximum call stack size exceeded". A global string replace that also matches the replacement's own body is a trap worth naming; the suite caught it in one run. Suite 220/220 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.48 — two ways audio escaped and played where it shouldn't (BUG-HUNT). FIRST, playback followed you out of the editor. FM.requestPlay awaits the frame-cache decode — seconds for a reversed or frame-blend clip — and then called FM.play() unconditionally. Every navigation path only calls FM.pause(), which sets FM.playing = false; nothing cancelled the in-flight request, so the awaited continuation woke up and started playback for a screen that was no longer on. Verified in the report by driving the real requestPlay: at 800ms Home is open and playing is false; at 3400ms, decode done, playing is TRUE with Home still open, the playhead advancing, the play button showing PAUSE, the clip's audio coming out of the project browser with no transport in sight, and the rAF tick plus full render loop running behind the overlay — on a phone, on battery. With loop on it never stopped by itself, and opening a DIFFERENT project instead started that one playing on its own. A generation token now cancels it: FM.pause() bumps it, requestPlay captures it before awaiting and re-checks after EVERY await rather than only the last, and a project switch that has dropped the media recs is skipped instead of throwing. SECOND, toggling Reverse mid-playback left two audio paths running at once. FM.play() starts a forward clip's element unmuted, and the reversed branch of the tick only ever wrote currentTime — it never paused or muted, because "a reversed clip's element was never started" is an invariant set at play() time that neither reverse toggle re-established. So ticking Reverse during playback left the element emitting the clip's FORWARD audio at full level over a backwards picture, and because that branch also skips the volume/fade/solo reconcile, the stray audio then ignored every later volume, fade or mute change until you paused. Un-reversing was the mirror image: nothing stopped the synthesized reverse buffer, so backwards and forwards audio played together. Three parts: the tick's reversed branch now silences the element every frame (enforcing the invariant where it is READ, so a future caller cannot forget it), both reverse toggles call FM.reconcileAudio(), and reconcileAudio STOPS the synthesized buffer when no reversed clip is left instead of returning early and letting it run out. Mutation-checked on the token. Suite 221/221 at desktop and at 380px. Worth saying plainly: the reverse-toggle half is reasoned and tested structurally, not LISTENED to — same limit as everything audio tonight.
- v6.49 — two edits that could not be undone (BUG-HUNT, both in the destroys-work-with-no-way-back class). FIRST, the AI validator accepted setProp path 'solo'. That flag is still live in the engine — the compositor does `if (soloActive && !L.solo) continue;`, the exporter mirrors it for audio, and soloSilenced mutes the rest in preview — but the per-layer toggle that could turn it OFF was removed in v1.75, and this case outlived the UI that could reverse it. Ordinary editor wording ("solo the hero title", "just show the title") makes the model emit it; every other layer then vanishes from the canvas AND the exported file, non-soloed video goes silent, and because solo lives on the layer object it is autosaved and survives reload — with no control anywhere in the app to clear it. The project just looks permanently gutted. The case is deleted; the op now falls through and is dropped with a logged reason like any other path we do not honour. SECOND, "Replace media…" produced no undo step and destroyed the original file. The replace changed only out-of-history state, so on an image→image swap NOTHING in the layer's JSON changed and history.commit's identical-state guard swallowed it — the next Ctrl+Z landed on a previous, unrelated action while the media stayed replaced. Verified in the report: one Ctrl+Z deleted an unrelated rectangle and the replaced image stayed replaced, with further undos unwinding edits the user never meant to touch. And the outgoing blob had already been deleted from IndexedDB, so the footage was unrecoverable at every step. Two halves: a serialisable `mediaRev` marker puts the replace INSIDE the history snapshot so the commit is a real step, and the blob is no longer deleted — an undo step is worthless if the file it would restore has been erased. Orphans are reaped by the boot sweep, the same rule deleteLayer already follows for exactly this reason. THE AI TEST IS WORTH A LINE. My first version drove FM.aiOps.apply and PASSED — because the AI panel is not wired in the harness, so it took an early return while the defect sat there untouched. It now fetches js/ai-ops.js and asserts the case does not EXIST, which holds whether or not the panel is live. Third time tonight a test could not reach the thing it was named after; all three were caught by mutation rather than by reading. Both mutation-checked. Suite 223/223 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.50 — the AI panel's two repeat-click buttons stop spending past the cap and stop faking their answers (BUG-HUNT, two findings in one file). Both live on the done screen — the Refine box and the ↻ re-roll buttons — which are exactly the two things a person clicks over and over. FIRST, NEITHER CHECKED THE SPEND CAP. generateScene gates both of its expensive stages on it; these had no check at all, and each click fires a full Opus VISION request — the capability digest as the system prompt plus a base64 PNG of the rendered frame, maxTokens 1500 — on YOUR OWN key. Past the cap the budget ring saturates at 1, so it stops conveying how much is going out. This is real money on a BYOK key, and the 25¢ cap the app enforces on its own pipeline was trivially exceeded by the two most-clicked buttons in it. Both now carry the same gate generateScene uses. SECOND, BOTH DERIVED "dry run" FROM A STICKY FLAG. Watching the no-key demo set _lastBuild.dry = true and nothing ever cleared it, so with a real key saved every later Refine silently ran the MOCK — no network, no model. The instruction was thrown away (the mock keyword-matches only gold/red/blue/green/big/small/bold/glow, so "make the background dark navy" fell through to a hardcoded fallback and added a drop shadow instead), the op was applied and committed to history, and the panel reported "Refined your scene · 1 op · Critic · Opus" for a call that never happened. Both now derive it from the live key. The test asserts against the SHIPPED SOURCE, for the same reason as the ai-ops solo test: the AI panel is not wired in the harness, so anything driving FM.ai would take an early return and pass while the defect sat there — and "this gate must be present" is exactly what a source assertion is good for. Mutation-checked. Suite 224/224 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.51 — an export can no longer be handed the PREVIEW frame cache (BUG-HUNT, high; the report lists it twice under two finders, which is a fair reflection of how bad it is). FM.buildFrameCache de-duplicates concurrent builds on the media record. The REUSE check was already careful — it compares fps AND scaled-ness, precisely so a downscaled preview cache is never silently reused for a full-res export — but the very next line handed back ANY running build regardless of what it had been started with. prepareCaches exists specifically to guarantee a full-resolution export cache: it force-clears a `scaled` one, then calls with no maxDim. While a PREVIEW build is still running, rec.frameCache is still null (it is only assigned when the build finishes), so that clear is a no-op and the export got the preview promise, awaited it, and encoded from it. What that delivered: a reversed or frame-blend clip encoded from 640px bitmaps on mobile or 960px on desktop, upscaled to the layer's full frame box — visibly soft and blocky in the MP4, GIF or PNG sequence while every other layer is sharp — and carrying at most 24 distinct frames a second inside a 30 or 60 fps export. The trigger is the ordinary flow, not a corner: opening a project with a reversed clip fires ensureReverseCache on load, that decode takes seconds to minutes, and pressing Export while "Preparing frames…" is still up is exactly what someone does. No warning, and re-exporting a minute later silently produces a different, sharper file — which is the kind of thing that makes you distrust the whole tool. The in-flight dedupe is keyed on fps and scaled-ness now; a request for a different shape waits for the running build and then builds the one that was actually asked for. prepareCaches also re-checks after any in-flight build settles, as belt and braces. Mutation-checked: reverting frames.js turns exactly that test red. Suite 225/225 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.52 — a text layer's anchor finally does something (BUG-HUNT). Every other layer type offsets its content by the anchor — shapes by `-sw * anchorX(tr)`, media by `-w * anchorX(tr)` — but the text branch drew at x=0 governed by textAlign and at `i*lh - total/2` vertically, i.e. permanently pinned to 0.5/0.5. That mattered because the REST OF THE APP already believed it worked: the inspector compensates x/y on every anchor write so the layer stays visually still, and canvas-edit hit-tests against `-w*ax .. w*(1-ax)`. With the compositor ignoring the anchor, that compensation had nothing to cancel, so the text SLID — measured, and my test reproduces the report's numbers independently: "HELLO" at fontSize 40 went from canvas x 97..224 to 31..158 on an anchor-0 write, a 66px jump. That is the exact opposite of what the anchor placer promises ("Keep it visually still"). The pivot did not move either, so scaling and rotating still happened about the centre, and the selection box drifted off the glyphs so the text could no longer be tapped to select. Text is laid out centred, so its box spans -w/2..w/2; translating by (0.5 - ax) * w maps that onto -w*ax .. w*(1-ax), which is exactly what the shape branch produces and what the inspector and the hit-test already assume. AT THE DEFAULT 0.5 THE TRANSLATE IS ZERO, so every layer that never touched its anchor renders byte-identically — asserted in the test, not just claimed. FLAGGED HONESTLY: a saved text layer that DOES carry a non-default anchor will now render in a different place. That is the place it was always meant to be, and there is no fix that leaves those layers untouched, because being untouched IS the bug. Mutation-checked. Suite 226/226 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.53 — Edit Points agrees with the compositor about which way is left (BUG-HUNT). applyLayerTransform ends with `ctx.scale(flipH ? -1 : 1, flipV ? -1 : 1)`, so the mirror is the INNERMOST step of a layer's placement matrix. point-edit re-derives that matrix by hand and composed only T·R·S·K — it never read flipH or flipV at all. So with a flip on, the point markers, insert rings, curve preview and tangent handles were drawn mirrored about the anchor, frequently on the opposite side of the shape actually on screen. Worse than cosmetic: toLocal is the exact inverse of the same wrong matrix, so the overlay tracked your finger perfectly while the RENDERED point moved the other way — drag a handle right and the shape's point goes left, which is the kind of thing that makes you think the tool is broken rather than mirrored. The X/Y readouts reported the mirrored position too. It is two taps apart in normal use: "Flip Horizontally" sits on every layer's ⋯ menu with no type guard, and opening Edit Shape auto-enters Edit Points. The flip is applied innermost in toCanvas (before the skew, matching the compositor) and undone last in toLocal. Measured: at flipH the u=1 edge of a 100px-wide layer centred at x=200 mapped to 250 — still right of centre — and now maps to 150, a clean mirror about the anchor. toCanvas → toLocal round-trips to 1e-6 with both flips on, which matters because that inverse is what the drag uses and a self-consistently wrong pair still tracks the finger. toCanvas/toLocal are exposed on FM.pointEdit for the suite, because the bug lived in exactly that pair and a test has to call them rather than a copy of their arithmetic. Mutation-checked. NOT fixed and left in the report: the same function also ignores layer.parent, so a PARENTED point shape's overlay still sits at raw local coordinates — canvas-edit handles that via parentXform and point-edit does not. Different fix, and it wants the compositor's own _layerCTM rather than more hand-derivation. Suite 227/227 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.54 — Edit Points stops re-deriving the compositor's matrix and just asks for it (BUG-HUNT, the parent half of the finding v6.53 half-closed). point-edit built the layer's placement matrix by hand from scale/rotation/skew/anchor, and every drift between that copy and the real one has produced the same failure: the overlay tracks your finger while the rendered point goes somewhere else, because toLocal is the exact inverse of the same wrong matrix. v6.53 added the missing flips. Still missing was the PARENT CHAIN — a parented point shape's markers sat at the layer's raw local coordinates, detached from where the compositor actually draws it. Measured with the fallback forced: the overlay put the top-left point at (-10, -20), off the canvas entirely, while the compositor drew it at (251.9, 58.8). toCanvas and toLocal now go through FM._layerCTM, which runs the compositor's own applyLayerTransform into a probe context and returns the resulting matrix — so it carries the parent chain, the z-perspective and the flips by construction. It cannot drift, because it IS the thing the hand-derivation was trying to reproduce. The hand path stays as a fallback for browsers where getTransform is unavailable, and it keeps v6.53's flip fix, so that route is correct too rather than merely present. The new test states the contract directly — for any layer, parented or not, the overlay's mapping must equal the compositor's matrix — and checks the inverse round-trips through a rotated, scaled, parented rig, because that inverse is what a drag uses and a self-consistently wrong pair still tracks the finger. Mutation-checked by forcing the fallback. Suite 228/228 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.55 — a fade-in on a reversed clip no longer starts part-way up (BUG-HUNT). When a reversed clip begins AFTER the playhead, `into` is negative and the source is correctly scheduled to start at `base = when - into/pr`, a future time. But the fade envelope was anchored at `when`, not at `base`: `setValueAtTime(0, when)` followed by `linearRampToValueAtTime(vol, base + fi/pr)` is ONE straight line spanning the silent gap AND the fade window, so by the time the audio actually starts the gain has already climbed to vol × |into| / (|into| + fi). That is a pop at the top of the fade — the opposite of what a fade-in is for. MEASURED, not reasoned: I rendered both scheduling patterns through a real OfflineAudioContext and read the envelope back. With a 0.4s gap and a 0.6s fade, the old pattern reads **0.40 at the moment the audio starts** — matching the predicted formula exactly, which is what independently confirms the diagnosis — and 0.70 at the fade's midpoint. Anchored, it reads 0.000 at the start and 0.50 at the midpoint, i.e. an actual fade. The fix re-anchors with `Math.max(when, base)`, which is a no-op whenever the clip has already started (`into >= 0`, so `base <= when`). Worth noting WHY this counts as an oversight rather than a design choice: the keyframed-volume branch immediately above already clamps every point with `Math.max(when, base + b/pr)` for exactly this reason — this branch just never got the same treatment. Suite 228/228 at desktop and at 380px.
- *(no version — a measurement, and a correction of my own advice)* The v6.55 fade fix showed that Web Audio envelopes ARE measurable here: render through an OfflineAudioContext and read the samples back. So I turned that on the thing I had told Ezra needed his ears — whether the three cheap-to-rebuild audio params (Distortion Drive, Bit Crush Bits, Lo-Fi Amount) can be keyframed. A click is a sample-level discontinuity, so it is measurable after all. Sweeping Bit Crush's curve every frame across its full range gives a worst sample-to-sample jump of 0.2127 against a static-curve control of 0.0313 — **6.8x, an audible click**. And the shape of it is not what I would have guessed: it is not about how far you sweep. 12 → 8 bits measures 0.031 (clean, identical to the control) while **6 → 5 bits — one single step — measures 0.0544, 1.7x the control and audible**, because at low bit counts the quantisation levels are coarse and a small change moves every output sample. So the earlier note in REQUESTS.md ("these three genuinely could be done") was right about COST and wrong about VIABILITY, and I have corrected it in place rather than leaving him to discover it by ear: Bit Crush is keyframable in its upper range and clicks in the low range, which is precisely where anyone would want to automate it. Then I measured those two as well, with their real shipped curve builders, and **all three click**: Bit Crush 6.8x its static control, Distortion 2.8x, Lo-Fi 1.7x. My "smooth curves may be clean" caveat was also wrong — Distortion is a tanh at 4x oversampling and is the second worst. THE REASON MATTERS because it points at the real fix: every one of these is implemented as "rebuild the transfer curve", and swapping a curve is a STEP CHANGE — the same input sample maps to a different output the instant it happens. Smoothness *in x* cannot help; what is missing is continuity *in time*. Animating these properly means crossfading two shaper nodes, or driving the parameter through a real AudioParam instead of a curve rebuild — a bigger job than flipping a `keyframable` flag, which is exactly the thing worth knowing before starting rather than after.
- v6.56 — the pattern effects stop resizing when the preview quality changes (BUG-HUNT; the same class as v6.43 and v6.44, third instance). drawPixelEffect hands every pixel function the plate scale as its 6th argument, with the contract stated inline: "effects sized in ABSOLUTE pixels multiply by it so a reduced plate still matches the export". Halftone honours it. Checkerboard, Stripes, Crosshatch and Block Noise did not even declare the parameter, so their px-denominated size/spacing indexed REDUCED plate pixels and the pattern period in project space became size/ps. Press play and the quality ladder changes the tier — the checkerboard visibly resizes mid-playback, and the exported file matches none of it. Measured with a median half-period along the centre row, converted back to project px, at plate scale 1 / 0.5 / 0.28: **checker 24 → 48 → 85.7 (257% spread) becomes 24 → 24 → 25 (4.2%); stripes 8 → 16 → 28.6 (257%) becomes 8 → 8 → 10.7 (33.9%)** — the residue on stripes is integer rounding of a 4-plate-pixel period at 0.28, not drift. Scale 1 is identical before and after (24 and 8), so exports and 1:1 previews are unchanged. STATED PLAINLY: crosshatch and blocknoise got the same one-line treatment but are NOT measured here — my probe reads horizontal runs along one row and neither draws that shape, which I know because the HALFTONE CONTROL also reads zero. The control is doing its job: it says the instrument cannot see this family, rather than letting me report a clean number for something I did not observe. And a self-inflicted one worth recording: the first cut of that probe filled the layer #808080 and thresholded at >128, so every sample read false and all four patterns measured 0 — the control caught it immediately, which is the entire argument for putting a known-good case inside the sweep. Suite 228/228 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.57 — two stacked Pixelate effects stop letting the sharp original bleed through (BUG-HUNT). drawPixelate renders the clean layer into a module SINGLETON plate and then re-enters drawLayer, so with a second Pixelate above it the inner call's target IS that plate — already holding the clean, un-pixelated layer — and it composited source-over without clearing. The result was a mosaic with the sharp original showing through everywhere pixelation had made the plate transparent. Every sibling effect (tint, posterize, threshold, duotone, RGB split, fog) has a `ctx.canvas === scratch` guard for exactly this; pixelate had none. The guard alone is NOT the fix here, which is why it needed more than a one-liner: the `size <= 1` early return blits the plate into ctx, so clearing first would erase its own source on that path. Depth-pooling the plates removes the aliasing entirely instead of special-casing each branch, and is the pattern this file already uses for pixel effects and 3D tilt — and the same pattern the still-open nested-group unit bug will need. Measured on a 240x240 comp, 100x100 shape, stack [coarse 40, fine 12]: **pre-fix 9216 fully-opaque pixels, which is EXACTLY what a single fine pass alone gives — the coarse stage was being undone. Post-fix 5184, genuinely coarser.** 4032 pixels differ, max alpha delta 255. TWO PROBE MISTAKES WORTH RECORDING, both mine, both caught before they became a false green: the first version stacked two pixelates at IDENTICAL sizes, which is idempotent and can never show the bug (0 differing — I nearly reported that as "fixed"); and the first comparison was one-effect vs two-effect on the same build, which conflates "the second effect did something" with "the ghost is gone". The right measurement is the same configuration across the two builds. That is the second probe tonight where the instrument, not the app, was what needed fixing first. Suite 228/228 at desktop and at 380px.
- v6.58 — stacking two Mirror effects no longer undoes the first one. drawMirror rendered the clean layer into one shared scratch plate and then blitted the mirrored strips out of it, so a second Mirror ended up reading and writing the same bitmap: its blits landed on top of the pixels they were reading and replaced nothing, making the inner Mirror a complete no-op. That is the standard kaleidoscope build (Left→Right plus Top→Bottom), and the visible symptom was worse than "one effect does nothing" — with a square sitting entirely in the discarded half, adding the second Mirror RESURRECTED it at its original position, 2500 pixels the first Mirror had correctly removed. Each nested Mirror now gets its own plate. Same fix and same cause as v6.57's Pixelate. Verified before/after on the exact reported scene, and locked in by a regression test that was mutation-checked against the old build.
- v6.59 — adding Tiles with "Repeat: Whole clip" no longer throws away the other effect on the layer. Every canvas effect renders the clean layer into one shared scratch plate and writes its result into a second one; Tiles' whole-clip mode also asks for a bigger plate reaching past the frame, and building THAT re-rendered the layer, which ran the layer's other effect again and overwrote both plates underneath the Tiles render still using them. The result was not subtle: a square drifting across the frame under Tiles came out with a full-size copy of itself stamped at its starting position, pixel-for-pixel identical to deleting the Drift effect altogether. Each nested render now gets its own plates, including the expanded one, which had the same fault a level up. Same family as v6.57's Pixelate and v6.58's Mirror — this was the last of the three. Verified before/after on the reported scene and locked in by a mutation-checked regression test; suite 230/230 at desktop and 380px.
- v6.60 — a group inside another group finally behaves. Putting a group inside a group and setting opacity (or an effect, or a blend mode) on both did not do what it said: depending on which order you happened to create them in, either the inner group was silently ignored — its opacity, effects and blend simply gone, no error — or its contents were drawn twice, once per group, coming out much too solid. A leaf at 50% inside a group at 50% should read 25%; it read 50% one way and about 75% the other. Groups now nest properly rather than competing for the same layers. Checked hard because this is the code the export runs through: 16 grouped scenes rendered at export size before and after, and every non-nested one — plain group, group with an effect, blend mode, mask, two side-by-side groups, a group with an adjustment layer, a colour-graded group — comes out pixel-for-pixel identical. Only the nested ones changed, which is the whole point. Suite 231/231 at desktop and 380px.
- v6.61 — favourites are easy to get to now (queue 92). The "Recents & favourites" block used to be a sideways pager: Recents was page one and everything you had starred hid on pages two and three behind a swipe right, marked only by three small grey dots. That paging is gone. The block is just Recents, and pulling DOWN on it opens the full favourites browser — the block follows your finger with a bit of resistance, a ★ Favourites label is uncovered behind it and turns blue once you have pulled far enough to let go, and the browser slides in from the direction you were already pulling. The "All favourites" strip is still there and still just a tap, so nothing is reachable only by a gesture. The pull only arms when the list is already at the top, which is what stops it fighting normal scrolling, and it is not armed at all if you have Reduce Motion on. The sideways pager stays in the AUDIO effects browser for now — say the word and it gets the same treatment.
- v6.62 — the film grain on the home cards is calmer (queue 94). Ezra: "the film grain in the menu is too jumpy and too noticeable, need to make it move smoothly and less noticeable." It was deliberately jumpy — v6.23 stepped it between five unrelated offsets 8 times a second on the theory that real static re-randomises every frame — but on his phone that reads as flicker rather than texture. Both dials came down independently: it is now 42% fainter (.055 -> .032) and it DRIFTS, one unbroken 19-second loop instead of 8 jumps a second. The offsets are multiples of the 64px noise tile so the loop closes with no visible seam. Cheaper too — one compositor-friendly animation instead of 8 repaints a second on every card.
- v6.63 — the rotate dial snaps every 45 degrees (queue 99). Eight detents at 0/45/90/135/180/225/270/315, drawn on the rim so you can see them coming, turning blue and buzzing as you catch one. It only snaps on the DIAL — the Rotation number box beside it still scrubs and types any angle you like, which is the same split as the trackpad snapping while the canvas drag stays free. Dragging past a notch releases it normally, and a full turn still lands on 360: the angle is accumulated raw and only snapped on the way out, so the drag never creeps.
- v6.64 — "importing the audio cuts parts of the file out" (queue 72), and it turned out to be TWO different bugs wearing one complaint. (1) The waveform drawn on the clip only ever read the LEFT channel, while playback and export mix both — so anything hard-panned, any one-sided old stereo mix, any interview miked to one side, drew as a flat line under sound you can plainly hear. Nothing was ever missing from those files; only the picture of them was wrong. It now takes the peak across every channel. (2) The real one: a clip's length came from the file's own header and was never checked. An mp3 with a damaged length header makes the browser GUESS from the first second's bitrate, and the guess can be wildly short — so the clip was created shorter than the song and the end was genuinely gone. Measured on such a file: 26.4 seconds of audio that claims to be 11.2, now imported at its full 26.4. It costs about 25ms, because the length now comes from the same decode the waveform was already doing a moment later.
- v6.65 — a clip that runs past the end of its own source no longer tries to resume it. If the transport asks for a moment that is beyond where the audio actually stops, the player now holds silent instead of calling play() on a finished clip, which some browsers answer by rewinding to the start. Being straight about this one: it is a sensible guard, not a proven cure. I built a probe to demonstrate the restart and Chrome would NOT do it, so this is not yet the explanation for a song looping its first half-second — the real lead is the speed-ramp maths handing back a tiny time, and that is written up rather than guessed at. Ordinary resuming (the playhead leaving a clip and coming back) is measured unchanged.
- v6.66 — dragging a clip on the timeline no longer selects it (queue 100). Ezra: "I want to be able to drag layers without selecting them, right now it just selects it but doesn't show the ui." That half-state was deliberate and wrong: v5.23 selected the clip on grab so you could see what you had hold of, then hid the phone panel so it didn't cover the timeline — leaving the layer selected with nothing on screen saying so. Selection now happens on RELEASE and only if the clip never moved, so a drag leaves your selection exactly as it was and a tap still selects. The clip sliding under your finger is the feedback. The regression test that enforced the old rule was rewritten to the new one rather than deleted, with both sides of the decision recorded.
- v6.67 — three timeline/home fixes. (1) The timeline glide carries much further: a hard flick used to cover about 3.7 seconds of timeline and now covers nearly 9, so you cross a long project in fewer throws. That is done by lowering the friction, not by throwing it harder — throwing harder would make small flicks twitchy while leaving the long tail, the part you actually feel, exactly as short. (2) On PC, swiping to the end of the timeline no longer sails past and snap back. The strip could scroll further than the project is long; the time stopped at the end but the view kept going, and a settle timer hauled it back a sixth of a second later. The end is now a wall the scroll stops against, with nothing left to correct. (3) The home-card film grain, third attempt. It no longer MOVES at all — both earlier versions animated the noise's position, which is why one lurched and the other looked like a sheet sliding behind the card. It now re-rolls in place between six separate noise tiles, the way the app's own Film Grain effect does, and every card is started at a different point in the cycle so they boil independently instead of flickering in unison.
- v6.68 — the home-card grain is smooth now. The level was right, so nothing about how strong it looks has changed; what changed is that it no longer CUTS. v6.67 swapped between six noise tiles instantly, five times a second — it did not move, but an instant swap is still a flicker, and flicker is what "jumpy" has meant each time. Instead of slowing the cutting down (which would only give a slower flicker) it stops cutting altogether: each card now holds two noise fields and gently dissolves between them, so the grain is always melting from one pattern into another and never snaps. The two layers are exact opposites, so the overall strength stays at the same level right through the dissolve — only the pattern changes, never the brightness. Each card gets its own pair of fields and its own timing, so no two cards move together.
- v6.69 — removed the duplicate grid button from the canvas view rail. Ezra: "you've added the grid button twice in that menu." He was right — two buttons in the same rail both toggled the guides. The one removed was also the wrong one to keep: it flipped the setting itself, while every other button on that rail presses the control that OWNS its setting, which is what stops the rail and the settings panel ever disagreeing. Nothing is lost; the guides are still one tap away on the button beside it.
- v6.70 — Snapping and Onion skin now tell you what they did. Both change nothing you can see at the moment you press them — snapping does nothing until your next drag, onion skin does nothing unless there is a neighbouring frame to ghost — so they were the two buttons in that rail that could look broken while working perfectly. They now say the resulting state ("Snapping on — clips and keyframes stick to edges"). The message is attached to the switch itself rather than the button, so pressing it from the rail, the settings panel or the keyboard all report the same thing.
- v6.71 — the little triangle at the top of the playhead now sits exactly over the line. It was one pixel to the right, which is three on your screen, and you were right to spot it: the line is drawn as a border and the triangle was being positioned from just inside that border rather than from the line itself. The line has not moved — it is what every time in the app is measured against — only the triangle.
- v6.72 — everything on screen says "Colour" now, not "Color". The category is Colour & Light, and so are Colour Temperature, Colour Balance, Colourize, Hot Colour, Stroke Colour, Replace Colour, Spot Colour, Four-Colour Gradient and every "Colour" swatch label. Only the words you READ changed — the internal names the effects are saved under are untouched, so nothing in an existing project shifts.
- v6.73 — the Colour & Light previews actually show what each effect does now. Most of that section had no picture of its own, so forty-odd tiles were the SAME photograph with a series of quiet grades on it, which reads as nothing happening no matter how well the effects work. Thirteen effects were moved onto photographs chosen for them — Spot Colour onto the one shot with a vivid band it can keep, Teal & Orange onto a colourless one instead of a photo that was already teal and orange, Grayscale onto the strongest black-and-white subject, Highlights & Shadows onto a picture that actually HAS crushed shadows and blown highlights — and eleven had their preview settings corrected. Two of those were real bugs rather than taste: Soft Glow's preview radius worked out to ONE PIXEL, so its tile had no glow in it at all, and Halation's two radii were collapsed into one, which is precisely the thing that effect exists not to be. Radial Shadow also moved off the small card, where it could only ever draw a rim, onto a subject with room for the shadow to spread and fade.
- v6.74 — frame-rate lists are now 15/25/30/50/60/120 everywhere (24 dropped as asked, Custom still reaches it), the export list is ordered ascending to match the canvas one, and the AI's own fps snapping uses the same set so it can't land on a rate the menu no longer offers; home-card film grain turns over 5x a second instead of once per 1.3s — still cross-faded, so it boils without strobing; and the effect registries are null-prototype with own-property guards, closing a live crash where an effect named `toString` in a project file resolved to a function off the prototype chain and took the whole effects panel down.
- v6.75 — home-card film grain finally flows: the cross-fade's timing function was `ease-in-out`, which flattens the curve at both ends of every cycle, so the grain came to a dead stop twice per cycle and restarted — the "noticeable start and stop" Ezra reported four times. Changed to `linear` (and .5s → .36s). Measured on the real keyframes: ease-in-out was effectively motionless for 12.5% of each cycle, linear for 0.0%; rate variation CV 0.545 → 0.056. My three earlier attempts all changed the DURATION, which cannot fix a pause.
- v6.76 — pin anything to the top of home: ⋯ → "Pin to top" on projects, templates and elements, flipping to "Unpin" once set, with no cap on how many. Pinned cards lift to the front as a stable partition so your existing order still holds inside each block, marked by a small blue tack on the thumbnail's top-right (same dark plate as the OPEN badge; hidden in Select mode, where the tick owns that corner). Pins are stored per tab in localStorage rather than on the record, so a project you export and re-import never arrives pinned on someone else's home. Search deliberately ignores pins — best match wins when you have typed a query.
- v6.77 — the overpull slam Easter egg finally works on a phone, and pinned cards got real flare. The slam never regressed: `git log -L` shows the block is byte-identical to the day it shipped, so it had been broken on real touch the whole time and only ever worked under synthetic tests. Two causes: `dy <= 0` permanently nulled the pull, and a real finger's first pointermove often reports the same clientY as the touch down (dy === 0) — now only a deliberate upward move of 12px cancels; and iOS fired pointercancel the moment Safari claimed the drag for its own rubber-band, so a scoped touchmove preventDefault keeps the gesture ours. The hard 150px clamp is gone too (queue 131's "freeze"): travel past 150px is compressed to 28% rather than cut off, so it answers your finger at any distance and still slams. Mutation-checked — restoring `dy <= 0` reproduces the dead egg exactly. Pinned cards now carry a cyan leading rail (an inset shadow, so nothing shifts), a wash across the first half, a soft throw and a brightened name; the rule had to be mirrored into theme-glass.css because `html[data-theme="glass"] .hm-card` outranks a bare `.hm-card.is-pinned`.
- v6.78 — the black strip down the right edge (queue 135) is defended against: while home is up, #app parks at translate3d(100%,0,0) and #add-fab at translate3d(100vw,0,0), both position:fixed — harmless until anything lets the page pan sideways, at which point what you see beside the app is the bare page background, top bar included. It never reproduced on desktop (13 interleaved open/close cycles sampled every frame, no transform-without-fixed state, scrollWidth never exceeded clientWidth), so rather than guess which element wins the race on iOS the sideways scroll itself is gone: overflow-x: clip on html and body. Nothing in this app is meant to pan horizontally. Forcing window.scrollTo(600,0) now leaves scrollX at 0 even with the editor deliberately parked off-screen; vertical scrolling is untouched.
- v6.79 — the bar at the bottom of every screen was `theme-color`. It was #12151b in both the meta tag and manifest.json, and on iOS 26 a standalone PWA tints the area around the page with theme-color — including the home-indicator safe area. That band sat under the splash, home and the editor alike, reading as a bar because #12151b is lighter than the app's ground and lighter still than the splash's pure black. Nothing in the page could have painted it (the splash is position:fixed inset:0 at z-index 10000), which is what ruled the page out. Both now #000000, matching the splash and the manifest's background_color. This is very likely the same band as the right-edge strip in queue 135, which is why that survived the v6.78 overflow fix.
- v6.80 — diagnostic probe removed, and the bottom-bar question is finally settled by measurement rather than guesswork: innerH 894 · visualH 894 · safeBottom 34 · dpr 3 · standalone yes, with the magenta page-bottom band sitting ABOVE the remaining strip. The page ends ~34pt (the home-indicator safe area) above the screen bottom, so the bar is OUTSIDE the page and no CSS can paint there — which retires both earlier fixes and every "an element is too short" theory. Prime suspect now: iOS caches a PWA's launch config at add-to-Home-Screen time, so the installed icon never picked up viewport-fit=cover. Next step is a fresh install, not a code change.
- v6.82 — the overpull slam no longer shows the editor through the gap: the shake moves home up to 13px and twists it .34deg, and home is fixed inset:0, so the viewport was briefly uncovered at the edges. It now carries a 140px ring of its own background on the same element, so the ring travels and rotates with the shake and the gap is always home's colour. Verified by painting the editor bright red and freezing home at the shake's worst frame — no red anywhere (elementFromPoint cannot check this; it ignores box-shadow). Also measured, for Ezra's question about the two screens stacking: renderScene runs 0 times in 1.5s with home open AND 0 times idle in the editor, so the covered editor costs nothing per frame — the canvas only draws on demand.
- v6.85 — the black bar, finally: index.html painted the document canvas pure #000 while the app's ground is #060c0f. The canvas covers the whole web view, which under viewport-fit=cover is larger than the layout viewport, so every sliver the page did not cover read as a black bar — right edge, bottom edge, splash, home and editor alike. Five earlier attempts hunted an element that was too small; a four-agent audit proved none exists (no width rule, no safe-area-inset-right use, no horizontal overflow, no narrowing commit since v6.73) and his own probe read 440 across every metric. html now paints the app's ground, themed so classic and glass each set their own, and the splash surround matches so it cannot show during load either.
- v6.86 — new text layers are the right size on every frame shape, and Edit Text stops looking like a keyframe. The "180pt renders tiny" report had the wrong diagnosis: fontSize reaches the renderer correctly (measured linear at 40/160/180/400pt). The DEFAULT was wrong — `P.height/12` gives exactly 180 on his 4:3 2160p project, and scaling by height is only consistent while height is the short side, so landscape and square frames got text at half the intended size (a 1920x1080 project defaulted to 90pt, 8.5% of the frame). Now min(width,height)/6.75: portrait is byte-identical at 160, his 4:3 goes 180 → 320, landscape 90 → 160. Separately, the Edit Text card was falling through to the element icon, whose path is literally the keyframe diamond; text and caption layers now get their own capital-T glyph in the same gradient family.
- v6.87 — a locked layer now wears a red padlock on its timeline preview (#117). Built as a DOM overlay in a `.th-thumb-wrap`, not painted into the 38x24 bitmap, because `renderThumb` also feeds the parent-picker and the ⋯ menus. The thumbnail dims to .62 under a scrim so the badge reads over a bright frame. Two things the build got wrong first and a screenshot caught: a `drop-shadow` filter blurred into a 13px glyph and turned #ff4d4d muddy (replaced with a hard dark halo drawn under the red), and the scrim — a `::after`, which the spec paints after every child — sat ON TOP of the padlock at z-index:auto and composited the red down to rgb(151,48,50). `getComputedStyle` reported the bright red the whole time; only the pixels disagreed. Measured after the fix: rgb(255,77,77) exactly. New regression test (232 total) asserts built-when-locked, absent-when-not, visible, red, over the thumbnail, gone after unlocking, and explicitly stacked above its own scrim; mutation-checked by disabling the badge and watching it go red. Also hardened the splash test, which threw a false red on the first cold-cache run after the repo folder moved.
- v6.88 — a captions layer stops killing its own timeline row (#136). Nothing was ever locked: a caption track's cue chips can blanket its whole clip bar, and each chip seized the pointer on `pointerdown` with `stopPropagation` + `preventDefault`, so the clip's own handler never ran — no scrub, no hold-to-move and no scroll, on that one row. The clip is the default owner now; on touch a cue has to be press-and-HELD to take the gesture, at 300ms so it always beats the clip's own 350ms hold by the clock instead of by a race. A clean tap on a cue still parks the playhead on it, and a mouse still grabs a cue on the way down (desktop scrolling is the wheel, which none of this touches). New test drives a real synthetic touch through a chip and asserts all three paths — drag-reaches-the-clip, hold-still-grabs-the-cue, mouse-unchanged; mutation-checked by restoring the old seize and watching it report time 0.000. Suite 233/233 at desktop and 380px.
- v6.89 — Paste Style draws the inspector's own category icons (#127). It had kept a private table of flat single-path glyphs, so when the cards were regraded to the coloured gradient set in #77 the two drifted and Ezra's screenshot caught both on screen at once. The fix is the deletion of that second table, not a re-copy of the paths — the grid asks `catIco()` the same question the cards ask, so there is nothing left that can go stale. `catIco('text')` was added for the one tile the inspector has no category for; the Text tile used to be a literal "Aa" in text. Off tiles are desaturated rather than recoloured, because a self-coloured glyph cannot show on/off by changing `color`. New test parses the reference through the same parser and compares every tile's markup against the inspector's; mutation-checked by giving one tile a private glyph again and watching it go red. Suite 234/234.
- v6.90 — speech detection now says WHY it found nothing, and its real-speech quality is finally measured (#152). Ezra: "im pretty sure the auto detect speaking and auto make the captions doesnt work… would be better to not add it then add a shit version." The suite's only VAD test asked whether it could tell bursts from a steady tone, which could never have answered him. Measured properly against three synthesised spoken sentences with exact ground truth: 3/3 utterances on a clean voice within ~100ms, 3/3 with a music bed 18dB down, 2/3 at -12dB, 0/3 at -6dB, and — importantly — zero invented cues on music with nobody talking. So the detector works and his report is still true: he points it at a song, there is no voice above the music, and "No speech found" reads exactly like a broken button. It now distinguishes the two cases from the level distribution (a voice swings it — clipDbStd 100 on clean speech; a music bed does not — 0.18 with no voice, against 4.5 at -18dB where detection still worked) and says "that reads as music, not talking", with the full stats in the console. New regression test goes red if a clean voice stops being found within 250ms or if a cue is ever invented over music; fixtures committed under tests/_fixtures/vad. Suite 235/235.
- v6.91 — the scratchy audio, found (#148). Ezra: "the audio i import is making a realy scratchy popping noise that hurts my ears… this is related to the long on going lag issues." He was right about the link, though not by the route anyone expected. Five independent readings of the audio path found nothing that survived a skeptic — there is no ScriptProcessorNode anywhere, so lag cannot starve the audio thread; no double-connect; no unramped boundary reachable by a plain imported song. What none of them examined was the drift correction. Measured in a real browser, four seconds of ONE plain audio clip with no effects at speed 1: 208 of 240 sync decisions were a rate trim and `el.playbackRate` was written 55 times a second, wandering the full ±10% band and pinned at the ceiling. `preservesPitch` defaults on, so a rate change is answered by a WSOLA TIME-STRETCHER — re-priming one tens of times a second is precisely a scratchy warble, and since nothing is dropped it left no trace in the seek counter. It never converged because `el.currentTime` is latched to the last block handed to the audio device and therefore reads a constant output latency behind the true position; a proportional controller cannot remove a constant, so it leaned on the throttle forever. A busier machine means a bigger, noisier latency — that is the real tie to the lag. Fixed by learning the constant with a slow EMA and subtracting it (real drift accumulates and outruns the filter, so it is still corrected) and rate-limiting trim writes to 4/s, which is finer than a ±10% correction can act on anyway; a speed ramp or preview-rate change is still honoured on the next frame. Measured after: 6 writes in 4s (1.5/s) and median error 152.9ms → 23.4ms, inside the dead band. The test harness's fake element was a PERFECT clock, which is why it had reported everything healthy — it now models an 80ms latency, and the new test goes red at 26.5 writes/s with the fix disabled. Suite 236/236 at desktop and 380px.
- v6.92 — Settings gets a default colour for new shapes (#142). Two states rather than one: a colour you pick, or Random — the app's long-standing fresh-hue-per-shape behaviour, which also doubles as the reset — with the swatch showing a spectrum on random and the colour itself otherwise. It hooks the ONE line in makeLayer that decides a shape's fill, so it reaches every route that spawns one (the add menu, freehand, vector drawing, the ⋯ menus) without a second list to keep in sync, and it is creation-time only so shapes already on a timeline are untouched. The exemption Ezra asked about holds by construction: anything arriving WITH a fill — saved project, template, element, AI layer, duplicate — passes it in and is never repainted, because the line is `props.fill || FM.defaultShapeFill()`. Validated at both ends (load and use), since settings live in hand-editable localStorage and this string goes straight to a canvas fillStyle. The test guards the exemption specifically and mutation-checking it reddened not just that test but five effects tests too, which is a fair measure of how load-bearing that `props.fill ||` is. Verified at 380px in both states. Suite 237/237.
- v6.93 — the add menu says which buttons change section and which add a thing (#145). Ezra: "make the background of all the buttons like elements shape etc a different colour to the ones in inside each section, like text etc. so it shows the difference in action, and then you can add colour to all the sub section buttons… on pc they should be sharp and detailed and have black outlines." Tabs and cards shared ONE `.addmenu-tab, .addmenu-card` background rule, so a row of destinations and a grid of actions were the same slate tile with the same rim. Split by role and carried by two devices so it survives either theme: tabs become a recessed well (light falls INTO them, like a control strip), item cards get a tinted plate behind the icon plus a matching rim. Colour comes from a curated eight-hue ring walked by the item's index across the whole tab — chosen over a colour per item (forty more decisions, and saved elements are created at runtime so they could never have one) and over hashing the label (every button its own hue, no two with any relationship, which reads as accidental). Media tiles are exempt: they show the user's own frame and a plate over a photograph is noise. PC icons are authored at 24px with stroke-width 1.8 and rendered at 34px, so the line stays put while the figure grows — that thinning IS the "goofy". Desktop gets 2.2 weight plus the black outline he asked for, as a hard zero-blur shadow (a blurred one muddies a small glyph, measured on the v6.87 padlock). Two false starts, both caught by the v5.05 shape-icon regression test: a 34px plate squeezed the shape art to 20px under border-box, and letting the wrapper grow let the flex item shrink to 22px — the dense 6-across grid has no room for a plate, so those tiles take the tint on the stroke and no box. The glass theme needed its own tab rule: `html[data-theme="glass"] .addmenu-tab` is (0,2,0) and outranks a bare `.addmenu-tab`, the third time that trap has bitten this file. New test compares COMPUTED backgrounds, since asking the stylesheet would have passed while the screen stayed wrong. Suite 238/238 at desktop and 380px.
- v6.94 — the PC top strip stops repeating the project name (#146). Ezra: "on pc get rid of the project name editor thats at the top, its already at the bottom." He is right that it is a duplicate, but only half the time: #proj-name is dual-purpose — with a layer selected it renames THAT LAYER, and only with nothing selected does it show the project name. That is why his screenshot shows "IF I HAD ONE" at the top and "If i had one slowed" in the inspector: a layer name and a project name, not two copies of one thing. Deleting it outright would have silently removed layer renaming from the PC top bar, which he did not ask for, so it hides only while it would be the second copy of the same project name and returns as the renamer the moment something is selected. Studio layout only — the classic layout has no #proj-name-s to duplicate. The test asserts BOTH halves, since the failure mode of a naive fix is losing a capability rather than showing a wrong pixel; mutation-checked. Suite 239/239.
- v6.95 — dragging a caption cue's edge finally moves the cue (#149). Ezra: "when dragging the cue length for captions it should show it changing live not just wait for you to let go then jump." My first read of this said the chip already updated live, because the move handler does set its left/width every frame — that read was wrong, and only measuring caught it. tests/_cuelive.html samples the three candidate surfaces through a real drag: the cue DATA moved live, and the chip's RENDERED width sat at 0.0px for the entire drag then stepped to 462.5 the instant the release rebuilt it. A rendered width of zero is the tell — the chip was DETACHED. startCue calls selectLayer(), which rebuilds the timeline and throws away the chip captured one line earlier, so every pointermove afterwards restyled a node that had left the document. Styling a detached node raises nothing and paints nothing, which is exactly why the code looked right and the screen did not. Fixed in two halves: skip the rebuild entirely when the layer is already selected (the common case), and re-acquire the chip from the live DOM by its data-ci whenever a rebuild does take it, so a drag is never left holding an orphan. Measured after: the chip tracks the finger for 101.9px and does not move on release. The test measures RENDERED width rather than the data or the style property, since both of those were correct throughout; mutation-checked. Suite 240/240 at desktop and 380px.
- v6.96 — the PC text editor stops covering the text you are typing (#147). Ezra: "this pop up menu on pc is so shit, it literally covers up the text while you edit it, get it off the canvas." Measured before touching anything (tests/_tecover.html, 1280x860): the editor CARD covers 0.0% of the canvas — layoutDesktop already reserves a band for it and that works — and the Aa options panel covers 100.0%. The complaint was entirely the popover, so that is what moved, into the side column where the app already keeps vertical lists of controls, which is where he said to put it. Measured after: 0.0%. It falls back to the old over-the-stage placement when that column is too small to hold it (a narrow window, Studio's short inspector band), so it can never be worse than before. Only the popover moved: relocating the card as well would have been re-architecture for a surface measured at zero coverage. The second half of his report — the 169px the card reserves, "making it smaller" — is NOT fixed and is written up as outstanding: the column is 286px against the card's 320px minimum, so it needs a real re-layout, and it lands in the same strip #143/#144 are rebuilding. The test asserts geometric overlap with the canvas rather than "is it in the right parent", since a popover in the right parent could still sit over the picture; mutation-checked at 100.0%. Suite 241/241.
- v6.97 — the effect sliders glide like the timeline again (#116, his second time asking). The glide was never missing: #45 added it and it is attached to every one of these controls. #103 then retuned the TIMELINE's friction from 0.9 to 0.947 on his "the glide ends too quick" — taking a full-speed flick from ~3.7s of timeline to ~8.8s — and left the sliders on 0.9, under a comment that read "same friction as the timeline's momentum". True the day it was written, false from #103 onward. So the sliders were not missing a glide, they were wearing the timeline's OLD one, which next to the new one is exactly "stiff". Fixed with the same lever #103 used, because friction sets the distance (v0·16.67/(1−f)) and launch speed does not: a full-speed flick now carries ~979px of ruler against ~395px, 2.5x further, with the velocity clamp raised only modestly (2.5 → 3.2, mirroring #103's 0.022 → 0.028) so a light flick does not turn twitchy, and the stop threshold lowered from 0.008 to 0.004 so the longer tail is not cut off while still visibly moving. The durable half is the test: both tunings are exposed (FM.glideTuning, FM.timeline.momentumTuning) and the suite now fails if they ever differ, plus a floor so neither can drift back to the pre-#103 value. A comment could not hold that invariant — it stopped being true in silence and the only symptom was a person asking twice. Mutation-checked by restoring 0.9. Suite 242/242 at desktop and 380px.
- v6.98 — onion skin moves to the layer ⋯ menu, and out of both global menus (#122). Ezra: "shouldn't onion skin not be in the view options and app settings? Idk why it would be there since it only effects one layer, it should just be in the three dots when you have a layer selected." The code agrees with him: drawOnionSkin() opens with `const sel = FM.selectedLayer(...); if (!sel) return;`, so it ghosts the SELECTED layer and does nothing without one — App settings, which opens with nothing selected, was offering a switch that could be flicked while unable to act. That is the answer to the "check what happens when nothing is selected" note the request carried. Moved rather than copied: the view-bar button and the settings row are both gone, leaving one door, with a ✓ on the menu label because that menu has no switch furniture to show state. The hidden #btn-onion stays as the single implementation so the toast and button state cannot drift from the menu. Two existing tests had the old requirement baked in (Settings must lead with Onion skin) and were updated with his reversal quoted in place, so they are not "fixed" back later. Mutation-checked by deleting the new entry. Suite 243/243 at desktop and 380px.
- v6.99 — Linear Repeat stops squishing (#123), and the add-menu colour moves onto the whole card (#145, corrected). Ezra on the repeat: "Linear repeat effect is shit and needs work, currently it just squishes horizontally when you do it" — an exact diagnosis. It was a per-pixel warp mapping each cell's 0..1 across the FULL source width, so every copy held the whole picture crushed into 1/count of the frame. A repeat draws copies at their own size, which a coordinate remap cannot do, so it is a canvas effect now (Tiles' machinery) that draws the layer's plate again at an offset, nothing scaled, with Copies / Spacing / Direction / Fade out and spacing measured against the CONTENT's own width so 100% butts copies edge to edge at any layer size. Its test measures drawn ink and fails if a copy is narrower than the original. On the add menu he corrected v6.93 with the whole Text card circled: "I meant the whole shape around it, so you get rid of that little square bubble around then that's colourful, and since you made the top icons go dark when not selected you've already done enough." So the tinted plate behind each icon is gone and the tint is on the button itself — which also fixes the off-centre glyphs he spotted (the plate's own padding and border were pushing them), removes the two special cases the dense Shape grid needed, and leaves the tabs exactly as they were. Suite 244/244 at desktop and 380px.
- v7.01 — a duplicate lands exactly on its original (#156). Ezra: "Duplicating stuff should duplicate it in its exact position, not move it slightly." cloneLayer added +30px to x and y — and to every keyframe of an animated path — under the comment "nudge so the copy is visible". That was a fair default while "Duplicate in place" sat beside it in the layer menu as the other choice; it stopped being fair in v5.91, when he circled six entries including that one and said "Remove the circled options in this menu". From then on the nudging version was the only duplicate in the app and there was no way to get an exact copy at all. The animated half matters more than the static one: a path shifted 30px is a whole animation displaced, which is far harder to spot and to undo. Paste loses the offset with it, matching the AM behaviour that function already follows for time (it re-times to the playhead). The copy stays tellable apart now that it sits underneath the original — selected on creation, named "… copy", next clip colour — and the test asserts those two, since with the offset gone they are the only things left doing that job. Mutation-checked by restoring the nudge. Suite 246/246.
- v7.02 — the black bar, for the last time: the canvas had the app's colour but none of its light (#154).
